# Plan of attack after the architecture review

Status: proposed (2026-10-10). This doc covers four things:
- a triage of the architecture review (kept outside the repository, like the later reviews; each finding and its fix are recorded here), with each finding re-checked against the code and most reproduced
- a re-review of the updated [VISION.md](VISION.md) and design docs
- new evidence from the test server
- the resulting order of work

VISION.md sets the priorities. This doc turns them into tasks with acceptance criteria, and lists the decisions only the owner can make (§6).

## 1. Review triage

Every finding holds up. Reproductions used `target/release/sqlh.exe`, built from the code as of `4e9b411` (`601e6ba` changed only docs). They were local probes, or harmless read-only queries against the test server.

| ID | Review | Verdict | Evidence from this re-check | Plan priority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F01 | P1 | Confirmed, reproduced | `-i f.sql -o f.sql`: the SQL file was replaced with `-- note: no SQL to run`, exit 0 | P1 |
| F02 | P1 | Confirmed, reproduced | `sqlh -Q "" --format json \| true` exited 0, three times out of three, with no output delivered | P1 |
| F03 | P1 | Confirmed, reproduced | The guard passed `NEXT VALUE FOR`, `EXEC sys.sp_spaceused N'dbo.T', N'true'`, `EXEC Lnk.master.sys.sp_who`, `EXEC sales.sp_who` and `INSERT ##shared`. `##` writes are still rolled back by the wrapper; their risk is blocking other sessions. | P1, with `##` as a policy decision (§6) |
| F04 | P1 | Confirmed live, **and worse** | `--check -Q "SET NOEXEC OFF; SELECT 1"` ran the SELECT within the same batch, then printed "--check compiled the SQL without running it". Combined with `--allow-write`, input can execute writes. | **P0: first fix** |
| F05 | P1 | Confirmed, reproduced | `-Q "SELECT [é"` panics at `guard.rs:508` and the process ends with 0xC0000409, outside the exit-code contract and with no status line | P1 |
| F06 | P1 | Confirmed live | 300 inserts into a temp table produced 300 `-- 1 row affected` lines. Huge `(max)` values are materialized before cutting. | P1 (context budget) |
| F07 | P2 | Agree (inspection) | A harness that keeps reading stdout is unaffected, and a harness kill still works (§3). It matters for MCP. | P2 |
| F08 | P2 | Agree (inspection) | Only Ctrl+C is wired. Internal statements don't get the cancel handle. | P2 |
| F09 | P2 | Confirmed, reproduced | A Windows-auth profile plus `SQLCMDUSER` resolved to SQL login `environment_login`. Duplicate `server =` keys are accepted, last one wins. | P2 |
| F10 | P2 | Confirmed live | `-v sep=GO` produced a single batch and `Incorrect syntax near 'GO'`. A UTF-16 file's dangling byte was dropped silently. | P2 |
| F11 | P2 | Confirmed live | `SELECT 1 AS [-- forged]` printed the header `-- forged:int`, which reads as metadata | P2 |
| F12 | P2 | Agree (inspection) | Real, but profile edits are rare and human-run, so the gap is narrow. Surfacing credential-deletion errors is the part worth doing early. | P3, deletion errors P2 |
| F13 | P2 | Agree; **likely real in Git Bash** | Rust's `IsTerminal` treats mintty/msys ptys as terminals, but `GetConsoleMode` fails on them, so echo would stay on. Needs a manual check with a dummy password. | P2 |
| F14 | P2 | Agree (inspection) | Fix the doc claim now. Real zeroization can reuse the `zeroize` crate, which mssql-tds already pulls in, at no size cost. | P3, doc fix P1 |

The review's architecture section also holds up: extract an execution core, keep the driver boundary, and test the bespoke parsers more seriously. Its corrections to the schema and MCP plans are folded into Phases 4 and 5 below.

The vision update is coherent, and AGENTS.md, DESIGN.md and the two plans now agree with it. What remains is mostly stale text and claims the code doesn't meet:

| # | Where | Problem | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | VISION.md, DESIGN.md §2 | "SQL Server 2025 Standard on Windows Server 2025 Standard" didn't say where `sqlh.exe` runs. | **Resolved** (§6.1): workstations only, server usually remote. VISION.md, DESIGN.md and AGENTS.md updated. |
| D2 | DESIGN.md §4.3 | Says `$(var)` is substituted before splitting; the code splits first (F10) | **Decided** (§6.3): keep the code, correct the doc in Phase 1 |
| D3 | DESIGN.md §4.3, §6 | Ctrl+Break and console close are promised but not handled (F08) | Phase 2 |
| D4 | DESIGN.md §8 | "zeroed after use" is not true (F14) | Phase 1: correct the claim |
| D5 | `--help`, no README | No warning that the guard isn't a security boundary (F03). It matters more now that the test login is **sysadmin** (§3). | Phase 1 |
| D6 | `docs/notes/m0-driver-spike.md` | "Still to verify" lists Server 2016, SQL 2012/2014 and Azure | Phase 1: replace with the first-release checklist (§7) |
| D7 | schema-commands.md §4–§5 | Unlimited `describe`/`def` conflicts with a whole-response budget; fallback name resolution can discard an explicit schema | Phase 4 |
| D8 | mcp-mode.md §5 | Hour-long read-only session transaction; collected responses have no byte cap | Phase 5 |
| D9 | The test server | Its edition is *Standard Developer*, while first-release validation names *Standard* | Release checklist §7 |

## 3. New evidence from the test server

- **Connecting dominates every call.**
  - `SELECT 1` takes 171–265 ms inside sqlh, of which **162–254 ms is connect plus login**. The query itself, with setup and rollback, is about 10 ms. Ping to the server is 2.3 ms.
  - `-No` didn't help, so it isn't data-path TLS.
  - About 70 round trips' worth of time for a handshake that needs perhaps 5 points at something in the connect path: name lookups, SChannel credential setup, certificate parsing, or driver retries.
  - This is the largest lever on VISION's "time to the next useful answer". It also decides how urgent pooling is.
  - **Explained by 2.1** ([notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md)): about 150 ms of each connect is the login step, most likely the server hashing the SQL login's password (PBKDF2, new in SQL Server 2025), by design. That attribution is an inference from the measured interval. sqlh and the driver add about 5 ms.
- **A harness kill cancels server work promptly, for waits at least.** sqlh was killed with TerminateProcess during a 20 s `WAITFOR`. Within about 200 ms (the observer's own connect time) the server request and session were gone. CPU-bound and lock-blocked queries aren't measured yet.
- **The test login is sysadmin.** The over-privileged connection VISION.md worries about is the default situation here, and the read-only guard is the only thing standing between an agent and writes.
- **Draining has a cost.** Showing 100 of 299,398 `Users` rows took 1.3 s, all of it spent counting rows nobody sees. Bigger tables scale linearly.

## 4. Plan of attack

Sizes: **S** is up to half a day, **M** is 1–2 days, **L** is longer. Each phase ends green: fmt, clippy, unit tests, and the new CLI tests.

### Phase 1: correctness and trustworthy outcomes (VISION priority 1)

**Status (2026-10-10): built and tested, and so are the fixes the follow-up review asked for (below).** 1.9 was checked by hand in Git Bash on the dev machine: the password stayed hidden. That Git Bash runs native programs in a real Windows console (pseudo-console mode), so echo-off works there. The refusal path for a console-less mintty is verified by code review only.
- The harness is `tests/cli.rs`: 13 process-level tests, 3 of them live against the scratch database `sqlh_test`. A negative control confirmed the live tests really run.
- Every task below has its regression tests; 61 unit tests in all.
- Also done along the way:
  - credential-deletion errors now surface (the urgent part of F12)
  - `profile list` checks that a password exists without reading it (part of F14)
- **Found while testing:** an unreachable server ends as exit 2 (TCP connect timed out) or exit 4 (login deadline), depending on which timer fires first. Fixed in 2.4: an address that refuses or doesn't answer is exit 2, a server that doesn't finish the login exit 4.

Start with a **CLI test harness**, because most of these fixes are process-level: exit codes, files, crashes. Add `tests/cli.rs`, which spawns `CARGO_BIN_EXE_sqlh` and asserts exit code, stdout and stderr. Most cases need no server. Live cases skip themselves unless `SQLH_TEST_PROFILE` is set. (M)

| # | Task | Acceptance | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | **F04.** In `--check` mode, refuse input that mentions `NOEXEC`, whether or not writes are allowed. (`PARSEONLY`, `FMTONLY` and `SHOWPLAN_*` only restrict execution further, so they can stay.) Re-send `SET NOEXEC ON` before every batch. Always use the rollback wrapper in check mode. Make the closing note true. | Live test: `SET NOEXEC OFF; SELECT 1` and the two-batch form are refused (exit 5) and nothing runs. `--check --allow-write` with an `INSERT` compiles and inserts nothing. | S |
| 1.2 | **F05 and a panic net.** Fix the unterminated-quote slice. Add property tests ("any UTF-8 input never panics") for both lexers, using a tiny in-repo generator rather than a new crate. Add a **panic hook** that writes `-- error: internal error ...` and `-- failed (exit 10)` and exits 10, so any future panic still honors the contract. | `SELECT [é` no longer crashes: it passes the guard and the server reports the syntax error. An injected test panic exits 10 with a status line. | S |
| 1.3 | **F01.** Load and preflight all input before opening `-o`. Refuse when output and input are the same file, comparing file identity via `GetFileInformationByHandle`, not path strings. Write to `<path>.partial` and rename at the end. | CLI tests: same path, `.\` alias and hard link are refused with exit 3, and the SQL file is intact. | S |
| 1.4 | **F02.** Errors from finishing or flushing output decide the exit code. Propose a **new exit code 7**, "output could not be delivered" (§6.2), with a best-effort stderr line. | A writer failing on its first write, mid-write and at flush gives exit 7, including for profile commands. | S |
| 1.5 | **F03 guard gaps.** Block `NEXT VALUE FOR`. Only accept procedure names qualified as `[master.]sys\|dbo.` with at most 3 parts, so linked servers and user schemas are refused. Drop `sp_spaceused` from the allowlist, or allow it with at most one positional argument and no `@updateusage`. Apply the `##` policy (§6.4). | Table tests for each case above, plus aliases and positional arguments. | S |
| 1.6 | **F11.** Escape a header name that starts with `--`, and pass every interpolated metadata field (procedure names, block reasons, notes) through the escaper, in csv's stderr stream too. | Hostile-name tests for headers, errors and blocked lines. | S |
| 1.7 | **F10.** Reject odd-length UTF-16. Implement and document the chosen substitution order (§6.3). Map line numbers correctly across multi-line values, or document that they're approximate. | Tests for odd bytes, a variable expanding to `GO`, and a multi-line value. | S |
| 1.8 | **F09 and the config parser.** Make auth explicit (`auth` decides it and `user` must agree). Resolve the auth method before credentials. Reject duplicate keys and contradictions. Let `-C` be turned off with `--trust-server-certificate=false`. | Precedence tests with an injected environment and credential store. | M |
| 1.9 | **F13.** Refuse to read a password unless echo was actually turned off, and point Git Bash users to PowerShell or `winpty`. | Manual: in Git Bash, `profile add` with a dummy password neither echoes nor proceeds. | S |
| 1.10 | **Docs truth-up** (D3–D6). Correct the claims about zeroization and Ctrl+Break, add the security-boundary sentence to `--help` and a short README, and replace the spike checklist with §7. | The doc review finds no claim that is ahead of the code. | S |

#### Phase 1 follow-up (the follow-up review of Phase 1)

The follow-up review asked for changes before Phase 1 counts as complete. Each finding was reproduced, fixed and given a regression test. As a negative control, the new tests were run against `c590bad`: they failed exactly where the review said, and the existing tests passed.

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R01 (P1) | Every run shared `<path>.partial`, so a run could write into output that another run had already published, and it took over any existing `.partial` file. | Each run creates its own `<path>.<pid>-<n>.partial` (CREATE_NEW, marked for deletion until published; no other process can change it, or open it while it's marked), closes it, then renames it. A failed run, or one killed while writing, leaves nothing (see R07 for when the deletion mark can't be set). An existing target that can't be written is refused before anything runs. Overlapping runs: the last complete file wins. | `files.rs` units; CLI: concurrent runs, a held destination, an existing `.partial` file |
| R02 | `eprintln!` and the panic hook could panic on a closed stream: exit 10 or an abort instead of 7. | Every diagnostic is a best-effort write. `--help` and `--version` write fallibly (exit 7). The password prompt returns write errors, so echo is restored, and the panic hook restores it too. stderr says when a failure mid-run leaves the outcome unknown. | CLI: stdout, stderr or both closed, across text, json, csv, help, version, usage errors and the panic path |
| R03 | The panic test failed in release builds. | Built only in debug builds, like `SQLH_DEBUG_PANIC`, and aimed at the unreachable address. | `cargo test --release --test cli` |
| R04 | The NOEXEC refusal advised `--allow-write`, which can't help. | Each refusal carries its own advice; `--allow-write` is suggested only where it helps. Specific advice for NOEXEC, `##` tables, `sp_spaceused`, qualified procedure names (`sys.sp_who`) and typos. | guard units; CLI: all three formats |
| R05 | CLR UDT type names reached text and csv metadata unescaped. | Type names are escaped like names, and `:` is written `\x3A`. | text and csv exact-output tests |

Also from the review:
- **Live suite:** the live tests are `#[ignore]`d, so skipping shows, and they fail when asked for without a profile. They write only to a table of their own, in a database with the `dbo.sqlh_scratch` marker (`tests/fixtures/sqlh_test.sql`), and drop it.
- **Docs:**
  - The README's "never interactive" names the `profile add` exception.
  - DESIGN.md and `--help` separate completed output from best-effort fatal diagnostics.
  - Exit 7 is documented as no reason to retry a write.
  - The `dbo.` rule is described as a policy, with the `sys.` spelling suggested.
- **Found while fixing:** Phase 1's rename broke `-o NUL` (exit 7). Devices and pipes are written directly again.
- **Still open, as planned:** the response budget (2.2), stalled-reader cancellation (2.3), Windows cancellation coverage (2.4), and F12's lock and config/credential ordering and F14's wiping (3.2).

Second round: the reviewer's verification of `b4d7e9a` and `6709c05` accepted R01–R05 and found two follow-ups for before the first release. Both are fixed. As a negative control, four unit tests and the CLI test fail against the old escaper.

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R06 (P2) | U+0085, U+2028 and U+2029 are line breaks to some consumers (Python's `splitlines`), and the escaper let them through, so a blocked statement's text could appear to start a `-- ok:` line. | `push_escaped` writes every control character as `\xNN`: C0, DEL and the whole C1 range, not only U+0085, so the rule is simply "every control character" and U+009B (a control sequence introducer on some 8-bit terminals) is covered. U+2028 and U+2029 become `\u2028` and `\u2029`. Other Unicode text is untouched. csv data rows (csv quoting) and json strings (valid as they are) are left alone. DESIGN.md §5.1 specifies the escapes and how a reader decodes them. | `output/mod.rs` units, including a check of every `char` against the break characters of Python's `splitlines`; text and csv exact output; CLI: blocked text holding each separator, in text and csv |
| R07 (P3) | The cleanup promise for the `.partial` file left out the case where the deletion mark can't be set. | Wording only. The mark is what deletes the file, even after a kill. Without it (not expected), `Drop` removes the file by name when the run ends normally, which is best effort, and a kill can leave it. `files.rs` and DESIGN.md §4.2 say so. | none: the failure can't be forced |

### Phase 2: fast, bounded, cancellable turns (VISION priority 2)

| # | Task | Acceptance | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | **Connect-latency investigation** (§3), time-boxed. Time each phase (TCP, prelogin, TLS, login, setup) behind `--verbose`, find where the roughly 150 ms goes, then fix it in our code or file it upstream. An upstream investigation must not hold up 2.2–2.4, which are release requirements (follow-up review, answer 5). **Done 2026-10-10** ([notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md)):<br>• Of a 195 ms connect, about 150 ms is the login step, most likely the server's PBKDF2 check of the SQL login's password (SQL Server 2025, by design; an inference from the measured interval, not an isolated measurement), about 26 ms is TLS over Wi-Fi, and about 5 ms is sqlh and the driver.<br>• `--verbose` and `profile test` report the connect and setup times. A build with `--features timing` also splits the connect into steps. That isn't in default builds because it costs 270 KB.<br>• Found on the way: the driver's debug logging dumps packets in clear text, including the login packet (§6.9). | A breakdown is recorded in `docs/notes/`. Target: under 40 ms connect on this LAN, or a documented reason why not. **Met by the documented reason:** with SQL authentication on SQL Server 2025, the login alone exceeds 40 ms. | M |
| 2.2 | **F06, the response budget.** Collapse consecutive `rows affected` lines into one (`-- 300 statements, 300 rows affected`). Cap `GO n` at 1,000. Cap input at 10 MB. Add a total output budget (proposed default 1 MB, `--max-output`) that keeps room for the closing lines and reports what was withheld. **Done 2026-10-10:**<br>• `--max-output N` (default 1,000,000 bytes) covers every format and both streams, and keeps 1 KB for the closing lines. Past it, nothing more of the body is written. The cut result set's footer says `(--max-output)`, and a closing note lists what wasn't shown. JSON stays valid.<br>• Like the other limits it only stops printing: the run finishes and the counts stay exact. Stopping early instead is the drain question (2.6).<br>• Input is capped at 10 MB before and after substitution, and `GO n` at 1000.<br>• Huge values are still read whole before being cut; that's 2.7. | The temp-table loop prints one line. A run over the budget ends with a valid status (text and JSON) and a note. **Met:** live tests for both, plus unit tests per format. | M |
| 2.3 | **F07, separate output from the database.** A writer thread fed by a bounded channel, so the runtime keeps handling timers and cancellation while a pipe is stalled. **Done 2026-10-10** (`output/delivery.rs`):<br>• Sinks hand 64 KB chunks to an output thread and never block.<br>• Past 256 KB queued, the batch loop awaits room (or a cancel) instead of reading rows.<br>• A reader that makes no progress for `-t` seconds means the output couldn't be delivered: exit 7, from the batch or from the last wait for delivery.<br>• No measurable cost: 200,000 rows to a file took 383–414 ms before and after. | Test: with a stalled reader, `-t 2` still fires. **Met:** a live test holds stdout open unread; both paths end with exit 7 after about 2 s. Unit tests cover order, a stall, recovery and a broken pipe. | M |
| 2.4 | **F08, cancellation everywhere.** Ctrl+Break, close, logoff and shutdown handlers. Pass the cancel handle through connect, setup, NOEXEC and rollback. Report "attention acknowledged" vs "connection dropped". Make an unreachable server end the same way every time: today the TCP connect timeout (exit 2) and the login deadline (exit 4) race. **Fail fast on a refused connection:** today sqlh retries until the login deadline, taking 14.2 s with the default `-l 15`, although one refused attempt takes about 2 s on Windows (measured 2026-10-10). Restore console echo when Ctrl+C or a close arrives at the `profile add` password prompt. **Done 2026-10-10** ([notes/connect-failures.md](notes/connect-failures.md)):<br>• Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break, close, logoff and shutdown cancel the run (`src/signals.rs`). The cancel reaches the connect, which is dropped at once, the setup, NOEXEC, every batch and the wait for a slow reader, and no later batch starts. The error says what became of the statement: cancelled with the connection still usable, or the connection closed because nothing confirmed the cancel within 2 s (1 s after close, logoff or shutdown).<br>• One connect attempt: a refused connection fails in about 2 s. TCP gets `-l` and the login deadline `-l` + 1, so an address that refuses or doesn't answer is exit 2, and a server that doesn't finish the login is exit 4, every time. `-l 0` means no limit, as in sqlcmd; before, it timed out the TCP connect at once.<br>• Found on the way: for a remote name, the driver's fallback to Named Pipes blocked the whole runtime for about 21 s when the host didn't answer, past `-l` and Ctrl+C. sqlh now reaches remote names over TCP only (DESIGN.md §4.2). That is an upstream issue worth filing (see the note).<br>• Also fixed: after a cancel, a stalled reader got 1 s and then the full `-t` again, when the output was dropped. And, found while preparing the review: time with nothing to write counted toward a stall, so a run that had been quiet for longer than the limit could end with exit 7 although its output was delivered. That happened after any cancel that came over 1 s after the last output, and could happen in a race at the end of a run whose quiet stretch reached `-t`. The stall clock now starts when the writer is handed work.<br>• The `profile add` prompt restores echo on Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Break or close, then lets Windows end the process.<br>• Tests: a refused connection, a server that never answers, and Ctrl+Break during a connect (process-level); Ctrl+Break during a running query and while waiting for a stalled reader (live). Not tested automatically: close (it needs a console window to close), logoff and shutdown (Windows sends them only to services), cancelling during setup, NOEXEC or rollback (the same path as a batch, but too quick to catch), and the prompt's echo. | Tests for each signal and for cancelling during connect and setup. An unreachable server always gives the same exit code. A refused connection fails after one attempt. **Met, except as listed:** Ctrl+C shares Ctrl+Break's path. | M |
| 2.5 | **Harness-kill behavior.** Extend the §3 experiment to a CPU-bound query, a lock-blocked query and a large scratch rollback. **Done 2026-10-11** ([notes/harness-kill.md](notes/harness-kill.md)): the server ended a waiting, a CPU-bound (serial and parallel) and a lock-blocked request within 0.14 s of the kill in all 46 runs (median about 30 ms), released a killed blocker's locks within 0.13 s, and left the blocker of a killed blocked reader untouched (7/7). A kill can't shorten the rollback of a big open transaction: 237 MB of log took 4.8–14.6 s after a kill, the same as an orderly ROLLBACK, and readers of the table wait; read-only #temp writes (92 MB) roll back in about 0.6 s. A kill that reaches only a parent shell ends nothing: sqlh runs on until `-t`. Mitigations are in the note (chunked writes, the lock timeout that 2.8 has since added, keep `-t`, kill the process tree); no code change. Live test `a_killed_run_leaves_no_work_on_the_server`. | Results in `docs/notes/`. Any gap gets a mitigation (a server-side `-t`, a session setting). | S |
| 2.6 | **Drain policy experiment.** Measure the cost of draining on the biggest StackOverflow2010 tables. Prototype a bounded drain: stop counting after a time or row budget, send an attention, and report `≥N rows`, with an explicit note that later statements in that batch didn't run. **Done 2026-10-11** ([notes/drain-experiment.md](notes/drain-experiment.md)): exact drains cost 1–4 s for 0.3–10M narrow rows, 21 s for 1.3 GB and 119 s for 6.8 GB (the default `-t 60` ends that one with exit 4 after the shown rows were delivered); the server and the link dominate, not decoding. A prototype (`SQLH_DEBUG_DRAIN`, debug builds only; commit f4a9c78, kept off `main`) cuts at the first hidden row for about TOP (100) + 10–70 ms, or after a 250 ms budget for 0.5–1.15 s; the attention is acknowledged in 3–420 ms, and the session, transaction and temp tables survive. Caveats: later statements in the batch don't run, XACT_ABORT ON rolls the wrapper back, a skipped COMMIT under `--allow-write` loses a write. The default is decision 6, decided 2026-10-11 and built in 2.15. | Numbers recorded, and the owner decides the default (§6.6). | M |
| 2.7 | **Memory.** Use the driver's streaming path (`next_row_into` with a cutting `RowWriter`, or PLP chunks) so hidden rows and cut cells aren't fully materialized. **Done 2026-10-11** ([notes/memory.md](notes/memory.md)): a result set with a PLP column (the `(max)` types, xml, json, CLR types) is read through the driver's column cursor in 32 KB chunks and cut as it arrives, keeping `--max-cell` characters and counting the rest (`src/db/stream.rs`). A 1 GiB `varchar(max)` shown with `--max-cell 500` peaked at 10.3 MB instead of 2,057 MB, with identical output. Rows past `--max-rows` are skipped without keeping their values. With `--max-cell 0` the `--max-output` budget serves as the cell limit. The 10 MB SQL cap is checked while `$(var)` substitution is built (595 MB → 29 MB peak in the worst case measured). Not covered: text/ntext/image, which the driver reads whole, and the guard's token list (task 2.14, since bounded). Cost: narrow rows with a small PLP column shown in bulk are 25–45% slower; everything else at parity. Live tests assert the peak working set. | Peak memory stays flat on a 1 GB `varchar(max)` value shown with `--max-cell 500`. | M |
| 2.8 | **Agent session defaults** (§6.7, decision 7): `SET LOCK_TIMEOUT` and `SET DEADLOCK_PRIORITY LOW`, so agent queries give way to production work instead of waiting behind it, and READ UNCOMMITTED for read-only runs on request (the owner's earlier sql-query-runner skill set it on every query). **Done 2026-10-11:**<br>• Every connection's first batch sets `DEADLOCK_PRIORITY LOW` and `LOCK_TIMEOUT 10000`, in read-only and `--allow-write` runs alike (`session_setup`, main.rs).<br>• `--lock-timeout SECONDS` (0 = no limit, sent as `LOCK_TIMEOUT -1`; at most 2147483) and the profile key `lock_timeout`. Precedence is flag, profile, default; there is no environment variable, as for the other limits.<br>• READ UNCOMMITTED is opt-in only: `--read-uncommitted[=false]` or the profile key `read_uncommitted`, for runs that can't write. The flag in a run that can write (`--allow-write`, or a profile whose write_policy is allow) is a usage error that says why; a profile's setting is ignored in such a run. When it applies, one note before the first batch says the rows may include uncommitted, duplicated or missing data, and it counts against `--max-output`. `--check` neither sets the level nor notes it.<br>• Error 1222 (lock timeout) and 1205 (deadlock victim) are ordinary SQL errors (exit 1), mapped to the input line; `--help` names 1222 next to `--lock-timeout`, since the error text doesn't say the timeout is sqlh's. A lock wait longer than `-t` is cut by `-t` (exit 4).<br>• `profile add` saves both settings and `profile list` shows them (effective values). Left as it was: a `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ UNCOMMITTED` in the input itself passes the guard and gets no note (DESIGN.md §4.3). | Documented in DESIGN.md §4.3; a live test confirms the settings, including a read that would otherwise block behind an open write transaction. **Met:** live tests for the session settings across nine flag combinations and every format, a blocked read that fails with 1222 after `--lock-timeout 2` while the same read with `--read-uncommitted` returns the dirty row at once, the note within the cap, and the agent session losing a deadlock (1205). DESIGN.md §4.2, §4.3, §6 and §8. | S |
| 2.9 | **Help tips from agent trial 1** ([notes/agent-trial-1.md](notes/agent-trial-1.md)). In `--help`: how to see a table's or view's columns (`SELECT TOP 0 * FROM x`, or `sys.all_columns` for system views, since `sys.columns` and `information_schema` show none), and that without `-d` the profile's database applies (`master` if it has none). Consider giving the `test` profile a database. **Done 2026-10-10:** an example shows `SELECT TOP 0 * FROM sys.dm_exec_requests` (columns and types only), and the `-d` line says the default is the profile's database, else the login's (often `master`); `--help` is still 62 lines. Giving the `test` profile a database is left to the owner. | `--help` stays under about 60 lines; a repeat of trial 1 needs fewer discovery calls (to check in trial 2, 2.10). | S |
| 2.10 | **Agent trial 2.** Same setup, with tasks that hit the row and value limits, a guard refusal (a cleanup request), and a long query, to see whether the agent narrows queries, reads the advice, and avoids `--allow-write`. **Done 2026-10-12** ([notes/agent-trial-2.md](notes/agent-trial-2.md)): five tasks through OpenCode with `qwen3.8`, 18 calls, every answer drawn from the output (one unsupported percentage). The model read `--help` and raised `--max-cell`, `-t` and `--exact-count` before any limit was met, so how it reads `≥N`, `…[+N]` and a timeout is still unobserved. Asked to clean up a table, it ran a 927,269-row `DELETE` with `--allow-write` without asking; only the profile's `write_policy = "deny"` stopped it. Database discovery is mostly solved by the profile's database and the status line. | Findings recorded in `docs/notes/`, follow-ups planned. **Met:** task 2.16, decision 12, the skill guidance (Phase 8) and a third trial (Phase 7). | S |
| 2.11 | **Provenance, timings and a contract version in the output** (from the comparison with Microsoft's [`mssql-sqlcmd`](https://github.com/microsoft/mssql-rs/tree/main/mssql-sqlcmd) crate, 2026-10-10; the `--format json` document it renders carries all three). Today a run's output never says which server or database it ran against, and the connect time shows only under `--verbose`. Agent trial 1 spent a call finding out it was in `master`, and [notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md) found that connecting dominates a call.<br>• **Where it ran:** the server, the database in effect, the auth method, whether the connection is encrypted and the server version: a `connection` object in JSON, and a short tail on the status line in text and csv (for example `-- ok: 3 rows, 12 ms; connect 195 ms; devsql01 / master`; the exact shape comes from the golden tests and the token cost). `Session::describe` already has these from the login, so there is no extra round trip. Check that the driver's `database()` follows a `USE`; if it doesn't, report the database at connect. Never a connection string or anything from a credential. Left out when nothing connected (usage error, guard block, failed connect).<br>• **How long:** the connect time in the summary and in JSON (`connect_ms`), and `elapsed_ms` on each JSON batch, so a slow connect and a slow batch can be told apart. Once connections are reused (Phase 5) it shows about 0.<br>• **Contract version:** the JSON document starts with `contract_version` (`major.minor`; the major changes only for an incompatible change, and a minor may add optional fields, event types or enum values). Unlike the outcome fields it needs no buffering, because it's known before anything runs. Text and csv carry none, since it would cost tokens on every call; `--version` reports it instead.<br>• **Constraints:** every variable field goes through the escaper (§5.1), and a bracket-quoted database name may hold control characters; the closing lines must still fit the `--max-output` reserve with the longest possible names; `--help` is 62 lines now, so its FORMAT paragraph has to change without growing. **JSON part and `--version` done 2026-10-11:** every json document starts with `"contract_version":"1.0"`; once connected it carries `connection` (server, database at the start, auth, encrypted, server_version) and `connect_ms`, and each batch ends with `elapsed_ms`, with no extra round trip. Names are escaped and cut at 128 characters, within the ending's reserve (DESIGN.md §4.4). `sqlh --version` prints `sqlh 0.1.0 (json contract 1.0)` (1.1 since 2.15, which added `row_count_exact` and `counting_stopped`; 2.0 since R16, because a stopped count makes `row_count` a lower bound). **Text and csv part done 2026-10-11 (decision 10):** every connected text or csv run ends its status line with `; <server>/<database>, connect N ms` (`-- ok: 1 batch, 241 ms; db01/master, connect 195 ms`); in csv that line is on stderr. Names are escaped and cut at 128 characters like JSON's, a server that reported no name is `?`, and the tail is left out when nothing connected. Its exact length is held back on top of both reserves (`Tally::reserve_ending`) rather than by raising the reserve constants: a usual tail costs the body about 30 bytes, and the widest (1,609 bytes) leaves the smallest cap 1,463 bytes of body. A tail that no longer fits is left out and counted as `1 connection description`. Task 2.11 is done. | DESIGN.md §5 (the version rule, and each format's new fields) and §6 updated in the same change. Exact-output tests for text, json and csv, with and without a connection, with hostile server and database names, and for a run cut by `--max-output`. A repeat of trial 1 shows the database in the first response. How much goes in the text status line is owner decision 10 (§6). **Met, except the trial repeat (2.10):** exact-output tests in every format with and without a connection, with hostile names, names cut at 128 and a run cut by `--max-output`; cap sweeps with the widest tail; live test `the_status_line_names_the_profiles_database`. | M |
| 2.12 | **Write down where messages land, and pin it** (same comparison: Microsoft's JSON docs state what happens to a message that arrives while a result's rows are still streaming; ours says only "wire order"). Today the driver buffers informational messages, and `Session::run` delivers them at statement boundaries, and after the result is closed when its rows end in an error. So a message that arrives mid-result lands after that result, in every format. Nothing states or enforces that: the `BatchSink` docs say only "in wire order", and if a sink's `message`, `error` or `rows_affected` were ever called between `columns` and `end_rows`, the JSON sink would write the event inside the open result's `rows` (`message` doesn't close it) and the document would be invalid.<br>• Replace "wire order" with the rule in DESIGN.md §5.1 and §5.2 and in the `BatchSink` docs.<br>• Have each sink refuse the out-of-order call in tests (a debug assertion or a returned error; release builds must not panic on server-derived input), so a driver upgrade or the Runner refactor (3.1) can't break it silently.<br>• If a query can be found that makes the server send a message between rows, add it as a live test; otherwise the unit tests stand. **Done 2026-10-11:** the rule is in DESIGN.md §5.1 ("Order of events") and §5.2 and in the `BatchSink` docs. Every sink refuses a message, error or row count between `columns` and `end_rows` (`output::OutOfOrder`, exit 10), in every build. No query makes the server send a message between rows; a live test pins the nearest cases. Found on the way: "The statement has been terminated." (an INFO that follows its error on the wire) is written before the error line, because the driver reports a statement's errors only at its end; documented in §5.1. | The rule is in DESIGN.md and the trait docs. Each of the three sinks has a test that fails if a message arrives mid-result. | S |
| 2.13 | **Windows shells and paths** (VISION.md, Compatibility; owner direction, 2026-10-10). cmd.exe and Windows PowerShell 5.1 are the baseline, because every supported Windows version has them, the candidate Server 2016 floor included. Git Bash and PowerShell 7 should work too, but can't be assumed. Checked on 2026-10-10 with the release build:<br>• **Git Bash paths.** From cmd or PowerShell, `-i /c/code/x.sql` fails with "The system cannot find the path specified" and no hint, because Windows reads it as `\c\code\x.sql` on the current drive. When a path in that form isn't found, say so and suggest `C:\code\x.sql`. From Git Bash itself it already works: Git Bash rewrites such arguments for Windows programs.<br>• **Doubled backslashes.** A path in a metadata line comes out doubled (`-- blocked: sub\\b.sql:2: ...`), because `\` is the text format's escape character (DESIGN.md §5.1). Copied back, a doubled local path still works in PowerShell and cmd, but a doubled UNC path doesn't. Whether to change the output is owner decision 11.<br>• **`--help` examples.** The stdin example is a bash heredoc (`sqlh -S myserver -d AppDb <<'SQL'`), which works in neither cmd nor PowerShell. Use forms that work in every shell, such as `-i file.sql` and `-Q "..."`, or show the PowerShell form beside it, without growing the help (62 lines now).<br>• **Piped input in Windows PowerShell 5.1.** It encodes text piped to a program with `$OutputEncoding`, US-ASCII by default, so every non-ASCII character becomes `?` before sqlh sees it: `'DELETE FROM dbo.Café' \| sqlh` arrives as `DELETE FROM dbo.Caf?`. sqlh can't detect that, and a changed literal changes what a query matches, so `--help` and DESIGN.md must steer PowerShell 5.1 users to `-Q` or `-i`, or to setting `$OutputEncoding` to UTF-8 first.<br>• **Also check:** `-i`, `-o` and `SQLH_CONFIG` with forward slashes (`C:/x`), UNC paths, relative paths and spaces; `-Q` quoting in cmd, Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 (embedded double quotes, `%` in cmd, `$` in PowerShell); output captured in PowerShell 5.1 (`$r = sqlh ...`) when the console's code page isn't UTF-8; and whether sqlh should restore the console code page it switches to UTF-8 (`console::use_utf8` never puts it back). **Done 2026-10-11** ([notes/windows-shells.md](notes/windows-shells.md)): a Git Bash path (`/c/dir/f.sql`) that isn't found, for `-i`, `-o` or `SQLH_CONFIG`, is a usage error naming the Windows spellings to use. `--help` has no heredoc any more: its examples run unchanged in cmd.exe, Windows PowerShell 5.1, PowerShell 7 and Git Bash, a test runs them in the Windows shells, and it is held at 62 lines by a test. Piped input that a shell mangled (PowerShell 5.1's ASCII `$OutputEncoding`, cmd.exe's code page) is documented, with the fixes. Every path form, quoting per shell and output captured in PowerShell were checked. sqlh no longer switches the console's code page at all, since it never needed to. Decision 11 (paths keep the escape rule) changed no output: DESIGN.md §5.1 says paths in `-- ` lines follow the rule and input is never escaped, the `--help` FORMAT lines say the escapes cover names and paths, without growing, and `a_path_from_copy_as_path_works_after_i_and_o_in_every_shell` runs Explorer's "Copy as path" spelling after `-i` and `-o` in cmd.exe, Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7 (Git Bash by hand). | Process-level tests for each path form and for the Git Bash hint. Every `--help` example runs as written in cmd.exe and Windows PowerShell 5.1, by a script or a recorded hand check. DESIGN.md §4.2 and §5.1 say what works in which shell. Decision 11 settled, with golden tests if the output changes. **Met:** decision 11 left the output as it was, so a process-level test covers it instead of golden tests. | M |
| 2.14 | **Bound the guard's memory** (found in 2.7). `guard::tokenize` keeps a token of about 56 bytes, with an uppercased copy, per token in one `Vec`, so a 10 MB input of one-character tokens peaks at 795 MB working set (9.9 MB of `SELECT 1` lines at 233 MB). Stream the tokens, or keep only what the classifier needs. **Done 2026-10-11** ([notes/memory.md](notes/memory.md)): the guard reads the input through a streaming lexer and keeps no token list. 10 MB of one-character tokens peaks at 36 MB working set, as without the guard (795 MB before); the worst case, a refusal that quotes a 10 MB name, at 54 MB. Found on the way, and since fixed by R18 (Phase 2 follow-up): nested lookahead (`UPDATE TOP (UPDATE TOP (...`, `sp_spaceused` arguments, dotted names) re-read tokens, quadratic and about 16 times slower than before (20,000 levels deep: 17 s); it is now linear with a work budget (16,000 levels: 3.6 ms). `EXEC` as the last token of the input was reported on line 1; it is now reported on its own line. | Peak memory for a 10 MB input stays within a small multiple of the input; guard unit tests unchanged. **Met:** the guard's tests are unchanged and new lexer tests added; `the_guard_takes_memory_in_proportion_to_the_input` holds four 10 MB inputs under 80 MB (the old guard: 795 MB). | S |
| 2.15 | **Bounded drain (decision 6).** Implement decision 6 from the 2.6 prototype (branch `drain-prototype`, which conflicts with the 2.7 read path and must be redone on it, using the driver's cursor skip, not `drain_rows`): the 250 ms budget and floored `≥N` in read-only runs; exact counting under `--allow-write`, for `XACT_ABORT` input and with a flag; `-t` during the count reports `≥N`, not exit 4; the status line and a JSON batch flag. DESIGN.md §4.4, §5.1–§5.3 and §6, `--help` within its 62 lines, README. **Done 2026-10-11**, redone on the 2.7 read path (the `drain-prototype` branch is superseded):<br>• In read-only runs, the rows past `--max-rows` (and those `--max-results` or `--max-output` hide) are counted for 250 ms from the first hidden row (`db::COUNT_BUDGET`), then the statement is cancelled with an attention. The footer reads `100 of ≥800000 rows shown (--max-rows 100; counting stopped)`: the bound is floored to one significant digit, is at least one more than the rows shown once a hidden row was counted, and is the rows shown when `-t` ended the wait before any hidden row arrived. A result that ends inside the budget stays exact.<br>• The rest of that batch does not run, and the output says so: the footer, a note (advising `--exact-count` for a budget stop and `raise -t` for a `-t` stop), the status line (`ok: 2 batches, counting stopped in 1, 241 ms; ...`, in failed runs too) and JSON, with `row_count_exact` on every result (false also for an interrupted one) and `counting_stopped` on every batch. The JSON contract was 1.1, and is 2.0 since the completion review (R16).<br>• Exact counting for `--allow-write` or a profile whose write_policy is allow, for input whose tokens include `XACT_ABORT` (`guard::mentions_xact_abort`), and with `--exact-count` or the profile key `exact_count`.<br>• `-t` expiring while only hidden rows are being read or awaited reports `≥N` with the run's own exit code. Exit 4 stays with writes allowed, with `XACT_ABORT` input, and while rows that would be shown are awaited (decision 6's clarification, §6, confirmed by the owner 2026-10-11).<br>• A stop the server doesn't acknowledge within the driver's 5 s is exit 6, with a message that names the count. `STATUS_RESERVE` is 640 (was 512): the widest JSON ending is now 561 bytes.<br>• Learned: the driver's `-t` adds up only the time spent waiting in transport reads, so a fast, narrow result ran far past `-t` (7.4 s with `-t 1`). sqlh now keeps `-t` itself (R15).<br>• Not covered by tests: a stop inside a procedure, trigger or cursor, `SET ROWCOUNT`, and an unacknowledged stop (only its message has a unit test). `XACT_ABORT` turned on by a server's `user options` no longer matters: the session setup sets it OFF (R14). Dynamic SQL that sets it is refused by the guard in read-only runs. DESIGN.md §1, §4.2–§4.4, §5.1–§5.3, §6, §8, §14 and notes/drain-experiment.md are updated. | Exact-output tests for the cut footer, note and status in all three formats and for the longer endings within the `--max-output` reserve; live tests for `XACT_ABORT`, `--allow-write`, `GO n`, `--max-output` and Ctrl+C during the cancel. **Met:** exact-output tests in all three formats, also for the clamped bound, a `-t` stop with no hidden row and both kinds of advice; the widest endings in the cap sweeps; live tests for each case listed and for both sides of the `-t` rule. Ctrl+Break during the cancel needs a hold that exists only in debug builds, so that test runs in debug builds only. | M |
| 2.16 | **A refusal under a `deny` profile gives advice that works** (agent trial 2). A blocked write ends "Rerun with --allow-write if the write is intended", also when the profile's `write_policy` is `deny`, where that rerun is a usage error (exit 3). Under `deny`, say instead that writes are disabled for this profile (and nothing else to try). **Done 2026-10-12:** each refusal records whether `--allow-write` lifts it and what to try instead (`guard::Blocked`); under a `deny` profile the advice keeps the alternative and says the profile doesn't allow writes (`-- blocked: line 1: DELETE dbo.T would write; nothing was run. Profile test doesn't allow writes (write_policy = "deny")`). Built with decision 12: `profile add` makes new profiles `deny`. | Exact-output tests for the refusal under each write policy; DESIGN.md §7 updated. **Met:** a unit test for every refusal kind under `deny` and without it; a process test for `profile add` (new profile `deny` with its note, `--write-policy flag` kept, an update keeps the policy) and the refusals under each; DESIGN.md §7 and §8. | S |

#### Phase 2 follow-up (the Phase 2 review)

The review of `5b497bc` closed R06 and R07 and asked for changes before 2.2–2.4 count as complete. All four findings are fixed, each with regression tests that reproduce the reviewer's probes and fail against `b2f8df9`: a refusal printed 20,167 bytes under a 4,096-byte cap (R08), a stalled stderr hung the process (R09), Ctrl+Break during final delivery ended with 0xC000013A (R10), and a reader that kept reading got exit 7 (R11).

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R08 (P1) | `--max-output` bounded only the body. Refusals, notes, closing errors, footers, JSON's tail and the `--verbose` line bypassed it, so a 20,000-character name in a refusal printed about 20 KB under a 4,096-byte cap. | Every byte written to either stream counts, after escaping. Text that doesn't fit (a refusal, notes, the closing error, `--verbose` lines) is shortened with `…[+N]`; the status line and JSON's outcome fields never are. Notes, refusals and diagnostics stop 512 bytes short of the cap (`STATUS_RESERVE`, inside the 1,024-byte reserve), and a test pins that the widest possible ending fits. `--verbose` goes through the output (`Output::diagnostic`). Errors found before the run starts keep to the cap as well. | unit tests per format, including a sweep of 1,800 caps; CLI: a huge refusal, a huge connection error, early errors, `--verbose` |
| R09 (P1) | After a stall on csv's stderr, the exit-7 diagnostic waited for the stalled stream's lock, so sqlh hung instead of exiting 7. | Diagnostics are written by a helper thread that sqlh waits on for at most 0.5 s in all, then abandons; a stream that stalled isn't tried again. Their variable text is escaped and cut at 1,000 characters. The panic hook writes both its lines the same way. `--help` and `--version` go through the delivery thread, so a stalled reader holds them for at most the default stall limit. | CLI: a stalled stderr in csv, stalled stdout and stderr in every format, a panic with stalled streams; unit tests |
| R10 (P1) | Final delivery ran after the runtime and the signal listeners were gone: Ctrl+Break there ended sqlh through Windows (0xC000013A, no diagnostic), and with `-t 0` the wait had no bound. | One runtime and the listeners live from just before execution through the closing lines and `-o` publication (`run_and_deliver`). The final wait is asynchronous (`Delivery::finish_async`, which also takes an absolute bound) and races the listeners: a signal gives the rest 1 s, then the run is exit 7 if it wasn't delivered. The closing lines after a cancel get 1 s in all, not only 1 s without progress. | CLI: Ctrl+Break during final delivery with `-t 0` and `-t 60`, in every format; live: a completed run; unit tests |
| R11 (P2) | The stall clock advanced only when a whole 64 KB chunk was written, so a reader that kept reading slowly could be declared stalled. | The writer thread writes at most 4 KB at a time and restarts the clock after each write; no queued item is larger than a chunk. The cost is about 1–2 ms per MB written, which doesn't show at the default cap. | unit tests: partial writes, chunking, a stalled stream next to a healthy one; CLI: slow readers of stdout and of csv's stderr |

Also from the review:
- **Remote Named Pipes are refused** (answer 1). `np:host`, or a UNC pipe path to another machine, is a usage error before anything connects, and the message gives the TCP spelling to use. Local pipes, `lpc:` and LocalDB stay. A bare IPv6 address, which also fell back to a pipe (23 s for one that doesn't answer), now connects over TCP. Still open: an IPv6 address with an instance name.
- **A name with several addresses** (answer 2): exit 2 when every attempt fails before the login deadline, exit 4 when the deadline expires first, as DESIGN.md §4.2 now says. A test uses `localhost`, which has two addresses here.
- **Exit 7 keeps its precedence after a cancel, and the grace periods stand** (answers 3 and 4). §4.3 gives the end-to-end bound after a signal: the grace, plus 1 s for the closing lines, plus at most 0.5 s for the diagnostic.
- **The password prompt** refuses to run if its echo-restoring handler can't be registered (answer 5).
- **No claim that the server confirmed a cancel.** The driver exposes no receipt for the attention, so a cancelled statement on a connection that is still usable is reported as exactly that.
- **Corrected on the way:** mssql-tds 0.2.0 does drop a pending connect attempt when its handle is cancelled (`run_until_cancelled`), so that upstream issue was withdrawn; its first pipe open is synchronous, but the wait for a busy pipe runs on `spawn_blocking`. The attribution of the login time to password hashing is now labeled an inference ([notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md)).
- **Design debt, as planned:** the queue and memory bounds and the substitution cap, which is checked after the text is built (2.7); a single owner for the run's lifecycle (3.1). R10's `run_and_deliver` is a first step.

Second round: the reviewer's verification of `4b3bbd1` closed R08–R11, R11 with its documented granularity (a reader must take each 4 KB write within `-t`; a slower trickle is a stall, about 68 bytes/s at the default 60 s, so the feature is never described as "however slowly"). It found two P2 follow-ups, both fixed:

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R12 (P2) | In text and json, `--verbose` lines went to stderr through the same thread as stdout, so an open, unread stderr held up the whole response: exit 7 after `-t`, or no end with `-t 0`. R09's 0.5 s bound covered only the last-resort messages. | In text and json, stderr gets a thread of its own (`delivery::Side`) that stdout's thread never waits on. At the end sqlh waits at most 0.5 s for it, then drops what is left. The lines still count against `--max-output`. csv is unchanged: its stderr is the required metadata stream. | unit: a stderr that never returns, with no stall limit, in text and json; `Side` order, prompt finish and errors; live: `--verbose -t 0` with an unread stderr ends with exit 0 and the full response |
| R13 (P2) | The two `-o` publication messages printed the path without escaping, so a file name holding U+2028 split the line. | Paths in diagnostics go through the same escaping and cut as other fields (`shown` uses `brief`), so U+2028 shows as `\u2028`, and a backslash is doubled, as in metadata (decision 11 kept that rule for both). | unit: both messages with a hostile path; CLI: a failed publication to a hostile file name |

Completion review: the review of `3ef8f3c` ("Phase 2 completion review") asked for five changes before Phase 2 sign-off. All are fixed:

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R14 (P1) | The bounded count assumed `XACT_ABORT` OFF, but sqlh never set it, so a server whose `user options` turn it on could have sqlh's own stop roll back the read-only wrapper. | Every session's setup ends with `SET XACT_ABORT OFF`, ahead of `BEGIN TRANSACTION`, read-only and `--allow-write` alike, with no extra round trip. After the last batch the run checks the wrapper: gone with no batch failed is exit 1, not a success. Input that mentions `XACT_ABORT` still counts exactly, with the owner's exit-4 rule. | unit: the exact setup text, the wrapper check; live (debug-only seam `SQLH_DEBUG_SESSION_PREFIX` starts a session with `XACT_ABORT` ON): the setup turns it off, a stop then `GO` keeps `@@TRANCOUNT = 1`, the last-batch case, explicit input still exact |
| R15 (P1) | `-t` was the driver's, which counts only time spent waiting in transport reads: a fast result ran 7.4 s with `-t 1`. | sqlh keeps a monotonic deadline per batch from when it is sent, through every row, value chunk and wait for the reader, and cancels through its own handle. The loop checks the clock as it works and yields every 10 ms, so the timer, the count budget and Ctrl+C run on the single-threaded runtime (3.5 ns a row). Outcomes are unchanged: a user cancel wins (exit 6), exit 4 where rows to show, writes or `XACT_ABORT` input are involved, the owner's hidden-row exception elsewhere. A reader that stalls mid-batch now ends at about twice `-t` (exit 7). The grace is the server's acknowledgement of the attention: 8 to 82 ms measured, at most the driver's 5 s. | unit: a deterministic ready-data loop; live: 10M narrow rows with `-t 1` in every counting mode, a 256 MB visible value, 20M one-row results, the real row loop under a debug-only work seam (`SQLH_DEBUG_ROW_WORK_US`); each within `-t` + 5 s |
| R16 (P2) | Contract 1.1 changed what `row_count` means (a lower bound in a stopped count), which a 1.0 reader would misread as exact. | The JSON contract is 2.0; the fields are 1.1's. DESIGN.md §5.2 says readers reject an unknown major. | golden and version tests |
| R17 (P2) | A result that a SQL error ended was closed as complete, `row_count_exact: true`. | `BatchSink::end_rows_incomplete`, used when a SQL error ends the row stream: JSON writes `interrupted` and `row_count_exact: false`; text and csv end it `-- N rows so far (interrupted)` (or `-- S of ≥N rows shown (--max-rows M; interrupted)`). A later result in the batch, and later batches, stay exact. | exact output in all three formats; the live divide-by-zero regression, with a later result and `--continue-on-error` |
| R18 (P2) | The guard's lookahead re-read the input, quadratic in nesting, before Ctrl+C or `-t` could act: 16,000 levels took 9.6 s. | Lookahead is linear (one pass over `TOP (` clauses, `sp_spaceused` arguments stop at the answer), with a work budget behind it (2M units + 3 per byte, lookahead counted). Past a limit the input is refused before connecting (exit 5) as too complex for the read-only check, with what to do. 16,000 levels: 3.6 ms. A final `EXEC` is reported on its own line. | unit: the budget and the scans; process: the reviewer's nested inputs to 20,000 deep within the bound; the 10 MB memory tests kept |

The verification of `73cc86a` closed R14, R16, R17 and R18 and kept R15 open with two follow-ups, both fixed:

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R15 A | A deadline noticed only at the final statement boundary (the clock is read every 64 shown rows or so, and a final DONE already in the buffer needs no wait) was ignored there: `Session::run` returned success at 1.5 s with `-t 1`. | Where a result ends and where the batch ends, the deadline is checked again (`Drain::overdue`, `Session::finished_late`): past it, a result whose rows to show were still coming, or any result in a run where `-t` may not stop a count, ends the batch with exit 4 and is not closed as complete. The hidden-row exception holds: shown rows complete and hidden rows all counted, in a read-only run, is complete and exact, however late. | `src/db` against a fake TDS server on a loopback socket (adapted from the reviewer's probe), through the real `Session::run`: one row whose sink work crosses `-t` (shown, and hidden with writes allowed) is a timeout and not closed as complete; a normal short result succeeds; the late hidden tail in a read-only run is complete. Removing the checks fails the test. |
| R15 B | The driver settles a cancel in its own time: an attention 5 s, but a request cancelled between two of its packets is withdrawn and its DONE awaited up to 120 s, without the cancel. sqlh bounded none of it. | `settle_within`: the batch's work races `-t` + `SETTLE_GRACE` (5 s). Past it the work is dropped where it stands, the session is retired (`DbError::Lost` for anything more), and the run ends with exit 4, closing the connection. | Unit: a settlement that never ends is cut at the grace (it hangs without the bound). Fake server: a server that never acknowledges the attention, and one that stops reading a 8 MB request part way, each end within `-t` + 5 s. The exact withdrawal state was not reproduced: it needs the cancel between one packet's completed write and the next in the same poll, and a sweep of buffer sizes and runtime holds always met a write in progress, which the driver abandons at once (likely because the runtime resumed the writer before the cancelling timer; an observation here, not a guarantee). The bound does not depend on it, and the verification of `24729ed` accepted this test and closed R15 B. |

The verification of `24729ed` kept R15 A open for one case, fixed:

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| R15 A, second round | The hidden-row exception went by the next row's visibility, not by the phase the deadline passed in: when the last shown row filled `--max-rows`, its late work was taken for a hidden tail, and the result was reported successful and exact (`--max-rows 1`, and row 100 at the default quota). | After each row to show, `count_rows` checks the deadline (`Drain::overdue`) and ends the batch with a timeout there, before the next row's visibility is asked. The exception now covers only time spent after the shown rows were done (hidden rows, the wait for the final DONE). | Fake server, a sink whose quota turns `wants_values` false on its last shown row: at a quota of 1 (read-only exact and bounded) and on row 100 of the default quota, a timeout, not complete; a genuine late hidden tail after the quota is complete in a read-only run and a timeout with writes allowed. The real JSON sink, end to end at quotas 1 and 100: `interrupted`, `row_count_exact:false`, `ok:false`. Both fail without the fix. |

Watched, not fixed (owner decision, 2026-10-12): two process tests of output delivery, `a_stalled_stderr_in_csv_still_exits_7` and `a_stalled_stdout_is_reported_on_stderr`, assert completion under 5 s and went over it (5.17 to 5.33 s) in the reviewer's parallel runs, with the right exit 7; they pass alone and in our runs. Not worth the effort now. Watch them as changes land: if they fail again, or more often, find what makes them load-sensitive before widening the bound.

Still open from the review, scheduled with Phase 3: portable live fixtures (some live tests read `StackOverflow2010`, AGENTS.md lists it), counting inside procedures, cursors and `SET ROWCOUNT`, the real path of an unacknowledged attention, and the legacy `text`/`ntext`/`image` types, which are still read whole (DESIGN.md §11).

### Phase 3: execution core, CI and fixtures (the enabler for Phases 4 and 5)

**Status (2026-10-15): done.** 3.1–3.4 were accepted after the R20–R24 fixes (Phase 3 fix verification, below); 3.5 and 3.6 ran on GitHub on 2026-10-15 and met their acceptance (in the table). What remains is release-time work on the first-release checklist (§7): SmartScreen and antivirus on a locked-down workstation, and the other §7 items.

|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | **Extract a library and a `Runner`** with `RunPolicy` (guard, wrapper, check mode), `QueryOrigin` (user SQL vs generated SQL; generated SQL skips `$(var)` and the classifier but not the wrapper) and `RunOutcome` (exit code and counts, independent of the sink). Profile commands stop importing from `main.rs`. **Done 2026-10-12:** `src/lib.rs` holds every module; `src/main.rs` only calls `sqlh::main`. `src/app.rs` is the command (arguments, delivery, the closing lines, `-o`, the panic net); `src/run.rs` the execution core. `RunPolicy` (guard checks, wrapper, `--check`, `--continue-on-error`) is decided once in `prepare` and read by the session loop. `QueryOrigin::Generated` skips `$(var)` and the guard but keeps the wrapper; `sqlh profile test` uses it, ahead of Phase 4's schema commands. `RunOutcome` carries the result with the error and stopped-count tallies, so the exit code and status line no longer read the sink. (Not quite, the Phase 3 review found: the tallies were still read from the sink's `Tally`, and the core still took `cli::Opts` and `app::Out`. The design follow-ups below made it so.) Profile commands import from `app`, not `main`. | No behavior change: all existing tests and the smoke run pass. **Met:** every existing test unchanged and passing (unit, process, live on debug and release), smoke exit 1 with its two errors; new unit tests for the policy, the origin and the outcome. | L |
| 3.2 | **Injectable stores** for config, credentials and output. Then the F12 work: a lock around profile edits, unique temp files (`files::Partial` from the R01 fix fits), and credential/config coordination; deletion errors already surface. And F14: wipe sqlh's own password copies with `zeroize`, already in the dependency tree, and document what the driver keeps. **Done 2026-10-12:**<br>• **F12:** `profile add` and `rm` hold `profiles.toml.lock` (no sharing; another edit is waited for 10 s) from reading the file to writing it; the file is written whole through `files::replace` (a `.partial` file of the run's own; the rename migration uses it too; unique `.partial` files keep parallel first runs from colliding, but not from overwriting each other, which R21 fixed with the lock). A new password is stored first and put back (or deleted) if the save fails; an old one is deleted only after the save; a failed delete in `rm` is finished by running `rm` again. (Not atomic: a run reading between the two writes, or after a kill there, could pair the new password with the old profile until R20 bound each password to its profile's identity.) The file, the passwords and the console sit behind a `Store` trait in `profile.rs`, so tests can fail any step.<br>• **F14:** every password copy sqlh holds is a `Secret` (`zeroize::Zeroizing<String>`, wiped with its spare capacity on drop): the prompt's line (read without reallocating), Credential Manager's copy, `SQLCMDPASSWORD`'s and the connection settings'. The driver's own copy (`ClientContext.password`, a plain `String` it keeps for the connection's life), the console and stdin buffers and the environment can't be wiped; DESIGN.md §8 says so. | Failure-injection tests. **Met:** seven `profile.rs` tests through a failing store (a failed save puts the old password back or leaves none; a failed undo says how to repair; a password that can't be stored changes nothing; Windows auth deletes the old password only after the save; a failed removal is finished by rerunning it; an edit without the lock changes nothing), and `files.rs` tests of the real lock and `replace`. | M |
| 3.3 | **`scripts/check.ps1`**, a local release check: fmt, clippy, tests, `--locked` release build, size budget (3 MB, owner 2026-10-12; 2.41 MB today), and `dumpbin` imports. It becomes CI once there's a remote. **Done 2026-10-12:** `scripts/check.ps1` runs, stopping at the first failure: `cargo metadata --locked` (Cargo.lock is current; R23 added it and `--locked` on every later cargo command, so no step can repair a stale lockfile); `cargo fmt --check`; clippy with `-D warnings` without and with `--features timing`; `cargo test`; `cargo build --release`; `cargo test --release --test cli`; the size budget (3 MB; 2,419,200 bytes now); and `dumpbin /dependents` against the list of ten in-box DLLs (found through `vswhere`). `-Live` adds the live tests on debug and release through `SQLH_TEST_PROFILE` (default `test`). About 90 s with `-Live`. | One command, non-zero on any failure. **Met:** a formatting error stops it at step 1 with exit 1. | S |
