Add disposable fixtures (sequence, procedure, trigger, FK, filtered index) in the scratch database `sqlh_test` (§6.5). **Done 2026-10-12:**<br>• **Golden smoke:** `the_smoke_script_matches_its_golden_output` runs `tests/smoke.sql` in text and JSON and compares the whole output with `tests/golden/` after normalizing times, the server's name and version and the database's name; `SQLH_BLESS=1` rewrites the files after a deliberate change. The script's server-dependent query (the first rows of `sys.all_objects`) is now a 150-row series.<br>• **Synthetic data:** the bounded-count and deadline tests read a cross join of `sys.all_columns` (7-digit values, about 125,000 rows per MB, like `Votes`' ids) instead of StackOverflow2010, so the suite needs only `sqlh_test`.<br>• **Fixtures** (`Objects`, made after the scratch-marker check and dropped however the test ends): the F03 shapes refused end to end with the sequence unchanged; a write reaching a filtered index and firing a trigger, and a foreign key's error 547 mapped to its line; a procedure's large result counted exactly under `--allow-write`; a cursor's FETCHes past `--max-results`; `SET ROWCOUNT`. F04 already had `check_never_executes`.<br>• **Unacknowledged count stop,** through the fake TDS server: rows of a result in progress, then silence; the run ends with `COUNT_STOP_UNCONFIRMED` after the driver's 5 s.<br>• **The low-privilege login** (decision 5): `Reader` makes a login with only `db_datareader` and `VIEW DEFINITION`; it reads, its write is refused by the guard, and with `--allow-write` by the server (error 229).<br>• **Cleanup:** `sqlh_test` uses SIMPLE recovery, and `tests/fixtures/shrink_sqlh_test.sql` gives back space an experiment took (1.49 GB to 136 MB on 2026-10-12). | `SQLH_TEST_PROFILE=test cargo test --test cli -- --ignored live` checks real server behavior, including the F03 and F04 regressions. **Met:** 48 live tests on debug (42 on release, where the debug-only seams are absent). | M |
| 3.5 | **GitHub Actions CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), added 2026-10-10 at the owner's request and modeled on ssms-executor's `maintenance-ci.yml`:<br>• Runs on pushes to `main` and on pull requests, on `windows-latest`, with `permissions: contents: read`.<br>• Checks out and runs `scripts/check.ps1` (3.3), so CI and the local check can't drift. The Rust toolchain comes from `rust-toolchain.toml`.<br>• Runs no live tests (there is no server in CI) and holds no secrets, so forks and PRs can run it.<br>• Pins action versions, as ssms-executor does.<br>**Written 2026-10-12, first run 2026-10-15:** `ci.yml` checks out (`actions/checkout@v7`), runs `rustup show` so the pinned toolchain and its components install, and runs `pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/check.ps1`. Its first run is the first push. Watch for differences on the runner: `windows-latest` has Visual Studio 2022 (check.ps1 finds `dumpbin` through `vswhere`, any version), and the Ctrl+Break and harness-kill tests have no console of their own there. | A push or PR runs the same checks as `scripts/check.ps1` and fails when any of them does. **Met 2026-10-15:** the first push ran CI ([run 38672627149](https://github.com/tkwj/sqlh/actions/runs/38672627149)) on the `windows-2025-vs2026` image, and `check.ps1` passed in 740 s (12 min 44 s for the job, a cold build): 319 unit tests (four ignored), 62 debug and 59 release process tests (49 and 43 live ones ignored), a 2,488,832-byte exe (1 KB more than the local build) and the same ten in-box imports. The runner has Visual Studio 2026, not 2022; `vswhere` found `dumpbin` either way, and nothing needed changing. | S |
| 3.6 | **Signed release workflow** (`.github/workflows/release.yml`), added 2026-10-10 at the owner's request and modeled on ssms-executor's `dotnetfx.yml` ([workflows](https://github.com/tkwj/ssms-executor/tree/master/.github/workflows)). The pattern to copy:<br>• Triggers on pushes to `main` and `workflow_dispatch`, with `permissions: id-token: write` and `contents: write`.<br>• Gates every signing and publishing step on `github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' && github.repository_owner == 'tkwj'` and a push or dispatch event, so pull requests and forks never reach the signing identity.<br>• Takes the version from the run number (`0.1.${{ github.run_number }}`; ssms-executor uses `3.1.`) and puts it in the exe, so `sqlh --version` matches the release. Stamping `Cargo.toml` would break `--locked`, because the lockfile records the root package's version. Pass the version at build time instead, for example an environment variable read with `option_env!`.<br>• Signs in to Azure with `azure/login` through an OIDC federated credential (secrets `AZURE_CLIENT_ID`, `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`; no client secret). Then it runs `dotnet tool install --global sign --prerelease` and `sign code artifact-signing sqlh.exe` against the owner's existing Artifact Signing account and certificate profile (ssms-executor uses account `tkwj`, profile `tkwj-github`, endpoint `https://eus.codesigning.azure.net`).<br>• Generates a third-party notices file from the dependencies' license fields (`cargo-about`, pinned) and zips it with the exe and sqlh's `LICENSE`, since MIT and BSD require their notices in binary distributions. Uploads the workflow artifact, and runs `gh release create v<version> ... --draft --generate-notes`, so the owner reviews each release and publishes it by hand.<br>Where sqlh differs: there is no gallery step, because the GitHub release is the publication (AGENTS.md). The build is `cargo build --release --locked`, and the 3.3 checks (size budget, `dumpbin /dependents` imports in-box only) run before signing. Signing changes the exe, so the signature is verified (`signtool verify /pa /v`) and a SHA-256 file is computed after it, and both ship in the release.<br>**Owner-only setup:** the GitHub repository and the remote; the three secrets; a federated credential for the new repository on the Azure app registration (its subject names the repository and branch); and a check that the existing certificate profile covers signing an exe.<br>**Settled (owner, 2026-10-12, decision 8):** releases are built only on `workflow_dispatch` and on version tags (`v0.1.0`), not on every push to `main`, which moves often before release. The version comes from the tag (`v0.1.0` gives `sqlh 0.1.0`), passed to the build through an environment variable read with `option_env!`, not from the run number; a dispatch build without a tag is marked as a pre-release build in `--version`.<br>**Written 2026-10-12, first run 2026-10-15:** `release.yml`, four jobs, so no code from the build (dependencies, build scripts, the exe itself) runs where the signing identity or write access can be reached (R22). `build` (`contents: read`) does the version, check, the unsigned exe's `--version` check and notices, and uploads the unsigned exe and the notices. `sign` (`id-token: write`, `contents: read`, in the GitHub environment `release`) downloads them (`download-artifact@v8`, which fails on a digest mismatch), signs, verifies the signature and packages, treating the exe as data. `verify` (`contents: read`) runs the signed exe and checks the sums. `publish` (`contents: write`, no Azure) drafts the release:<br>• **Version:** a tag must equal `v` plus `Cargo.toml`'s version, or the run fails; the version is then that (`0.1.0`). A dispatch builds `<Cargo.toml version>-dev.<run number>` (`0.1.0-dev.7`) and makes a pre-release. It reaches the exe as `SQLH_VERSION`, read by `option_env!` in `app.rs` (and by the version tests in `tests/cli.rs`); a change to it rebuilds the crate (checked locally).<br>• **Check:** `scripts/check.ps1`, so the release exe has passed the 3.3 checks, size budget and imports included.<br>• **Notices:** `cargo-about` 0.9.2 (installed `--locked --features cli`) builds `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.html` from `about.toml` (accepted licences; the Windows target only; no build or dev dependencies) and the `about.hbs` template, with `--fail`. Locally on 2026-10-12: 109 crates, all accepted (106 MIT, 2 BSD-3-Clause, 1 Unicode-3.0).<br>• **Sign:** `azure/login@v3` with the three secrets, `sign code artifact-signing build/sqlh.exe` with ssms-executor's account, profile and endpoint, then `signtool verify /pa /v` (the newest under Windows Kits). The signed exe's `--version` is checked in `verify`, never here.<br>• **Package:** `dist/` holds `sqlh.exe`, `sqlh-<version>-windows-x64.zip` (the exe, `LICENSE`, the notices) and `SHA256SUMS.txt` over both; uploaded as a workflow artifact.<br>• **Draft:** `gh release create v<version> --draft --generate-notes` (`--prerelease --target <sha>` for a dispatch) with the three files.<br>• **Gate:** `sign` runs only in `tkwj`'s repository for a tag or for `main`, and `verify` and `publish` only after it. A fork, or a dispatch on another branch, runs `build` alone: its artifact is the unsigned exe and the notices.<br>• **No script injection:** no `${{ }}` expression appears inside a `run:` script; the ref, run number and commit reach the scripts through `env:`. The tag comparison is case-sensitive (`V0.1.0` fails).<br>Tested locally: the version step (a mismatched tag fails, a matching tag and a dispatch give the right versions), the signtool lookup, the package step, and the notices. Untested until the remote: the YAML itself, Azure login and signing.<br>**Owner setup before the first release:** create the environment `release` in the repository, with deployment branches and tags limited to `main` and `v*`. On the Azure app registration, add a federated credential with the subject `repo:tkwj/sqlh:environment:release` (GitHub's immutable subject, with the owner's and repository's ids; the first run showed the name-only form fails, see below); the job runs in that environment, so tag and dispatch runs share the subject. Add the three secrets, to the repository or the environment. | A tag `v<Cargo.toml version>` produces a draft release holding a signed `sqlh.exe`, its zip and SHA-256 sums. `signtool verify /pa /v` passes on it, and `sqlh --version` shows the release version. A dispatch on `main` produces a draft pre-release `v<version>-dev.<run>`. A fork or another branch signs and publishes nothing. **Met 2026-10-15:** the owner created the repository, the `release` environment (deployment rules: branch `main`, tags `v*`; made with `gh`), its three secrets and the federated credential. The first dispatch on `main` ([run 38673994989](https://github.com/tkwj/sqlh/actions/runs/38673994989)) built in 20 min, then failed at the Azure login (AADSTS700213): the repository, new in 2026, uses GitHub's immutable OIDC subject (`use_immutable_subject: true`), `repo:tkwj/sqlh:environment:release`, with the owner's and repository's numeric ids, and the credential named the old form. The owner replaced it with an "Other issuer" credential with that exact subject (safer: a renamed or re-created repository doesn't match it), and the rerun of the failed jobs passed: `sign`, `verify` (6 s) and `publish` (27 s) made the draft pre-release `v0.1.0-dev.1` (no tag is created until it is published). Checked locally: `SHA256SUMS.txt` matches both assets; `Get-AuthenticodeSignature` says Valid, signer `CN=Thomas Willwerth` (issuer Microsoft ID Verified CS EOC CA 04, a three-day certificate), timestamped by the Microsoft Public RSA Time Stamping Authority, so the signature outlives the certificate; `sqlh --version` prints `sqlh 0.1.0-dev.1 (json contract 2.1)`; the zip holds the same exe, `LICENSE` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.html`. | M |

### Phase 3 initial review, October 12, 2026

Reviewed `bdf87d2..cfc8862` at `cfc88629fb3f748ffcf10709dc5a7bd57d3a0edd`. Phase 3 is not accepted yet. The execution-core extraction had no observed CLI/output/cancellation regression. The local `scripts/check.ps1` passed: 281 unit tests, 57 debug process tests, 54 release process tests; release size 2,422,272 bytes and the expected ten Windows DLLs. License notices, workflow version selection, zip contents, and SHA-256 sums passed locally. Live tests, Credential Manager round trips, hosted CI, Azure signing, and publishing were not independently run in this review; hosted acceptance still needs the remote and owner setup.

R19's documentation fix in `bdf87d2` is verified and closed: DESIGN sections 7 and 8, decision 12, and help state that password prompting is conditional and policy changes can run unattended. The existing R15 regression tests passed; its previously accepted closure and limitations remain in force.

The following findings were raised by the initial review. Their fixes and verification are recorded below, without depending on the local-only review files.

| # | Finding and evidence | Required change | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| R20 (P1) | `profile add` writes the replacement credential before publishing the file, while query resolution reads the file and credential without the edit lock. An in-memory probe using production `add` and `resolve_with` resolved the old server/login with the replacement password intended for the new server. Termination between the two writes leaves that mismatch persistent; ordinary save-error compensation cannot repair a terminated process. F12 remains incomplete. | Preserve a coherent server/login/password identity and generation across readers and edits, and recover or fail closed after interruption. A reader lock must span both profile loading and credential acquisition; it alone does not solve crash recovery. Apply the identity rule to migration and qualify DESIGN section 8's atomicity claim. | Query between credential storage and file publication, and across an edit between file loading and password acquisition: matching identity/password pair or safe failure. Kill the editor before file publication, then resolve in a fresh process: no replacement password sent to the previous server. Existing compensation tests retained. |
| R21 (P2) | Migration runs outside `profiles.toml.lock` and checks for an existing new file only before reading the old one. A real-executable probe paused one migration before publication, let another process migrate and save a new profile, then resumed the first: the saved profile disappeared. Unique temporary files avoid collisions but do not prevent this overwrite. | Serialize migration and edits on the same lock, recheck preconditions under it, and hold it through credential migration and publication. Factor already-locked paths to avoid recursive acquisition. Correct 3.2 and DESIGN section 8's race/overwrite claims when fixed. | Deterministic migration-versus-edit tests preserve additions, policy changes, and removals. Two simultaneous first loads converge safely. Sequential migration and SQLH_CONFIG behavior retained. |
| R22 (P1) | The signing job executes the downloaded `build/sqlh.exe --version` after Azure login, with OIDC and repository-write permissions. Dependency or build-script code embedded in that artifact can execute with signing-job privileges, defeating the stated two-job isolation. Source inspection; no hosted exploit or signing run. | Check executable behavior/version in the read-only build job, or run the signed artifact in a separate read-only job with no OIDC, signing environment, or Azure login. Keep the privileged job's treatment of product artifacts limited to signature verification and packaging as data. Moving execution earlier in the same privileged job is insufficient. | No product artifact execution in the privileged job. Any signed-executable smoke test runs without signing/write privileges. Complete hosted signing/draft-release acceptance after owner setup. |
| R23 (P2) | Clippy and tests run unlocked before the locked release build. Offline in a scratch copy, a stale Cargo.lock failed a direct locked build, was repaired by unlocked Clippy, then passed the locked release build. A green check therefore does not establish that the committed lockfile was used unchanged. | Enforce locked resolution before any check can repair Cargo.lock, and consistently protect Clippy, tests, and workflow preparation. | The complete check fails on a stale scratch-copy lockfile without modifying it; a current lockfile passes both feature sets, tests, and release checks. |
| R24 (P2) | `Reader::create` constructs its cleanup guard after asserting the login/user/grant batch succeeded. A partial setup failure leaves the login/user behind. `Objects::make` registers cleanup after asserting success, and `Probe::create` has the same late-guard pattern. Cleanup failures are discarded. Source inspection; no deliberate failure injected into the owner's server. | Arm ownership and idempotent cleanup before setup writes. Clean up partial setup and report failures with repair identifiers, without passwords or a second panic during unwinding. Keep the marker check before writes. | Inject failure after login creation and after an object CREATE commits but before setup reports success. Remove owned state or explicitly report cleanup failure. A fake runner can verify ordering without real server writes. |

Design follow-ups before Phases 4 and 5: `run` still depends on `app::Out`/CLI input and sink-owned accounting; generated SQL retains the normal wrapper policy, so an `allow` profile removes the wrapper despite the stronger comment; the unbounded password `read_line` can exceed its 1,024-byte initial capacity despite the no-reallocation claim; Phase 4's “Human-set only” description premise must reflect R19. These are recorded scope/wording debts, not additional reproduced query regressions.

The fixes in `e89f808`, verified below (the design follow-ups above are not taken up here):

| # | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| R20 | Each stored password is bound to what it was stored for: the credential's user name is the login, and its comment carries the profile's identity (`config::identity`: a 64-bit FNV-1a hash of server, case folded, login, `encrypt` and `trust_server_certificate`). A run sends a stored password only when it fits the profile it read (`config::fits`: same login and, when present, same identity; a pre-R20 credential is checked by login). One that doesn't fit is never sent: the run takes the profiles lock, waiting for an edit under way, and reads both again (`resolve_with`'s second attempt); still no fit means an interrupted edit, and the run fails with exit 3 and `run: sqlh profile add NAME`. `profile add` keeps a password only when it fits the profile as saved, so the repair asks for it again rather than rebinding the replacement to the old server; a kept password whose identity changes is stored again with the new binding; a failed save puts the previous password back with its binding. The migration binds each moved password to its profile's identity, keeping the old credential's login. DESIGN.md §8 now says the file and the password are not atomic, and what is guaranteed instead. Not solved: a lock held by readers for the whole read (the optimistic read and the locked re-read make it unnecessary), and legacy credentials left mismatched by an older sqlh with the same login. | `profile.rs`, production `add` and `resolve_with` against the fake store: a run during an edit's two writes, for a server, login, encryption and password-only change (a safe failure, or the same identity); an edit between a run's two reads (read again under the lock, the edit's pair); an edit killed by a panic between its writes (a fresh run refuses; the repair prompts; then the new pair); a kept password rebound to new encryption; a legacy credential checked by login. Existing compensation tests kept. `config.rs` (on request, `--ignored credman`): the same refusal with the real Credential Manager; `credman.rs`: the binding's round trip and the moved credential's binding. |
| R21 | The migration runs under `profiles.toml.lock` and checks again under it that it is due (`load_at`, `migrate_from_sqlharness`), through the credential moves and the file's publication. Edits and a run's locked re-read load with `load_locked`, which migrates without taking the lock again. The old file's password now replaces one under the new name (that file describes the profile). 3.2's and DESIGN.md §8's race claims are corrected. | `config.rs`, real files and the real lock in a temp folder, for an addition, a policy change and a removal: a migration that finds the move done keeps the edit; an edit started during a migration waits and lands on top; a first load that finds the move due while an edit holds the lock waits, then reads the edit, and the file keeps it. Two first loads at once move the password once and read the same profiles. No old file, or a `SQLH_CONFIG` file, moves nothing. |
| R22 | `release.yml` has four jobs. `build` (read-only) builds, checks and runs the unsigned exe's `--version`. `sign` (`id-token: write`, `contents: read`, environment `release`) signs, verifies the signature with `signtool` and packages the exe as data; it runs nothing it downloaded. `verify` (read-only) runs the signed exe's `--version`, verifies the signature again and checks `SHA256SUMS.txt`. `publish` (`contents: write`, no Azure) creates the draft release from the verified artifact, running nothing from it. | Source; the verify step's sum check run locally against a packaged `dist/`. Hosted signing and the draft release still need the remote and the owner setup. |
| R23 | `check.ps1` starts with `cargo metadata --locked` and passes `--locked` to every cargo command that resolves dependencies (clippy, tests, the release build, the live tests); the release workflow's `cargo metadata` too. | A scratch copy with `Cargo.toml` at 0.1.1 and the committed `Cargo.lock`: the check fails at step 1 (exit 1) and the lockfile's hash is unchanged. The full check passes on the current lockfile. |
| R24 | The fixtures run their SQL through a `Server` (the test profile, `Live`, or a fake). A `Cleanup` holds idempotent steps (`DROP ... IF EXISTS`, `IF SUSER_ID(...) IS NOT NULL DROP LOGIN`), each armed before the SQL that makes its object, and only after the scratch-marker check; it runs them in reverse order when dropped, and reports a failed step with the SQL that finishes it (never a password, never a panic). `Probe`, `Objects` and `Reader` all use it; `Live::write` can't panic. | Against the fake server: a reader whose login batch fails after the login exists is cleaned up (the login's drop, then the probe's table, no password in it); an object whose CREATE fails is dropped with the rest in reverse order, and a failed drop is reported while the others still run; without the scratch marker nothing is written or dropped. The live tests pass with the new fixtures. |

### Phase 3 fix verification, October 12, 2026

The review's design follow-ups, done 2026-10-12 and accepted in the October 13 review below:

| Follow-up | Change | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| `run` depends on `app::Out`, CLI input and sink-owned accounting | `run.rs` imports neither `cli` nor `app`. It takes a `run::Request` (input as `Input::Query`, `Files` or `Stdin`; `$(var)` values or none; the terminator; `--check`; `--continue-on-error`; where the connection line goes, `Report`), which `app::request` builds from the flags, keeping the `-Q`/`-i` refusal and its place in the order of checks. It writes to any `&mut dyn Output` and waits on any `&dyn db::Backpressure` (the CLI passes its `Stream` and that stream's gate). The run counts for itself (`Accounting`, through `Counted`, a `BatchSink` that forwards every event to the output): SQL errors, each batch's worst severity (which decides whether the batch failed) and the batches with a stopped count; `RunOutcome` takes them from there. The output's `Tally` no longer counts errors, and `Output::tally()` is gone, so there is one source for the exit code. | `run.rs`: errors counted once the output takes them (shown, or withheld by the budget), not when it refused one out of order; two stops in a batch are one stopped batch; which batches failed. `app.rs`: the status line from given counts. The process-level and live tests, unchanged, pin the exit codes and status lines end to end. |
| Generated SQL loses the wrapper under an `allow` profile | `RunPolicy::of` gives sqlh's own SQL (`QueryOrigin::Generated`) `allow_write: false` whatever the profile says, so it always runs in the wrapper transaction, with read-only counting. DESIGN.md §8 says so for `profile test`. | `run.rs`: generated SQL under a profile that allows writes keeps the wrapper; the user's SQL under it doesn't. |
| The password's `read_line` can outgrow its 1,024-byte buffer | `console::read_line_within` reads through `take(1024)` into a buffer allocated once at that size, so the read can't grow it; a longer line is refused (SQL Server allows 128 characters); invalid UTF-8 is refused with the buffer wiped. DESIGN.md §8 says what is true now. | `console.rs`: the buffer keeps its capacity, the line break is dropped, a line at the limit is read and a longer one refused, the last line without a break, invalid UTF-8. |
| Phase 4's "Human-set only" premise after R19 | 4.1 now says a description is set by whoever runs `profile add`, a person or an agent, and is data, never instructions. | Wording |

### Phase 4: focused schema discovery (VISION priority 3a)

Build `docs/schema-commands.md` on top of the Phase 3 `Runner`, with the review's corrections:
- fallback resolution never discards an explicit schema, and is defined for case-sensitive databases
- alias types resolved to their base type
- `#temp` only within sessions
- an explicit marker for metadata that is unsupported or hidden, rather than a bare NULL
- `describe` and `def` get a high default limit under the response budget, not unlimited
- framing that survives control characters in definitions

Validate on SQL Server 2025, and keep 2016 portability cheap but untested. (L)

**Built 2026-10-13, all but `deps`; R27–R33 closed in the October 14, 2026 fix verification below; `deps` built and accepted 2026-10-14, with nonblocking documentation follow-up R34 below** (schema-commands.md, "As built"; DESIGN.md §5.1 "Sections", §5.2 `label`, §9):
- **The run core** gained a command input: `run::Input::Command { name, plan }`, where `Plan` is steps known in advance or a lookup whose rows decide them (`Plan::Lookup`), run on the same connection inside the wrapper, shown nowhere, its errors a failure. Commands are `QueryOrigin::Generated`, so read-only whatever the profile. Boxing the session's future keeps the debug build within the main thread's 1 MB stack.
- **Output sections:** `output::Section` and `Output::set_sections`. Text: `-- label`, a names-only header with the first row, empty sections on a `-- no rows:` line, a footer only for what wasn't shown, `def` as a block framed by its line count with escaped lines and whole-line cuts under `--max-output`. JSON: `label` on result events (contract 2.1). CSV: the label in the stderr marker.
- **`src/schema.rs`:** the six commands; `N'...'` literals only; bracket-escaped patterns; the type expression with alias types' base; the lookup and its errors; per-type sections; `(hidden)` for definitions the login can't see; default limits for `describe` (2,000 rows, 4,000 characters) and `def` (no cell limit). `info` shares `profile test`'s query.
- **The review's corrections:** an explicit schema is never set aside, and the fallback is case-insensitive (defined for case-sensitive databases); alias types show their base type; `#temp` is refused before connecting; hidden metadata is `(hidden)`, not NULL; `describe` and `def` have high defaults under the response budget; `def` framing survives control characters.
- **CLI and help:** the first positional word names a command; `--text` for `find`; `-Q`/`-i` and `--check` don't combine with one. `--help` has a SCHEMA section and a `describe` example, still 62 lines.
- **Tests:** `schema.rs` (parsing, literals, patterns, resolution and its errors, per-type sections, `def` refusals, limits); text, json and csv exact output for sections, the definition block and its cut; CLI parsing; process: refusals before connecting, and the help example in every shell; live (`schema_commands_describe_what_the_catalog_holds`): a fixture with every feature, each command, both resolutions and the ambiguity, `def`'s block, `find --text`, `tables`, JSON labels.

**Evidence from agent trial 1** ([notes/agent-trial-1.md](notes/agent-trial-1.md)): five of eleven calls went to finding one system view's columns, including an invented view name. `describe` should cover system views and DMVs (`sys.dm_db_partition_stats`), not only user tables, so this costs one call.

**Also in this phase: profile descriptions and search** (owner, 2026-10-10, after comparing sqlh with the earlier sql-query-runner skill, which has both and uses them to pick a server). Finding out *which server* is the first step of many turns, and it costs context like any other discovery. It is independent of the schema commands, so it can ship ahead of them. (S)

| # | Task | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | **`description` profile key.** Optional, one line, up to 300 characters (environment, purpose, sensitivity: "prod reporting replica, safe for ad-hoc"). Set with `sqlh profile add NAME --description "..."`; a re-run that leaves it out keeps the saved value, like other options. Optional in the file so existing profiles keep working. Set by whoever runs `profile add`: a person, or an agent with a shell, since an edit that needs no password runs unattended (R19). So it is data, never instructions: shown escaped like any field, and trusted no more than the profile's other fields. **Done 2026-10-12:** `Profile::description`; `config::check_description` (at most 300 characters, counted as characters; no control character, tab included, no U+2028 or U+2029, and, after the October 13 review, no Bidi_Control character) is applied by the parser, with the line in the error, and by `profile add` before anything is read or written; `""` in the file is no description, and `--description ""` removes one. The key is written first in its table. `--description` on a run is a usage error, like `--write-policy`. | Parser tests (known key, over-length, repeated key, escapes); `profile add` keeps and replaces it. **Met:** `config.rs` (the key, 300 and 301 characters, 300 two-byte ones, `\"` and `\\` decoded, `\n` and `\t` refused, repeated, not a string, the round trip with a hostile one); `profile.rs` (kept by an edit without it, replaced, removed by `""`, a bad one refused before the lock); `cli.rs` (the flag); process: saved by `profile add`, refused with a tab, refused on a run. |
| 4.2 | **`profile list` shows it, and `profile list --search TEXT` filters.** A case-insensitive substring match over the name and the description. It never reads a password, as today. Every variable field goes through the text escaper like any metadata field (DESIGN.md §5.1), including in json. **Done 2026-10-12:** `description` is the last column (`NULL` when unset). `profile list --search TEXT` lowercases the text, the name and the description with Unicode's rules; the status line says `N of M profiles match(es)` and never echoes the text. The rows are a result set, escaped like any. **Text only (owner decision 13, 2026-10-13, after R25):** `profile list` briefly took `--format json|csv`, whose json put the result in a batch without the metadata contract 2.0 promises; the owner dropped the other formats rather than give a setup command a document shape of its own, so `--format` is now refused there. | Exact-output tests for text and json, including a hostile description; a search that matches only the description; no match gives an empty list, not an error. **Met** in text (json dropped, decision 13): `profile_list_shows_descriptions_and_searches_them` (a description with quotes, a backslash, `-- note:` and non-ASCII, exactly; a search in another case that matches only the description, one by name, and one that matches nothing: header, `-- 0 rows`, exit 0; `--format json` refused with exit 3); `cli.rs` (the flag, and anything else refused). |
| 4.3 | **Docs.** DESIGN.md §8 gains the key, the flag and the output shape; `--help` stays under about 60 lines. **Done 2026-10-12:** DESIGN.md §8 (the key and its rule, `--description` on `profile add`, `profile list`'s columns, `--search` and status line, text only, and decision 12's answer); `--help`'s two PROFILES lines carry `--description TEXT` and `--search TEXT` at 62 lines, 109 characters at most (`-C` stays on its own CONNECTION line); the README's tally moves the item to Built. | DESIGN.md §8 and `--help` match the code. **Met.** |

### Schema commands initial review, October 14, 2026

Reviewed `b5fbea6..14a8e7b`: `info`, `dbs`, `tables`, `describe`, `find`, `def`, command lookup, sections, limits and JSON 2.1. **Changes requested: R27–R33, all P2; this increment is not yet accepted.** The accepted profile-discovery increment and R25/R26 remain closed. `deps` is outside this review.

| # | Finding and evidence | Required change | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| R27 (P2) | `schema.rs:345–358, 415` treats NULL qualifier parts from PARSENAME as absent, but a supplied invalid part can also be NULL. Only the object part is validated, and the lookup does not return the schema part. A 129-unit explicit schema can broaden into the any-schema fallback; invalid database/server parts can lose their scope as well. The isolated resolver and release-exe TDS probes accepted the corresponding NULL-part/candidate replies as `dbo.p`, exit 0. SQL-side behavior is inferred from the generated predicates and Microsoft's PARSENAME/OBJECT_ID documentation, not independently executed on SQL Server here. | Validate the full multipart input, distinguish invalid supplied parts from omitted parts, and account for per-part UTF-16 limits and delimiters before accepting candidates. Invalid syntax exits 3 and never broadens resolution. | Real SQL Server cases with invalid 129-unit schema/database/server qualifiers naming an otherwise visible object all exit 3 without an object result. Retain valid brackets, double quotes, embedded dots, doubled closing delimiters, supported omitted-part syntax and explicit scope. |
| R28 (P2) | `schema.rs:354, 428`: SQL filters the database part against DB_NAME() under SQL collation; Rust subsequently uses ASCII-only case folding. In a CS database the SQL filter can suppress the current database's alternate-case spelling, which Rust admits, ending in a false “no object named” exit 1. Non-ASCII equivalents under a CI comparison can disagree in the other direction. Source finding; a fake empty lookup reply reproduces the caller outcome, not the CS SQL evaluation. | Decide whether the supplied database is the current database using one authoritative SQL Server identity/comparison rule, then use the decision in both the filter and resolver. Preserve the intended case convenience while excluding genuinely different databases. | A CS database resolves the intended alternate-case current-database name; cover relevant non-ASCII case equivalents and a different database's exit-3 refusal. No candidate-2016 gate is implied. |
| R29 (P2) | `output/text.rs:65–122`, `output/mod.rs:615`: every TextCut definition becomes a boolean and marks the output budget exhausted. The unmodified release exe and real driver against loopback TDS, with `--max-cell 10 --max-output 0`, emitted `CREATE PRO` and falsely reported output stopped at a 0-byte output limit. A 300-line body under a 4,096-byte budget framed 105 lines correctly, but its closing “42 definition lines” omission count was presented as exact despite 195 source lines being absent and the read total being unknown. | Distinguish explicit cell clipping, a read cap due to finite output, and whole-line rendering cuts. Identify a partial last source line, report the actual limit, retain uncertainty for unknown omitted-line totals, and avoid exhausting an unlimited output budget because a cell was cut. Update the contract and exact output together. | Session/driver-to-text tests for explicit cell limit with unlimited output, partial lines, read-time cuts, rendering cuts and combined limits; no 0-byte budget claim. The displayed-line count still frames the block exactly. Cross-check JSON/CSV cut accounting. |
| R30 (P2) | `schema.rs:654–681`, `output/text.rs:63`: visibility is checked only during lookup; the final definition read accepts NULL or no row. Loopback TDS returned a visible procedure then NULL: a blank one-line definition, exit 0. A zero-row final read also exited 0 with only a success status. Schema/visibility can change between the two reads; the wrapper does not freeze the catalog. | Validate the actual final definition data and intended target. A missing object or NULL/invisible body must take the documented exit-1 path, with a useful explanation, without another separate check/read race. | Successful lookup then NULL or no final row fails in text, JSON and CSV. Normal definitions and initial table/synonym/visibility refusals still behave correctly. |
| R31 (P2) | `run.rs:830–850`, `schema.rs:433–476`: Capture clones every value of every candidate row into nested vectors with no storage bound; resolution then makes another owned candidate vector, although only ten names are displayed. A large multi-schema catalog bypasses the display limits through this hidden result. An isolated production-Capture probe retained 50,000 candidate-shaped rows, with 6,400,000 schema-name bytes alone, still wanted more values and had cell_limit 0. This is a retained-data observation, not a peak-memory measurement. | Bound lookup storage separately from display settings, retaining only the candidates needed for resolution/diagnostics and obtaining remaining counts without storing every row, or cap the query with honest ambiguity reporting. Bound the expected metadata shape/fields; a cut capture must not look unique or exact. | Large replies have fixed bounded retained storage; uniqueness, ambiguity and “more” counts remain correct, and cancellation/deadlines remain effective while excess rows are handled. Oversized/unexpected replies fail safely. |
| R32 (P2) | `schema.rs:442` and qualified-name/list SQL builders: raw `.` and `, ` joins lose identifier boundaries before output escaping. The isolated resolver mapped both `[s.a].[b]` and `[s].[a.b]` to `s.a.b`. Such names and retry hints cannot reliably identify the original object. One column named `a, b` is indistinguishable from two columns in aggregate lists; ` DESC` in a name can look like a sort modifier. | Emit unambiguous, reusable multipart names and identifier lists, quoting/escaping components when needed. Apply one representation to results, definition headers, ambiguity errors and hints; retain safe SQL-literal argument construction. Update docs and goldens. | Legal dots, spaces, commas, `]`, quotes, reserved words and ` DESC` remain distinct. A reported qualified name passed as one argument resolves the same object; aggregate lists retain component and sort boundaries. |
| R33 (P2) | `schema.rs:311`: find --text passes sys.sql_modules.definition directly to PATINDEX, without a safe non-NULL argument or a visibility filter. Bodies can be NULL for encryption or lack of VIEW DEFINITION; Microsoft documents an error for a NULL PATINDEX expression. A visible object with a hidden body can therefore fail the command. This is a source/documentation finding, not an independently executed SQL failure. | Safely handle NULL source expressions and exclude invisible bodies from source matches; do not rely on predicate evaluation order or search a synthetic `(hidden)` marker. Record the visibility limitation. | Least-privilege metadata visibility plus an encrypted module does not fail find --text; visible bodies return correct matches/lines, hidden bodies create no invented matches, and object/column sections and ordinary SQL errors remain correct. |

Accepted design: arguments reach SQL as doubled-quote Unicode literals, no substitution or GO splitting occurs for commands, and Generated policy retains the read-only wrapper even under an allow profile. No argument injection or write-guard bypass was found. Static section labels and escaped headers are safe in current callers. The independent reader skipped the exact declared displayed lines of normal, cell-cut and budget-cut definitions without mistaking SQL comments for metadata; the 4,096-byte case emitted 3,201 bytes. Lookup output stays outside public results as intended. Explicit command defaults are reasonable, the exit-1 name/definition category and exit-3 syntax/scope split are accepted, and optional JSON `label` is additive under 2.1 with required batch provenance/timing preserved. The 62-line help retains encoding and safety guidance. No new dependency or DLL was added.

Future boxing: removing both boxes in an isolated debug build reproduced the main-thread stack overflow (0xC00000FD) after setup; restoring the outer session box alone made the same loopback def run succeed. The current debug session future measured 29,968 bytes, and with only the inner lookup box removed, 30,064 bytes. The failure is real, but a single future exceeding 1 MB is not established; construction/polling frames matter. Keep the outer box; the inner one was not required by this control, which does not establish its safety on every path. The original debug executable was forcibly rebuilt after the scratch builds used the shared cache.

Additional catalog validation requests: clarify and test columnstore/XML/spatial/JSON index-column representation (`key_ordinal = 0`; ordinary columnstore members have `is_included_column = 1`, so the special type-5/6 clause does not put them in keys); test the acknowledged XML aggregation failure for control-character identifiers; and verify find --text position/line arithmetic with supplementary characters under an SC database, since PATINDEX uses a fixed non-SC collation and the other position functions use the module expression's collation. These are coverage/representation follow-ups, not claimed independently executed live failures. Documentation nits: DESIGN's definition example has a literal U+2028 where visible `\u2028` belongs, schema-commands.md §9 item 5 still says unlimited defaults, and its JSON “never cut” wording needs to account for explicit limits when R29 is specified.

Independent `pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/check.ps1` passed in about 51 seconds: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied, 313 unit tests (four ignored), 62 debug process tests (49 ignored), 59 release process tests (43 ignored), a 2,472,448-byte exe and the expected ten in-box DLLs. Nine release-exe synthetic TDS cases exercised the findings; five isolated helper probes and three definition-framing checks passed. The owner confirmed that the real test profile/credential is unavailable to this execution session and directed source/isolated review. **Live tests were not independently rerun; developer reports 49 debug and 43 release live passes at 14a8e7b.** Production source and Cargo.lock were not edited. No owner's database, remote, hosted workflow or signing action was performed.

The fixes in `75e4d88`, after `f35a5fb` (verified below):

| # | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| R27 | sqlh parses the name itself, before connecting (`schema::parse_name`): up to four dot-separated parts, each plain or delimited (`[...]` with `]]`, `"..."` with `""`), an empty part omitted except the object's; each part at most 128 UTF-16 units. An unclosed or empty delimited part, text after a closing delimiter, a delimiter character in a plain part, a part over the limit or a fifth part is a usage error (exit 3); a server part is exit 3; nothing is ever treated as omitted because it is invalid. The lookup gets the parts as separate literals (`@db`, `@schema`, `@object`, NULL when omitted) and calls `OBJECT_ID` on the parts `QUOTENAME`d; `PARSENAME` is gone. | Unit: `names_are_parsed_part_by_part` (brackets and quotes with dots and doubled delimiters, `db..t`, `.t`, four parts; 129-unit schema, database and server parts, an astral character counting two units, five parts, empty, unclosed and trailing-text parts, the length guard; refusals through `lookup`), `arguments_are_literals` (the parts' literals). Process: `schema_commands_refuse_what_they_cannot_answer_before_connecting` (a 129-unit part, five parts, an unclosed bracket and a server part: exit 3 against a closed port, so nothing connected). Live: `def` of `[s×129].get`, `[s×129].dbo.get` and `[s×129].<db>.dbo.get`, each naming a visible procedure, exit 3 with no definition. |
| R28 | One decision, made by SQL Server: `@scope` is 1 when the database part is omitted or `DB_ID(@db) = DB_ID()`, 2 when `DB_ID(@db)` is NULL, else 0. The candidates query runs only under scope 1, and `resolve` refuses 0 (`is in database X ... run with -d X`) and 2 (`there is no database X on this server`), both exit 3; Rust no longer compares names. `DB_ID` compares database names by the server's collation, which is the rule that decides what the name means. | Unit: `a_name_resolves_to_one_object_or_says_why_not` (scopes 0, 1, 2; the current database in another spelling accepted on scope 1). Live: `<DB>..<parent>` in upper case resolves on the test server (case-insensitive server collation); `master.dbo.spt_values` exit 3 with `run with -d master`; `sqlh_no_such_db.dbo.x` exit 3. Not covered live: a case-sensitive *server* collation and non-ASCII database names (the test server's collation is fixed and the tests don't create databases); there the server's own `DB_ID` rule is what applies, by construction. |
| R29 | The text definition block (`TextSink::definition`) keeps the reason for each cut. The header starts with the lines shown, then what wasn't shown and why: `first N lines shown; M more lines not shown (--max-output)` for whole lines the budget couldn't fit; `first N lines shown[, the last one cut short]; K more characters not shown (--max-cell C)` for a read cut (by `--max-output` when there is no cell limit), with `the last one cut short` when the cut fell inside a line; `≥M more lines` when both apply, since the lines after a read cut are uncounted. Only lines left out for the budget exhaust it and appear in the closing note (`≥M definition lines` after a read cut: `Withheld.definition_lines_more`); a cell cut alone stops nothing. DESIGN.md §5.1, §5.2 (JSON: the read cuts a definition like any value) and §5.3 updated; the literal U+2028 in §5.1 is now `\u2028`. | Unit: `a_definition_cut_while_read_says_by_what` (a cut inside the first line with `--max-cell 10 --max-output 0`: no `--max-output` anywhere, the line marked; a cut after a line break; both cuts, with `≥18` in the header and note; no cell limit, the reason `--max-output`), `a_definition_is_cut_at_whole_lines_and_says_so`, `a_definition_is_a_counted_block`. Live, through the real driver and `Session::run`: `def <proc> --max-cell 10 --max-output 0` prints `first 1 line shown, the last one cut short; N more characters not shown (--max-cell 10)`, then `CREATE PRO`, exit 0, and nothing mentions `--max-output`. JSON and csv write the definition as a value, cut with `…[+N]` as any value, so the formats agree on the characters left out. |
| R30 | `def`'s batch reads the definition once into `@definition`, and `THROW 50000` (exit 1, an error in every format) when it is NULL or the object no longer has the schema and name it was looked up by (`COLLATE Latin1_General_100_BIN2`): `the definition of X can't be shown: since it was looked up, the object was dropped or renamed, or its definition was encrypted or hidden from the login; run the command again`. The row shown is literals for the name and type and that same variable, so it always has one row and is the value checked. | Unit: `def_refuses_what_has_no_definition_to_show` (the batch's check and the row from the variable). Live, by hand on the test server: the same batch shape with a NULL definition exits 1 in text, json and csv with error 50000 and its message. A scripted server can no longer return a NULL definition row from this batch without breaking the T-SQL; no fake TDS server exists in the Rust tests, so the race itself is not reproduced. |
| R31 | The candidates query is `TOP (11)` with `COUNT(*) OVER () AS total`; the ambiguity message lists ten and says `and N more` from the total. The lookup's sink (`run::Capture`) keeps at most 4 result sets, 16 rows each, values of 4,096 characters (its `cell_limit`, so the driver cuts longer ones as they arrive), and counts with `Counting::BOUNDED`; a reply past any bound (a skipped row, a cut value, a fifth result, a stopped count) fails the run (exit 10, `the name lookup returned more than it should`), and so does a total that doesn't fit the rows (fewer than them, or more while fewer than 11 came). The server's error, when there is one, comes first. | Unit: `a_lookup_keeps_a_bounded_reply` (16 rows kept; a 17th, a cut value, a fifth result and a stopped count each fail; the error first), `a_name_resolves_to_one_object_or_says_why_not` (11 rows with a total of 5000: ten names and `and 4990 more`; inconsistent totals fail). Live: the ambiguity of `twin` in two schemas, unchanged. |
| R32 | Every schema-qualified name sqlh shows has each part shown as it is when it is ASCII letters, digits and `_@#$` starting with a letter or `_`, else `QUOTENAME`d: in SQL (`schema::shown`, a BIN2 `LIKE` test) for `tables`, `find` (objects, types, columns' table, definitions), the `object` section, the foreign-key `table`/`references` (now from a join, not `OBJECT_SCHEMA_NAME`), and in Rust (`schema::quoted`) for the `def` header, errors and hints. Index keys and includes and foreign-key column lists quote each column the same way, so `[a, b], [c DESC] DESC` keeps its boundaries; a name alone in its field is unchanged. Reserved words aren't bracketed (sqlh's parser takes them). Lists now use `STRING_AGG ... WITHIN GROUP` (SQL Server 2017+) instead of `FOR XML PATH`, which fails on a control character in a name. | Unit: `names_are_shown_so_they_can_be_given_back` (dots, spaces, commas, `]`, `"`, a reserved word, ` DESC`, a leading digit, a non-ASCII letter; each shown name parses back to the same name; `[s.a].b` and `s.[a.b]` distinct; the SQL rule's text). Live: a table `<s1>.[x.y]` with columns `[a, b]` and `[c DESC]` and an index on both (descending second): `describe <s1>.[x.y]` shows `<s1>.[x.y]` and the keys `[a, b], [c DESC] DESC`; `tables <s1>.*` lists `<s1>.[x.y]`; `def <s1>.[v.w]` prints `-- definition: <s1>.[v.w] (VIEW), 1 line`. |
| R33 | `find --text` takes the body once as `ISNULL(m.definition, N'') COLLATE Latin1_General_100_CI_AI` (CROSS APPLY), so `PATINDEX` never gets a NULL whatever the evaluation order, and every position function counts in that one collation's units (the supplementary-character follow-up); `WHERE m.definition IS NOT NULL` leaves hidden bodies out, and the end of a last line is `DATALENGTH / 2 + 1`, not `LEN` (which drops trailing spaces). Documented: bodies the login can't see aren't searched. | Unit: `the_text_search_skips_hidden_bodies` (the SQL's NULL handling). Live: an encrypted procedure is found by name but has no `definitions` row, and the search exits 0; a reader login with SELECT but without VIEW DEFINITION (revoked) sees the view in `objects` but gets no `definitions` row for it, and does get one, line 1, for a function it was granted VIEW DEFINITION on. Not covered: a supplementary-character (SC) database, which the tests can't create. |

Catalog follow-ups: index `keys` are a B-tree index's key columns (`key_ordinal > 0`), and for every other type (columnstore, XML, spatial, JSON; `i.type NOT IN (1, 2, 7)`) the columns it covers; `includes` only for B-tree types (live: a nonclustered columnstore index shows `id, code` as its keys, a primary XML index `doc`). The control-character name case is answered by `STRING_AGG`. Docs: DESIGN.md §5.1 (definition cuts, U+2028), §5.2, §5.3, §9 (names, quoting, `STRING_AGG`, `find --text`); schema-commands.md (as-built list, §3, §4, §5 rewritten with "Names shown", §6.3, §6.4, §6.5, §6.6, §7, §9 item 5).

### Schema commands fix verification, October 14, 2026

Reviewed `f35a5fb..75e4d88` independently. **R27–R33 are closed; the six implemented schema commands are accepted. No new blocking finding.** `deps` remains outside this increment. The accepted profile-discovery increment and R25/R26 remain closed.

| Finding | Verification |
|---|---|
| R27 | Closed. The parser checks decoded parts and UTF-16 lengths before connection, preserves delimiters/embedded dots and supported omissions, and refuses malformed or explicit-server inputs. Separate literals and QUOTENAME replace PARSENAME, so invalid qualifiers cannot vanish into the fallback. Unit/process tests passed; six additional release-exe malformed-name cases against a closed port returned exit 3. |
| R28 | Closed. SQL's one DB_ID-based scope controls both candidate filtering and caller refusal; Rust no longer compares database spellings. Loopback scopes 0 and 2 returned exit 3 before any final batch. Scope 1 tests accept the server's identity decision. CS-server/non-ASCII database coverage remains unrun, but the implementation delegates that decision to SQL Server rather than duplicating its collation rules. |
| R29 | Closed. Read cuts retain their character count and cause; partial displayed lines are marked. Whole-line output cuts alone exhaust the body, and unknown omitted-line counts stay lower bounds in both header and closing note. The release exe/driver with `--max-cell 10 --max-output 0` reports only the cell limit, with no 0-byte budget claim. A separate budget case carries a lower bound in both places and stays within 4,096 bytes. Independent readers skipped the declared 1 and 143 lines to closing metadata. Text, JSON and CSV agreed on 26 omitted characters for the same body. Updated unit tests cover combined cuts and a cut at a newline. |
| R30 | Closed. The final batch reads into @definition, checks NULL and the intended schema/name, throws on failure, and returns that same checked variable with literal identity fields. It cannot legitimately return the original NULL/zero-row success shape. Source/unit verification plus synthetic error-50000 replies confirmed exit 1 in text, JSON and CSV; the tokens test error propagation rather than actual SQL execution. The developer separately reports real-server manual checks. No duplicate sink-side validation is required, and the DDL race itself was not reproduced here. |
| R31 | Closed for the production lookup. TOP (11), a fixed bounded catalog projection and a total replace unbounded candidate collection. Capture also caps result sets/rows, advertises a 4,096-character read cap, rejects cut values and marks skipped/stopped replies unusable. Eleven returned candidates with total 5,000 produced ten names plus `and 4990 more`. Twelve candidates, seventeen candidates and a one-row/two-total mismatch each exited 10 before a final batch. Unit tests cover extra result sets, cut values and stopped counting. |
| R32 | Closed. SQL and Rust share the ASCII/BIN2/plain-or-bracketed rule. Qualified results, FK targets, definition identity, errors and aggregate lists retain their boundaries. Unit round trips distinguish `[s.a].b` and `s.[a.b]` and cover the requested special/reserved/non-ASCII cases. Keeping reserved words plain is acceptable for the documented sqlh parser round trip, which quotes parts in its SQL lookup; the representation is not promised to be drop-in T-SQL in every grammar position. Developer live fixtures cover the matching catalog cases. |
| R33 | Closed. PATINDEX receives an explicitly non-NULL expression, while a separate filter excludes invisible bodies; no predicate-order dependency or synthetic hidden-body match remains. Position functions use that one non-SC expression and the last-line endpoint uses DATALENGTH. Source and regression tests passed. The developer reports encrypted-module and least-privilege live cases passed; they were not independently rerun. |

The brief's gaps are acceptable for these closures. A CS server/non-ASCII database must follow the server's own DB_ID identity rule, not ASCII folding or forced case-insensitivity. The final-definition variable/check/return design is sufficient: a fake server ignoring THROW and sending NULL would violate the SQL, not reproduce its original unchecked final read. The shared non-SC source expression resolves inconsistent position units. Its 200-unit preview can still split a surrogate pair; driver source uses encoding_rs for UTF-16 strings, so the clipped preview's final character can be replaced. This is a known nonblocking preview limitation; a later surrogate-safe boundary or explicit UTF-16-unit wording is appropriate. An SC database was not independently tested. Conservative brackets and the UNION's shown-name sort are acceptable presentation choices.

Catalog follow-ups: ordered STRING_AGG casts to nvarchar(max), removes XML serialization restrictions and is an accepted first-release 2025 choice; a 2016 path waits for adoption of that floor. The explicit non-B-tree branch now returns columnstore/XML/spatial/JSON indexed columns independently of key ordinal and included flags; B-tree/hash ordering and includes remain. Developer live fixtures cover XML and columnstore, not an independently run spatial/JSON fixture. The documentation nits and JSON definition-clipping promise were corrected; optional labels and required batch metadata remain compatible with JSON 2.1. The read-only wrapper and SQL-literal trust boundary remain intact.

**Follow-up, the preview cut (2026-10-14):** `find --text` cuts its preview at 200 UTF-16 units, or 199 when the 200th is a high surrogate (`UNICODE(SUBSTRING(t.whole, 200, 1)) BETWEEN 55296 AND 56319`, in the same non-SC collation as every other position), so the preview never ends in half a character and the driver has nothing to replace. Unit: `the_text_search_skips_hidden_bodies` asserts the cut. Live: a procedure whose line has the first unit of U+1F600 at unit 200 previews as the 199 units before it; with the fix disabled, the same test fails. schema-commands.md §6.5 says so.

### `deps`, built 2026-10-14 (accepted; R34 documentation follow-up)

The last schema command (schema-commands.md §6.7, which was two lines of sketch and is now the spec; DESIGN.md §9). Design choices made from experiments on the test server (SQL Server 2025), for the reviewer to check:
- **Sources:** `sys.sql_expression_dependencies` both ways, not `sys.dm_sql_referencing_entities` (one view serves both directions with the same flags; on SQL Server 2014+ that function needs no permission on the referenced object, only VIEW DEFINITION on the referencing ones: the CONTROL requirement first recorded here was the 2008–2012 rule, corrected by R34) or `sys.dm_sql_referenced_entities` (re-binds the module and fails on a missing reference). Observed: a deferred reference resolves once its target exists; a three-part name for the current database resolves, another database's doesn't; an unqualified `EXEC` is caller-dependent with no id; a schema-bound view has one row per column it uses (folded by `UNION`); a computed column or constraint also refers to its own table (left out); a check constraint's function call is schema-bound; an encrypted module's references are recorded. Foreign keys and synonyms aren't in the view, so both are added from `sys.foreign_keys` and `sys.synonyms` (a synonym's target is resolved with `OBJECT_ID` only when it is in this database).
- **Shape:** two sections, `uses` and `used_by`, each `name, type, via, note`. `via` names the column or constraint holding a reference (`column total`, `check CK_x`, `default DF_x`, `foreign key FK_x`); `note` is one of `other server`, `other database`, `caller-dependent`, `ambiguous`, `not found`, optionally followed by `schema-bound`. One level; no column-level detail.
- **`used_by` by name:** only caller-dependent references (SQL Server resolves them per caller), matched by name and, when written, schema; a reference SQL Server failed to resolve otherwise isn't claimed (it may mean an object in another schema).
- **Permissions:** without VIEW DEFINITION on the database, the view returns no rows at all (a `db_datareader` user with EXECUTE on a procedure saw none for it). The lookup reads `HAS_PERMS_BY_NAME(NULL, 'DATABASE', 'VIEW DEFINITION')`, and `deps` fails with exit 1 instead of answering "none".
- **Limits:** as `describe` (2,000 rows, 4,000 characters). `--help` lists it (still 62 lines; the SCHEMA lines were reworded to fit).

Tests: unit `deps_shows_both_directions_or_says_why_not` (sections, literals, the caller-dependent rule, the refusal, `view_definition`, `written`), parsing and limits. Live, in `schema_commands_describe_what_the_catalog_holds` (the fixture gained a check constraint calling a function and a procedure with an unqualified `EXEC`, a missing table and `master.dbo.spt_values`): `deps` of the orders table (its foreign key's table, the function via `check`, schema-bound; used by the procedure, view, TVF and synonym), the parent table (used by orders via `foreign key`), the function, the synonym, the procedure (`caller-dependent`, `other database`, `not found`), and the procedure it calls (used by the caller, `caller-dependent`); the reader without VIEW DEFINITION gets exit 1.

### Preview boundary and `deps` review (October 14, 2026)

Reviewed `0e67567..d4219c5` (`7a3e269`, `d4219c5`). **The preview fix and deps implementation are accepted, with one nonblocking P3 documentation finding, R34. No new production-code correction is requested. R27–R33 remain closed.**

The preview checks UTF-16 unit 200 of the actual CR-stripped line under the same non-SC collation as the position operations; a high surrogate reduces the cut to 199. This closes the earlier preview follow-up for valid UTF-16. The developer's live regression places a supplementary character across that boundary and reportedly fails when the fix is removed; it was reviewed but not independently run.

The two one-level sections (`uses`, `used_by`; `name, type, via, note`) fit repeated bounded agent investigation. Keep resolved-id edges and only labeled caller-dependent name matches; do not guess other unresolved reverse edges. Exit 1 without database VIEW DEFINITION is appropriate, and a partial FK/synonym answer would need an explicit partial-result contract. `not found` retains its "or none the login can see" qualification. The shorter help wording is acceptable. Typed class checks, constraint ownership, synonym scope gates using DB_ID, Unicode literal quoting, existing section labels and explicit limit overrides passed source review and isolated executable probes.

| Finding | Priority | Required follow-up | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| R34 | P3 | Correct schema-commands.md §6.7 and the deps Sources rationale above: `sys.dm_sql_referencing_entities` requires CONTROL on the referenced object only under SQL Server 2008–2012. On 2014+, it requires no permission on that referenced object; visibility of referencing entities governs completeness. Also document database VIEW DEFINITION **and SELECT on sys.sql_expression_dependencies** as the selected view's documented prerequisites. The current preflight checks the former; lack of SELECT may still produce a normal SQL permission error. No API replacement or runtime permission probe is required. | Closed October 18, 2026: original corrections verified and the remaining default-grant parenthetical removed in `bf00766`; Phase 5 fix verification below. |

Sources for R34: [Microsoft, referencing DMF permissions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/system-dynamic-management-objects/sys-dm-sql-referencing-entities-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17#permissions), [Microsoft, dependency-view permissions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/system-catalog-views/sys-sql-expression-dependencies-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17#permissions). This corrects the obsolete CONTROL claim in the original build record above; it does not invalidate the chosen catalog implementation. Synthetic SQL error 229 returned exit 1 in all three formats.

Nonblocking scope clarifications for §6.7: system objects are outside the dependency catalog's maintained coverage, so empty output is not exhaustive impact analysis ([Microsoft, Remarks](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/system-catalog-views/sys-sql-expression-dependencies-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17#remarks)); further `deps` traversal applies to local object targets, not TYPE/XML/partition classes, unresolved targets or remote names without an appropriate connection. DDL triggers are already explicitly excluded. UDT, ambiguous, other-server, CS-database and large-catalog cases remain unverified here; no compatibility branch or recursive expansion is required for acceptance.

All nineteen additional release-exe loopback cases passed: nonempty/empty sections across formats, VIEW DEFINITION 0/NULL refusal across formats, SQL error 229 across formats, row overrides per section, punctuation-safe literals and foreign/unknown database scope. JSON retained contract 2.1 and batch metadata; CSV labels stayed on stderr. These probes supply TDS results and inspect outgoing SQL, not execute catalog SQL.

**Live and Credential Manager tests were not independently run, as instructed; developer reports 49 debug and 43 release live passes at d4219c5.** The owner's profile/database was not used. Evidence is local in `artifacts/deps-review-check.log`, `artifacts/deps-review-tds.json` and `scratch/deps-review-tds.py`; the full review is in the ignored archive. Production source and Cargo.lock were not edited; no remote, hosted workflow or signing action was taken.

**R34 fix (2026-10-14, for verification):** schema-commands.md §6.7 now gives the 2014+ permission rule for `sys.dm_sql_referencing_entities` (none on the referenced object, VIEW DEFINITION on the referencing ones, partial results otherwise; CONTROL was 2008–2012) and the actual reason it wasn't chosen (one view for both directions with the same edges and flags, complete or refused through the database-level check). It documents both prerequisites of `sys.sql_expression_dependencies`, database VIEW DEFINITION and SELECT on the view, and that sqlh preflights only the first: missing SELECT gives the server's error 229, exit 1 like any SQL error, distinct from sqlh's own refusal. The Sources bullet above is corrected. The scope clarifications are in §6.7 too: a "Coverage" paragraph (persisted by-name references only, not dynamic SQL, application code or system objects, so an empty `used_by` isn't proof of no use), and the limits of running `deps` again on what it lists (local objects only; not TYPE/XML/partition entries, `not found` or `caller-dependent` names, or other databases and servers). Documentation only; no code changed.

### Phase 5: connection reuse (VISION priority 3b)

**Status (2026-10-18): 5.1 done; 5.2 (the engine) and 5.3 (`sqlh mcp`) built, self-reviewed in five rounds and fixed (the second round's fixes in `05032c0`, the third's in `becb56f`, the fourth's in `6e7a0d8`, the fifth's in `4519341` and `dc24d4b`, one for each of its two halves); the owner's external review of `2f53735..a8c5390` requested R35–R39, which are fixed, with the internal review of those fixes, through `367f8b0` and accepted by external fix verification at `c50dd3c` (R34–R39 closed); the follow-ups R40–R42 fixed (`aa06856`, `a55c70d`, `e60a1de`) and closed by external verification through `ab6eed7`, the increment accepted; decision 20 amended by the owner on 2026-10-18 after the fixes passed its gate (350 KiB, `serde_json` built for size; MCP 309.0 KiB as built). 5.4 (live tool tests) built 2026-10-18 (`fbbe1a8`, `a33d2ea`, `814ee12`; tests only): six live tests, and the review's reset comparison found none of its states outliving the reset (the isolation level remains the one the setup must set); `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` isn't green, on timing tests older than 5.4 ("Phase 5.4, built 2026-10-18", below)** (the table; "Phase 5.2 and 5.3, built 2026-10-17", "Phase 5.2 and 5.3 external review", "Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes" and the fix verifications below); 5.5 and 5.6 to do.

Decide the mechanism using the 2.1 results:
- **2.1's answer: connecting doesn't get fast** with SQL authentication on SQL Server 2025. Each login takes about 150 ms on the server, most likely in its password hashing (by design; inferred, not isolated), so reuse is needed rather than an optimization. Windows authentication skips that hash and may be fast enough without reuse; it wasn't measured.
- **Choose:**
  - **(a) MCP** as designed in `docs/mcp-mode.md`, with the review's prerequisites: a response byte cap, bounded request queues, reset-hygiene tests, and discarding any connection whose cancellation can't be proven clean.
  - **(b) A local per-profile daemon** that the CLI talks to over a named pipe, which gives reuse to Bash-driven agents without MCP.
  - **Decided 2026-10-15 (decision 14): (a) first,** on a reuse engine built as a library module so (b) can be added later on the same engine if an agent trial shows Bash-driven use stays the main path.
- **Decided 2026-10-15 to 2026-10-17 (decisions 14–20)**, and docs/mcp-mode.md rewritten to match: MCP first on a library engine (14); stateless calls (15); read tools only (16); after a cancel, a connection the driver kept is reused and the next call's verified reset is the check (17, amended after 5.1); a launch allowlist of profiles (18); 2 connections per profile, 15 idle minutes, one silent retry on a dead connection (19); MCP in the one exe, behind a 300 KB size gate (20; 350 KiB since the owner amended it on 2026-10-18).
- **Tasks**, in order:

| # | Task | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | **Spike: the two things the design rests on.** Live, on the test server: `prepare_reset_connection` drops a temp table, rolls back an open transaction, restores the database and the login's `SET`s (mcp-mode.md §8 item 4). And `src/db/` can tell a call's caller whether its attention was acknowledged (decision 17). Check mcp-mode.md §2 against the published 2026-07-28 revision. | A note in `docs/notes/` with what was observed; if the reset falls short, the owner hears it before 5.2 (the fallback is a reconnect per call). **Done 2026-10-17** ([notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md](notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md)): the reset restores a fresh login's state (temp table, transaction, database, options) on the same session at no measurable cost, and every kind of cancel tried (from outside, a count stop, `-t`, before sending, mid-rows) kept a connection that was clean after a reset; asserted by the live test `db::spike::spike_reset`. mcp-mode.md §2 matches the published revision. The driver gives no receipt for an acknowledged attention, so the owner amended decision 17's check to "kept by the driver, then a verified reset" (2026-10-17), and the missing receipt joined the list for upstream ([notes/upstream-issues.md](notes/upstream-issues.md), U4). JSON handling (`serde_json`) costs the exe 65 KB. |
