- **Either way:** replace the hour-long read-only session transaction with per-call wrappers, unless 2.5 and 2.6 show that a long transaction is cheap. **Decided 2026-10-15 (decision 15): calls are stateless,** each on a reset connection with its own wrapper; no sessions for now. **Decided 2026-10-16 (decision 16): read tools only;** writes stay on the CLI until Phase 6. (L)

### Phase 5.2 and 5.3, built 2026-10-17 (self-review record; external review below requests fixes)

The engine (`src/engine.rs`) and `sqlh mcp` (`src/mcp/`) were built on 2026-10-17 (commits `fa5695c`, the run's split, to `dc24d4b`); mcp-mode.md is the spec as built, and the 5.2 and 5.3 rows above hold the tests, the measurements and the live checks. These findings predate the owner's external review (October 18, below), so they carry no R-numbers: they come from a self-review in five rounds (not the owner's external reviewer), the first of `293b894`, the second an adversarial review of `293b894..e8175d4`, the third an adversarial review of the code at `05032c0` and the docs pass written for it, the fourth an adversarial review of the code at `becb56f` and the docs pass written for it, the fifth in two halves, a review of the code at `6e7a0d8` and then one of the code at `4519341` (its shutdown and the engine's open path) and the docs pass written for it, each finding verified against the code and, where it could be, by a probe of the exe, and the owner's reviewer saw them with the code.

**Found while building** (the first three by testing):
- **`describe` at compatibility level 100** (live, building 5.3): every `describe` of a table failed in a database at level 100 (StackOverflow2010), which refuses `STRING_AGG ... WITHIN GROUP` (R32's aggregation). The lookup now returns the level, and below 130 `describe` uses the ordered `FOR XML PATH` form, which every level parses (`d6237da`; schema-commands.md §3). Checked live on both databases. Superseded at 110 and 120 by the external review's R37: `STRING_AGG` from 110, `FOR XML PATH` only at 100 (`2504b7c`; Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, below).
- **The pool wait's message** said `all 1 of the profile's connections` whatever the pool's size (it printed the free permits); it names the pool's size (`4b61262`, found by the engine tests), and with `--pool 1` says `the profile's only connection was busy` (`becb56f`, round 3's refuted finding 6, polished anyway).
- **A server gone silent at `put_back`'s rollback** could hold an uncancelled call's reply about 10 s (the driver's timeout and its attention wait); the rollback has 2 s, then the connection is dropped (`8ff1096`, found reviewing the engine tests).
- **The isolation level leak** (below) was found by the self-review, reading the code against Microsoft's documented pooling behavior, and confirmed live: the spike's new assertion shows the reset leaving SERIALIZABLE in place.

**Round 1: the self-review's findings**, grouped (31 verdicts, 29 confirmed, several of them the same finding from different reviewers; the grade is the self-review's). Fixed in `3b3ef87`, `71b8918`, `12dcceb`, `0183d25` and `e8175d4`; where a later round changed a fix, the row says so:

| Finding | Fix or decision | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| **The isolation level leaked between calls (P1).** The setup set a level only for READ UNCOMMITTED, and `sp_reset_connection` keeps the level on SQL Server (all but Azure SQL Database), so a call's `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL` (which the guard allows) outlived it on its pooled connection: a later call could read uncommitted data without the note, or hold SERIALIZABLE range locks. Spike 5.1 had never read the level. | `run::session_setup` sets the level on every connection, before `BEGIN TRANSACTION`: READ COMMITTED (a fresh login's level, which honors `READ_COMMITTED_SNAPSHOT`), or READ UNCOMMITTED as before. One more `SET` in the same batch, no round trip; a CLI run reads as before (DESIGN.md §4.3). The spike reads the level now (docs/notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md §1). | unit `the_session_setup_carries_the_agent_defaults`; engine `every_call_sets_the_isolation_level_the_reset_leaves_alone`; live `spike_reset` (a bare reset leaves SERIALIZABLE, the setup gives READ COMMITTED) and `mcp_calls_share_a_connection_and_nothing_else` (through `sqlh mcp --pool 1`: SERIALIZABLE in one call, READ COMMITTED in the next on the same session) |
| **The server could outlive its client (P2).** With 64 or more responses unwritten, the loop stopped reading the channel that carried both stdin's end and stdout's failure, so a client that died without killing its children (the probe: a detached client sending 5,000 pings) left sqlh running. | The stdout thread reports its failure through a shared flag and a wake-up outside that channel; the loop checks it before anything else, and nothing more is queued once it is set (mcp-mode.md §2, The end). | process `a_gone_client_ends_the_server_with_stdin_still_open` (`e8175d4`: stdout closed, 500 pings, stdin held open; exit 0 within 15 s) |
| **Calls in flight were unbounded (P3).** Only queued responses were counted, so a client could start any number of calls (the probe: 1,000 calls took the working set from 7.5 MB to 337 MB). | At most 64 calls run at once (`MAX_IN_FLIGHT`); one more gets a tool error at once, so stdin keeps being read and cancels keep arriving. Round 2 made that answer the run's usage error in the call's format (below). | process `calls_past_the_in_flight_limit_are_refused_at_once` (`e8175d4`: the 65th of 65 stalled calls answered first, exactly; the 64 still answer) |
| **Responses were dropped at exit (P3).** After stdin closed, a slow reader got 1 s for the last responses; the rest were lost, silently, with exit 0. | The flush wait goes on while responses keep draining (it ends after `FLUSH_WAIT`, 1 s, without progress), up to `END_GRACE`, 6 s, and `--verbose` logs how many were never written. Since `becb56f` a console event that ends the serving bounds this wait, the calls' settling and `close_all` by its own grace instead (`Signal::grace`, below; one during an end already begun isn't received, open below). Progress was counted in whole lines until `dc24d4b`, so a line the client took longer than 1 s to read was cut off; since then it is counted in bytes, so a long last response a slow reader is still taking in is written whole (round 5, second half, finding 2, below). | process `a_slow_reader_gets_a_long_last_response_whole` (`dc24d4b`: a reader taking 64 KB every 50 ms gets a `-32022` that echoes a 2,000,000-byte version, and the `ping` after it, whole); a client that stops reading, and the count of unwritten responses, have none (open, below) |
| **Ids (P3).** A request whose `id` was `null` (or another non-string, non-number), and a message with neither `method` nor `result`/`error`, got no answer; a cancel followed by a call reusing the id took the new call's cancel out of the map. | `-32600`, without the id when it can't be one. An id stays taken until its call has settled, and a call reusing it meanwhile gets `-32600` (`a call with this id is still running`). Round 3 found that this also answered a response whose `id` is `null`, which is now never answered, and bounded ids at 1024 bytes (`becb56f`, below). | unit `lines_are_requests_notifications_or_errors`; process `a_cancelled_call_gets_no_response`, which covers the cancelled call's silence but not the reused id's refusal: it accepts the `-32600` or an answer to a call of its own (open, below) |
| **Decided behaviours untested (P2 for the security ones, P3 for the rest):** an `allow` profile kept read-only, `$(var)` off, the 1 MB cap, a `database`'s bracket escaping (which keeps the argument from injecting SQL), a cancelled call's silence, a cancel during the wait for a connection, `close_all`. | Tests only: the code was right. | process `the_server_stays_read_only_and_reads_no_variables`, `a_cancelled_call_gets_no_response`; engine `a_calls_database_is_used_bracketed_and_the_next_call_gets_the_reset_alone`, `a_call_cancelled_while_waiting_for_a_connection_ends_promptly`, `close_all_closes_the_idle_connections`; the verdict's `sqlh --profile x profile` spelling of the must-come-first error got none (only `mcp`'s is tested; open, below) |
| **CLI-only advice in MCP results (P3).** Results offered `--exact-count`, `raise -t`, `-d`, `sqlh find`, `see sqlh --help`, `--continue-on-error`, and `--check`/`--allow-write` in the `check` tool's NOEXEC refusal: flags a client can't pass. | `output::Advice` (`Cli`, `Mcp`; set for every listed profile at launch) words each by its reader: the count-stop note, the unconfirmed count stop (`DbError::CountStopUnconfirmed`, worded by the run), both timeouts, the status line's usage label and unrun batches, the schema hints and the NOEXEC refusal (`0183d25`); then (`e8175d4`, found in the docs pass) the limits a footer or note names (`max_rows N`, `max_cell N`, `the server's --max-output`, `at most N per call`, through `Advice::max_rows` and its siblings), the dirty-read note (`the profile's read_uncommitted = true`), `check`'s closing note, and a failed connect's hints, which name the profile's keys (`db::Hints::Profile`: `trust_server_certificate = true`, `encrypt = "optional"`, `login_timeout`, `auth = "sql"`, the call's database or the profile's, narrowed to the profile's database in `becb56f`, round 3). mcp-mode.md §4 has both tables. The guard's reasons still mention `--check` and `--allow-write`: accepted, they describe, they don't advise. Left then: the `definition` tool's two refusals, which advised `sqlh describe` and `sqlh def` (reworded in `05032c0`, round 2 below), and a profile's resolution errors at launch (open, below). Round 2 also changed the timeout advice for a call at the server's limit. | `the_notes_advice_follows_what_stopped_the_count`, `the_wait_advice_names_what_its_reader_can_change`, `the_status_line_in_mcp_names_no_flag`, `refusals_under_a_deny_profile_never_offer_allow_write`, `a_name_resolves_to_one_object_or_says_why_not`, `tls_hints_from_schannel_status`, `connect_hints_in_mcp_name_the_profile`; no test for the MCP wording of the footers and notes (the output budget's note has one since `05032c0`), a cut definition's header, the unconfirmed count stop (in either form), the batch timeout, or the login errors (open, below) |
| **The schemas promised less than `plan()` enforces (P3).** `profile` was optional in the schemas with several profiles listed, though a call without it is refused; `database` had no length bounds; `30.0` was refused; `find` took an empty pattern the CLI refuses. | `profile` is required with several; `database` has 1–128; the required strings have `minLength: 1` and an empty one is refused; a whole number written as a float is accepted. | `the_tools_are_listed_with_closed_schemas`, `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema` |
| **`put_back` under NOEXEC (P3).** A `check` call cancelled or timed out left `SET NOEXEC ON`, so the rollback was only compiled and the connection dropped (safe, but the next call paid a login). | `put_back` sends `SET NOEXEC OFF;` as a request of its own before the rollback, both within 2 s. | engine `a_check_call_cut_short_turns_noexec_off_before_the_rollback` (a cancel and a timeout) |
| **Smaller ones (P3).** Two launch errors spanned two lines; `connect 0 ms` on reuse had no test; an `allow` profile's `read_uncommitted = true` was dropped though no MCP call can write; MCP's `info` lacked `sqlh info`'s connection note (which says whether the connection is encrypted). | One line each; the engine test asserts `, connect 0 ms`; the profile's `read_uncommitted` holds in MCP (mcp-mode.md §3); `info` carries its note. | `launch_errors_go_to_stderr_with_exit_3` (one line); `calls_on_one_profile_reuse_its_connection_with_a_reset` (the text's `, connect 0 ms`; the JSON document's `connect_ms: 0`, which the verdict also asked for, has no test: open, below); `info`'s note: live `mcp_calls_share_a_connection_and_nothing_else` (`connected in 0 ms`); the `allow` profile's `read_uncommitted`: no test (open, below) |
| **Argument errors were `-32602` (P2).** The 2026-07-28 tools page (Error Handling) makes only an unknown tool, a malformed `CallToolRequest` and server errors protocol errors; input validation errors (a wrong format, a value out of range) are tool execution errors with `isError: true`, which clients SHOULD pass to the model so it can correct the call. The triage first kept `-32602`, reading the page's `-32602` example (an unknown tool) as covering arguments; checking the page again showed it doesn't. | Fixed (`e8175d4`): `tools::Refused` splits the two. `-32602` only for no tool name, an unknown tool, or `arguments` that isn't an object; every other argument refusal is `isError` with the run's usage error (exit 3), in the call's `format` (checked first), through the output sink so client text is escaped, and an unknown argument lists what the tool takes (mcp-mode.md §4). | `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema` (both kinds; the JSON form; a key holding a newline stays on one line); `a_modern_session_discovers_lists_and_calls` (a missing profile is `isError`) |
| **`subscriptions/listen` got `-32601` (P3).** 2026-07-28's base protocol lists subscribe-and-notify among the patterns every implementation supports, and its subscriptions page lets a server acknowledge only what it honors. | The triage first kept `-32601` as a known deviation (the tool list never changes); fixed in `e8175d4` instead. It is acknowledged at once (`notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged`, `notifications: {}`, the subscription id in `_meta`), held open with its id taken (`-32600`, `a subscription with this id is still open`), ended silently by the client's cancel, and answered when the server ends (`resultType: "complete"`, the id in `_meta`); a legacy session gets `-32601` (mcp-mode.md §2). Round 2 added the teardown after the answer and the cap of 8 (below). | process `a_subscription_is_acknowledged_and_answered_at_the_end`; `a_legacy_session_initializes_and_calls` (the legacy `-32601`) |

That broke mcp-mode.md's rule that advice names what its reader can change. | `run::raise_timeout(settings, timeout)` takes the call's effective timeout and returns `None` at the server's limit (MCP only; the CLI keeps `raise -t`). **Round 3: an adversarial review of the code at `05032c0`** and of the docs pass written for it (9 verdicts: 7 confirmed, all P3, each verified against the code, and all but finding 7 reproduced against the exe at `05032c0` or a throwaway copy of the engine; finding 7 was confirmed from the code, since a login-time 4060 needs a server's error; finding 9 is a duplicate of 1; findings 4 and 6 refuted, below; `becb56f`'s commit message says "each reproduced", which is wrong for finding 7). All the confirmed ones are fixed in `becb56f`; mcp-mode.md §2, §4 and §5 describe the result:

| # | Finding | Fix in `becb56f` | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Only `tools/call` refused a reused open id.** With subscription 5 open, a `ping` (answered with a result like the subscription's closure), `tools/list`, `server/discover`, `initialize` (which also set the agreed version), an unknown method (`-32601`), a `subscriptions/listen` with `notifications: 3` (`-32602`, since `listen` checked `notifications` before the id), a `tools/call` without `clientCapabilities` (`-32602`, from the era) and a `ping` with `params` `[1]` (`-32602`, from the parser) were each answered under id 5; with call 7 in flight, `ping`, `tools/list` and `nope/x` were answered under 7 before the call's own result. `05032c0`'s claim that a reused id gets only the `-32600` held for a well-formed `tools/call` alone. MCP forbids reusing an id not yet answered, so only a broken client meets it. | One `in_use` check at the top of `request()`, before `initialize` and the era, for every method (the checks in `call` and `listen` are gone), and in `dispatch` for a parse error that carries a readable open id: any such request gets only the open id's `-32600`. | process `an_open_id_gets_only_its_refusal` (new: with subscription 5 open, a `ping`, a `tools/list`, a `listen` with `notifications: 3`, an `initialize`, a `ping` with `params` `[1]` and the method `nope`, each with id 5, get six `-32600`s and nothing else); a running call's reused id has no test that asserts the `-32600` (open, below), though it goes through the same check |
| 2 | **Ids had no bound below the 4 MB message limit,** and each call in flight holds three copies (the id, its JSON text, and that text again as the calls map's key): 64 calls with 3.9 MB ids peaked at 765 MB working set, against 9.8 MB with 10-byte ids and 187 MB with 900 KB of SQL each. Memory stayed bounded (about 0.8 GB at `MAX_IN_FLIGHT`), and only an absurd client sends such ids. | `protocol::parse` refuses an id longer than 1024 bytes as JSON text (`MAX_ID_BYTES`) with `-32600`, `Invalid request: an id is at most 1024 bytes` (`... bytes as JSON` since `6e7a0d8`, round 4's refuted finding 6), sent without the id: that bounds the calls map, the open subscriptions and every echo. | unit `lines_are_requests_notifications_or_errors` (a 1,026-byte id refused, a 1,024-byte one taken); process `an_open_id_gets_only_its_refusal` (a 2,002-byte id refused, without an id) |
| 3 | **Messages that must not be answered were.** An error or result response with `id` `null` got `-32600` (`id must be a string or a number`), since the id was checked before a response was recognized, and a notification whose `params` isn't an object (`notifications/cancelled` with `[3]` or `"x"`, `notifications/initialized` with `[1]`) got `-32602`. The MCP base protocol and JSON-RPC 2.0 §4.1 say a notification is never answered (`[3]` is a valid by-position notification), and the cancellation page that an invalid cancel SHOULD be ignored; it also contradicted mcp-mode.md's "a response is ignored". Harm: an extra error line with no id. | `parse` takes a message with `result` or `error` and no `method` as a response before it reads the id, so it is ignored whatever its id; a notification's `params` that isn't an object is taken as none. | unit `lines_are_requests_notifications_or_errors` (responses with id `null`, id `[1]` and no `jsonrpc`; a notification with `params` `[3]`); process `an_open_id_gets_only_its_refusal` (neither answered: the message count is exact) |
| 5 | **A reused connection gone silent failed its call with a bare `query timeout`, after the call's timeout plus 5 s, and wasn't retried.** In a throwaway copy of the engine, a fake server silent at the reused connection's setup: `timeout: 1` ended in 6.02 s and `timeout: 3` in 8.01 s, both `-- error: query timeout`, exit 4, one connection accepted (the next call reconnected). `dead_before_work` excluded every timeout, though `run::open` runs only sqlh's setup and `USE`, so nothing of the call had run, and decision 19 retries "a reused connection found dead (a network drop, a killed session)" with no exception for timeouts: only the code comment and the docs pass excluded them, and no owner decision. On a real blackholed path Windows TCP gives up after about 20 s and reports a lost connection, which was retried, so mostly calls with a timeout of about 15 s or less, or a server that stops answering while its TCP stack still acknowledges, met it. A deviation from an owner decision, so it needed the owner's word or a fix. | Fixed, within decision 19: `REUSE_PROBE` gives a reused connection's `run::open` 5 s instead of the call's timeout when that is longer or 0; `engine::retry_after` replaces `dead_before_work` and retries a timeout when the probe cut the request short or the driver marked the connection dead (an attention unanswered), and any other failure with the connection dead or retired, as before; a SQL error, a cancel and a timeout at the call's own limit on a live connection aren't. A timeout in `run::open` says where it struck, `query timeout after N s in the session setup` or `... changing to the call's database` (the CLI's setup too, DESIGN.md §4.3). The retry of a live but slow setup that the probe cut short went beyond "found dead", on the decision's own ground, recorded for the owner to confirm; round 4 found it costly (its finding 1), and `6e7a0d8` narrowed the fix: the probe bounds the setup alone, a setup it cuts on a live connection runs again on that connection, and `dead_before_work` is back, retrying only a dead connection (round 4, below; §6, decision 19). | engine `a_reused_connection_gone_silent_is_retried_after_the_probe` (silent at the reused setup, `timeout: 60`: retried after 9 to 20 s, the probe and the driver's attention wait, two connections accepted, the output the first call's), `a_setup_the_probe_cuts_short_runs_again_on_a_new_connection` (a 7 s setup: retried after the probe's 5 s; replaced in `6e7a0d8` by `a_setup_the_probe_cuts_short_keeps_its_live_connection`), `the_probe_is_never_longer_than_the_call` (a 3 s setup under `timeout: 1`: `-- error: query timeout after 1 s in the session setup`, no retry); the `USE` form got its test in `4519341` (`a_use_past_the_calls_timeout_fails_once`, round 5); a connection that dies at its `USE` has none (open, below) |
| 7 | **The MCP 4060 hint named the call's database** (`check the call's database, or the profile's`, from `e8175d4`), which can't cause a login-time 4060: the engine logs in with the profile's settings, and the call's `database` is a later `USE` in `run::open`, whose failure is a SQL error (911, 916), so a model could change or drop `database` while every call kept failing at login. | `...; check the profile's database`. | none: the login errors' hints in MCP need a server's error to build (open, below) |
| 8 | **`Method not found` echoed the whole method name** (a 300-character method gave a 390-byte line, where a 300-character tool name gave 189 bytes, cut), against `05032c0`'s "a name the client sent is shown at most 100 chars". The harness, not the model, picks methods, so it rarely reaches a model's context. Its `-32022` part was refuted: the schema defines `data.requested` as the version the client requested, so it is sent back verbatim. | `tools::echo` (now `pub(crate)`) cuts it too: `Method not found: ` and the first 100 characters, then `…`. `data.requested` stays verbatim, and mcp-mode.md §2 says why. | process `an_open_id_gets_only_its_refusal` (a 300-character method, exactly) |
| 9 | Only `tools/call` refused a reused open id, and `listen` checked `notifications` first. | **Duplicate of 1** (same root cause, same evidence). | as 1 |

Refuted: **4**, numeric ids beyond 64 bits coming back as a different number, and fractional ids accepted. Every integer in the signed and unsigned 64-bit ranges round-trips exactly (18446744073709551615 and 9007199254740993 included), but `-0`, which comes back as `-0.0`, equal but not the same text (the finding's own reproduction); only larger ones (outside RFC 8259's interoperable range, and produced by no MCP client) and exponent spellings, whose echo is numerically equal, change, and a fractional id is the client breaking MCP's string-or-integer rule, echoed exactly (mcp-mode.md §2, Ids). The external review overturned this refutation (R38: distinct integers past 64 bits became one id, so a subscription blocked a different id), fixed in `6e1a6e7`: only strings and exact 64-bit integers are taken (Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, below). **6**, the wait's `all 1 of the profile's connections were busy` with `--pool 1`: accurate and unambiguous, number agreement only; `becb56f` polished it anyway, `the profile's only connection was busy` (no test of that form: open, below).

Also in `becb56f`, from the open list and the docs: **the end after a console event** is bounded by the event's own grace (`Signal::grace`: 2 s after Ctrl+C or Ctrl+Break, and 1 s after a close, logoff or shutdown, since Windows ends the process about 5 s after one of those) at each step, the calls' settling, `Engine::close_all` and the flush, and by `END_GRACE` otherwise, `close_all` included (it had no bound); no test sends `sqlh mcp` a console event (open, below). The process's exit wasn't bounded with them until `6e7a0d8`: dropping the runtime could wait for a name lookup (round 4, finding 5). That holds for an event that ends the serving: one that arrives once the end has begun, after stdin closed or stdout broke, isn't received (open, below). **A call's `profile` matches in any ASCII case**, as `--profile` does (it had to match the profiles file's spelling exactly, which the docs pass recorded as built, decision 18's note); unit `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema` (`Reporting` reaches `reporting`). The exe grew 3,072 bytes (5.3 row).

**Round 4: an adversarial review of the code at `becb56f`** and of the docs pass written for it (6 verdicts: 4 confirmed, one P2 and three P3, each reproduced, in throwaway engine tests in scratch copies of `becb56f` and `05032c0` or with a scratch build of the exe; findings 4 and 6 refuted, below). All the confirmed ones are fixed in `6e7a0d8`; mcp-mode.md §2 and §5 describe the result:

| # | Finding | Fix in `6e7a0d8` | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (P2) | **The probe's retry threw away a live connection, so a failed new login could fail a call that would have succeeded.** `becb56f`'s `retry_after` retried any timeout the probe caused (`probed || ...`), and a retry clears the profile's idle connections, so a reused setup slower than 5 s on a live server dropped that connection and the healthy idle ones and made the call depend on a new login. Reproduced: a reused connection whose setup took 7 s and a replacement the server closed at once, a call at `-t 60`: at `becb56f` exit 2, `connection lost ... (os error 10053)`, `-- failed (exit 2): connection failed, 7002 ms`, two connections; at `05032c0` `-- ok`, one connection. With every reused setup taking 6 s, 4 calls opened 4 connections at `becb56f` and 1 at `05032c0`. Under server-wide load, the incident a DBA investigates, the login is the costly step and the one most likely to fail; the docs pass for `becb56f` named only the probe's 5 s as the cost. A regression against `05032c0` in the case the tool exists for, though rare (a reused setup over 5 s on a live server). | A setup the probe cut on a connection the driver kept (the server acknowledged the attention) is reset again (`reset_before_next`) and `run::open` runs again on that connection, with the call's own timeout and no probe (since `4519341` the setup alone, `run::setup`: round 5, below); the idle connections stay. `engine::dead_before_work` is back in place of `retry_after`: a reused connection's failed `run::open` is retried on a new connection only when the driver marked it dead or retired it (a timeout included, when its attention went unanswered), never for a SQL error or a cancel, and never once the call's cancel is set. So only a dead connection is replaced, as decision 19 says (§6, decision 19's note). | engine `a_setup_the_probe_cuts_short_keeps_its_live_connection` (replaces `becb56f`'s `a_setup_the_probe_cuts_short_runs_again_on_a_new_connection`: a 7 s reused setup under `timeout: 60` succeeds in 5 to 15 s with one connection accepted, the resets at requests 4 to 6 `[true, true, false]`, connect at least 5,000 ms and the connection pooled again); `a_reused_connection_gone_silent_is_retried_after_the_probe` still retries a dead one; no test of a probe-cut setup whose connection then dies at the setup again (open, below) |

An 11 s delay with a third connection the server closes at once: exit 2, `connection lost ... (os error 10053)` at 10.03 s, three connections accepted, none left idle, the healthy one that had served only its first call dropped by the retry's clear. With a healthy third connection: ok at 10.03 s, `connect 10028 ms`, three accepted, only the new one idle. A 9 s delay: ok on the same connection at 9.0 s, `connect 9000 ms`, two accepted, two idle. A setup never answered: as the 11 s delay with a healthy third. The boundary is the driver's fixed 5 s attention wait after the probe's 5 s: by then the driver has marked the connection dead, so taking it for dead is inherent to the probe, not a code defect, and clearing the peers is right for the probe's real target, a firewall's idle cut (the code's comment: `the connection it replaces died while idle, and so, most likely, did the profile's other idle ones`); when the new login works, the call is no slower than a plain wait. The confirmed part is the documentation: `REUSE_PROBE`'s doc and a test comment said `a slow server keeps its connection`, mcp-mode.md §5 `without replacing a slow live one`, and decision 19's note defined live as the attention acknowledged, without "within the driver's 5 s". `check.ps1 -Live` passed at `9b5318d`. One earlier run of it at `9b5318d` failed one debug live test (49 of 50 passed); its name wasn't kept, and the next five runs of the debug live tests (250 test runs) and a full `check.ps1 -Live` passed. Recorded as observed, not explained; nothing in `9b5318d` touches the CLI's live paths.

Also in `dc24d4b`, from the open list as it stood at `4519341` (below, "Fixed in `dc24d4b`"): the CLI's runtime shut down in the background, launch errors escaped, the listeners' failure logged with `--verbose`, and the code comments `4519341` left behind. The exe shrank 512 bytes (5.3 row).

**Still open, for the owner's code review** (as it stood at `9b5318d`; superseded by the list in "Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes", below, which drops what the review's fixes answered: the console event during the end, R36; the size margin, now past the gate; and the tests since added):
- **A console event during the end** (found in the docs check at `becb56f`): console events are listened for only while the server serves, so once the end has begun because stdin closed or stdout broke, an event isn't received, though the listener still turns Windows' default handling off. Ctrl+C does nothing then, and a close, logoff or shutdown leaves the `END_GRACE` waits standing (up to 6 s each: for the calls to finish after stdin closed, to settle, for `close_all` and for the flush), so Windows can end the process about 5 s after the event, before the last answers or the subscriptions' teardown are written. For the owner's code review: keep listening through the end, or accept it as built (mcp-mode.md §2, The end; §10).
- **No automated test yet** for the flush wait's end when the client stops reading (a slow reader that keeps reading is tested since `dc24d4b`) and its count of unwritten responses; the end after a console event, within its grace (no test sends `sqlh mcp` one), and so a console event during an end already begun; `close_all`'s `END_GRACE` bound at an end after stdin closed or stdout broke; the exit with a name lookup still running (`shutdown_background`, measured by hand at `becb56f` and `6e7a0d8`; the CLI's, the same since `dc24d4b`, not even measured); the probe's boundary (a reused setup a live server answers after more than about 10 s, taken for dead and retried, the profile's idle connections dropped: round 5's second half reproduced it with throwaway tests); the probe on a call with no timeout (`timeout` 0, which gets `REUSE_PROBE` too; the engine tests' calls have timeouts of 1, 6, 30 and 60); a cancel during the probe's attention wait (neither set up again nor retried since `6e7a0d8`), and a probe-cut setup whose connection then dies at the setup again (retried by the rule), or whose setup again runs out at the call's timeout on a connection the driver kept (the call's own timeout, not retried); an `allow` profile's `read_uncommitted = true` holding in MCP, and the MCP dirty-read note; the MCP wording of the limit footers, of `at most N per call`, of a cut definition's header and of `check`'s closing note (no exact-output test, which AGENTS.md asks of an output shape; the output budget's note has one since `05032c0`); the MCP wording of the unconfirmed count stop (exit 6; its CLI form is untested too); the whole message of a batch timeout in MCP and of a lookup timeout in either form (their advice, `run::raise_timeout`, is tested, and so is the CLI's batch timeout); the SQL Server login errors' hints in MCP (18456, 4060, 18452); the console-stdin refusal at launch; a launch error's escaping (probed by hand at `dc24d4b`); the console-event listeners failing at startup, and the `--verbose` line that says so; the argument refusals of a `database` over 128 characters, `max_cell` out of bounds and a `timeout` over 4,294,967,295; a profile name cut by `tools::echo`, and `Unknown tool: X`'s cut (checked only by the probe at `05032c0`); a running (or cancelled, still settling) call's id reused by another request (the `-32600` `a call with this id is still running` isn't asserted: `a_cancelled_call_gets_no_response` accepts either outcome; the open-subscription case, through the same check, is tested with a `ping`, a `tools/list`, a `subscriptions/listen`, an `initialize`, a `tools/call`, an unknown method and a request whose `params` isn't an object, but not with a `server/discover` or the other malformed forms `dispatch` refuses the same way: a `jsonrpc` other than `"2.0"`, no `method`, a `method` that isn't a string); the pool-of-one wording of the wait for a free connection; a reused connection that dies at its `USE` after an acknowledged reset, a `USE` timeout whose attention goes unanswered among them (retried by the rule; a `USE` timeout on a connection the driver kept is tested since `4519341`); the `sqlh --profile x profile` spelling of the must-come-first error (only `mcp`'s is tested); and the JSON document's `connect_ms` on a reused connection, 0 or after a probe or a retry, and `info`'s `connected in N ms` after one (the engine tests assert only the text's `connect N ms`). mcp-mode.md §9 has the list.
- **Still advising the CLI in sqlh mcp:** a profile that fails to resolve at launch gets the CLI's resolution error, which can name `-S` and `-U` (a remote Named Pipes server: `connect over TCP: -S host`; a SQL-login profile without `server` and no `SQLCMDPASSWORD`: `drop -S/-U, or set SQLCMDPASSWORD`). The launch's own refusal of an unknown flag also says `(sqlh mcp takes its connections from its profiles)` for flags such as `--format`, and the MCP 18456 hint names the profile's saved password even when the password came from `SQLCMDPASSWORD`. Found in the docs pass's check, after `e8175d4`; not fixed (code, not docs; mcp-mode.md §3, §4). The docs check at `becb56f` found one more in a call's result: a `GO N` past 1000 is refused with the CLI's source label, `-- error: -Q:2: GO 999999999 would run the batch 999999999 times; the most is 1000`, where a guard refusal says `line N` (it names `-Q` as the place, not as advice; mcp-mode.md §4, Still naming a flag).
- **Fixed in `becb56f`,** from this list as it stood at `05032c0`: what still answered under an open id (round 3, finding 1), the end after a close, logoff or shutdown that ends the serving (above, "Also in `becb56f`"; one during an end already begun is open, above), and what a response echoed whole, now only `-32022`'s `data.requested`, which the schema defines as the version the client sent (findings 2 and 8).
- **Fixed in `6e7a0d8`,** from this list as it stood at `becb56f`: **decision 19's question**, the retry on a new connection of a live setup the probe cut, beyond the decision's "found dead": such a setup now runs again on its own connection, so only a dead connection is replaced, as decided (round 4, finding 1). The probe and the setup again are recorded as the decision's implementation, for the owner's review, with no question open on them (§6, decision 19's note; mcp-mode.md §5, §10). **The stale code comments:** `src/mcp/mod.rs`'s module comment and `END_GRACE`'s said the calls still running at the end get `END_GRACE` to settle, and now name the console event's grace too; `src/engine.rs`'s module comment, `run_pooled`'s and the retry path's describe the retry as for dead connections, which since `6e7a0d8` it is again, and `REUSE_PROBE`'s doc comment says what the probe does to a live connection.
- **Fixed in `4519341`,** from this list as it stood at `6e7a0d8`: **a `USE` timeout on a probed connection ran twice** (found reading the code for the docs pass to `6e7a0d8`, confirmed by round 5's first half): the engine's probe loop now runs the setup alone, and the call's `USE` runs once with the call's timeout (round 5, first half, above; mcp-mode.md §5).
- **Fixed in `dc24d4b`,** from this list as it stood at `4519341` (besides round 5's second half, above):
  - **The CLI's runtime is shut down in the background** (round 4's finding 5 suggested it as optional): `app::run_command` calls `runtime.shutdown_background()` once the run, its delivery and its `-o` publication are over, so a CLI run cancelled while its connect resolved a server name no longer waits for the lookup at its exit, and DESIGN.md §4.3's end-to-end bound after a signal has no exception left. Not measured in the CLI, and no test plays it (above).
  - **Launch errors are escaped** (found in the docs check at `4519341`): `app::mcp_log` writes its line through `output::escaped`, and `app::main` sends an argument refusal through it too, so a launch argument holding a line break can't split the one stderr line (`--idle $'1\n-- forged'` gave two lines; it gives `--idle 1\n-- forged: ...` on one, probed at `dc24d4b`), as the CLI's `-- error:` line escapes it (mcp-mode.md §3).
  - **Console-event listeners that can't be installed are logged** (found in the same check): `serve` still serves without them, under Windows' default console handling (the CLI fails such a run with exit 10), and with `--verbose` now says so, `sqlh mcp: console events can't be watched (E); Ctrl+C ends the process at once`, where it did without them in silence (mcp-mode.md §3). No test (above).
  - **The code comments `4519341` left behind:** `src/engine.rs`'s module comment (the setup is `run::setup`, carrying the reset, and the call's `USE` follows, `run::use_database`; the probe; the retry at the setup or the `USE`), `REUSE_PROBE`'s and `dead_before_work`'s docs (`REUSE_PROBE`'s also states the boundary of round 5's second half, and `dead_before_work`'s its 5 s condition, above), and in `src/run.rs` `open`'s doc (a run's first requests, which the engine runs apart) and `Opened`'s (a reused connection's connect time is 0 only when it was reused at once; after a probe or a replacement, the time from the first attempt).
- **The size gate's margin:** 5 KB at `dc24d4b` (302,080 bytes over Phase 4, 5,120 to spare; read as 300,000 bytes the gate would be passed by 2,080, where `4519341` was 2,592, `6e7a0d8` 1,056 and `becb56f` 32 over that reading, though every figure of the gate has been 1,024-byte KB, decision 20's 65 KB included; which reading holds is the owner's to say), room for about one more change the size of rounds 2 and 3, 3,072 bytes each, or three of round 5's first half, 1,536 (decision 20; mcp-mode.md §8 names a source of headroom, the tool definitions as a static JSON string). It was checked by the exe's size alone; `cargo bloat --release --crates`, which decision 20 names, isn't installed on the dev machine.
- Then 5.4 (live tool tests), 5.5 (interop) and 5.6 (agent trial 3).

### Phase 5.2 and 5.3 external review (October 18, 2026)

Reviewed `2f53735..a8c5390` at `a8c539014bad72ec5997da908b711ddcc6352e1f`. **Changes requested: R35–R37 are P2; R38–R39 are P3. No P1 finding.** The engine/MCP separation, shared run core, setup-only probe and read-only policy are sound foundations. The serving loop's response gate blocks cancellation and EOF, shutdown stops polling console events, and the aggregation selector unnecessarily regresses names at compatibility levels 110/120. Phase 5.2/5.3 is not yet accepted. The historical self-review records above remain evidence, not external closure. The fixes, and the internal review of them, are recorded in "Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes", below.

| Finding | Priority and location | Scenario, correction and acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| R35 | P2, `src/mcp/mod.rs:305–307`, stdin channel | At 64 unwritten responses the server disables `lines.recv`, which carries cancellation and EOF too. Independent release probe: a pending synthetic prelogin cancels immediately without backlog, but with 120 pings and unread stdout it stays open during 1.5 s and cancels only after stdout is drained. Separately, 120 pings followed by EOF leave the process running seven seconds later with no database work; draining stdout makes it exit about 15 ms later. Keep control/terminal events serviceable outside response admission, or apply a documented finite stalled-client policy that cancels work and ends the transport. Preserve queue bounds. Acceptance: unread-but-open stdout at a full queue, cancellation during connect and batch execution, EOF without draining, plus the existing slow-reader and broken-stdout controls. |
| R36 | P2, `src/mcp/mod.rs:333–383` | Console listeners remain installed during shutdown but are no longer polled, including in the blocking flush loop. Independent Ctrl+Break probe: serving ends in 47 ms; EOF first, then the event after 400 ms, takes another 5.64 s. Continue polling signals through all shutdown phases, cancel work immediately and shorten the remaining deadline without extending an earlier one; let the flush wait yield. Acceptance: real-process Ctrl+Break during the EOF wait and stalled flush, injectable close/logoff/shutdown signals for their shorter deadline, sessions released within the declared bound. |
| R37 | P2, `src/schema.rs:311–314` | Ordered STRING_AGG is supported from compatibility 110 on SQL Server 2025, but the new selector uses XML below 130. Valid catalog names containing XML-invalid characters can therefore regain R29's describe failure unnecessarily at 110/120. Five independent outgoing-SQL captures confirm XML at 100/110/120 and STRING_AGG at 130/170; no catalog SQL was executed. Select STRING_AGG at 110+, preserve ordering and nvarchar(max), keep the level-100 fallback separate, update docs. Acceptance: threshold cases plus an SQL-engine regression with a valid XML-invalid name at 110 or 120 and the ordinary level-100 case. [Microsoft boundary](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/functions/string-agg-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17#remarks). |
| R38 | P3, `src/mcp/protocol.rs:87,107–125`; id maps in `src/mcp/mod.rs` | serde_json's default number representation rounds large integer ids before validation. Independent pings at 18446744073709551616 and 18446744073709551617 echo the same rounded id; a subscription under the first falsely blocks the second as already open. A fractional id 1.5 is accepted. Preserve integer identity before floating-point conversion, compare consistently across calls/subscriptions/cancels and reject nonintegral ids. Explicit range refusals must precede execution and never substitute a rounded identity. Acceptance: numeric boundaries, distinct large ids, fractions, string/number distinction, cancellation and open-id collisions; remeasure size. [MCP ids](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index#requests). |
| R39 | P3, `src/mcp/tools.rs:316–318`, `src/mcp/mod.rs:550–558` | Method-envelope validation is incomplete: query with arguments:null gets an isError missing-sql result instead of -32602; subscriptions without the required notifications filter, or with toolsListChanged:"yes" and resourceSubscriptions:[1], are acknowledged and allocated. All reproduced independently. Validate these declared method fields before tool planning/subscription insertion. Omitted arguments may mean an empty object; explicit null is not an object. Require and type-check the known subscription filter fields. Acceptance: malformed envelope cases get -32602 and allocate no connection/subscription; valid envelopes with bad tool properties still get isError. [Published schemas](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/schema#calltoolrequestparams). |

**R34 verification, `6497dbb`:** the original CONTROL correction, SELECT prerequisite, preflight-versus-server-error distinction, coverage paragraph and traversal qualification are verified. One nonblocking wording item remains: schema-commands.md §6.7 adds SELECT `granted to public by default`, while the [current Microsoft permissions page](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/relational-databases/system-catalog-views/sys-sql-expression-dependencies-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17#permissions) describes a db_owner default. Remove the unnecessary parenthetical or qualify it as an evidenced observation on the tested target. No runtime change or owner access is needed. R34 remains open only for that qualification (removed in `bf00766`: Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, below).

**Judgment on the brief's seven questions:**

1. Accept decision 19 as built: setup-only probe, re-setup on an acknowledged live connection, one retry before user work when driver-dead. Clearing idle peers is a documented conservative idle-network policy with an availability cost under severe load. Keep the ten-second boundary explicit and retain regressions for its sides, replacement-login failure, cancellation during attention and a dead USE. No further engine code correction was found.
2. Do not accept swallowed console events during shutdown: R36.
3. No additional reset-state leak was independently demonstrated. Explicit isolation SET and driver-verified reset are appropriate. In 5.4 compare a fresh login with the next call on the same SPID after ROWCOUNT, TEXTSIZE, LANGUAGE, DATEFORMAT, DATEFIRST, CONTEXT_INFO and a global cursor, checking effects as well as values. App locks and sp_set_session_context are not on the current EXEC allowlist; assess them at the Session layer or when policy changes. Do not claim universal hygiene from the existing limited spike.
4. The intended -32602/isError split agrees with the [2026 tools page](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools#error-handling); R39 corrects concrete envelope exceptions. Do not turn all bad tool arguments back into protocol errors.
5. Accept the normal subscription lifecycle: holding an empty acknowledged subset open, then completion before teardown, matches the [subscriptions](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/subscriptions#graceful-closure) and [cancellation](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation) pages. Independent valid-subscription probe confirmed the order and metadata. R39 concerns input validation.
6. Current size passes the consistently binary calculation: +302,080 bytes (295 KiB), 5,120 bytes below 300 KiB; overall 2,789,888 bytes, 355,840 below 3 MiB. Accept this measurement, remeasure required fixes and record baseline delta. This review does not authorize raising the gate or settle the owner's decimal/binary wording question. Correctness takes priority over trimming solely for five KiB.
7. MCP advice is acceptable overall: call arguments are named where actionable, and launch flags explicitly identify operator/server actions. Add the listed missing exact-output advice cases in 5.4 or their corresponding fixes. No new advice abstraction is requested.

Independent `pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/check.ps1` passed in about 56 seconds: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, 349 unit tests (five ignored), 73 debug process tests (50 ignored), 70 release process tests (44 ignored), release size and the expected ten in-box DLL imports. Engine/protocol tests are included. Existing exact-output tests passed; `tests/golden` is unchanged, but live smoke goldens were not independently exercised. Ordinary CLI Advice::Cli/Closing branches retain their strings; R37 is the identified compatibility regression.

The additional synthetic process probes reproduce R35/R36/R38/R39 with controls, and five synthetic catalog replies capture R37's generated SQL. They do not execute SQL Server semantics. Input and response queues are bounded but can together retain hundreds of MiB at their worst-case default caps; those are estimates, not measured peaks. Preserve memory bounds in the backpressure fix and measure before raising the caps. The unexplained transient live-test failure in the self-review remains unexplained; capture its name/output if it recurs rather than treating passing reruns as a root-cause fix.

**No independent live SQL Server, reset-spike, CredMan or smoke-golden run was made.** Developer reports 50 debug and 44 release live passes at `9b5318d`, and the reset spike at `6e7a0d8`. The owner's profile/password/database were not accessed. Evidence: local `artifacts/phase5-review-check.log`, `artifacts/phase5-review-probes.json`, `artifacts/phase5-review-aggregate.json` and their `scratch/phase5-review-*.py` probes. Full review: ignored archive, `code-review-phase5-initial.md`. Only review/status docs were edited; no production source, Cargo, remote, workflow or signing action was changed.

### Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes (October 18, 2026)

The fixes for R34–R39 and the regressions and exact-output cases the review asked for (its questions 1 and 7), merged on `main` in `1ec0423` (`42d8d7c`, `2504b7c`, `bf00766`), `0950656` (`c3c4a63`, `c0d02cf`) and `d6e7b91` (`6e1a6e7`); then an internal review of those fixes, whose six findings were fixed in `9475781` (`4663978`, `f1d8b51`) and `367f8b0` (`a32859e`). **Verified and accepted at `c50dd3c`; R34–R39 closed, with nonblocking R40 below in Phase 5.2 and 5.3 fix verification.** docs/mcp-mode.md is the spec as built at `367f8b0`, with DESIGN.md §6, §9, §10 and §12 and schema-commands.md §3. At `367f8b0`, `pwsh scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed: 368 library unit tests, 88 debug and 82 release process tests, 51 debug and 45 release live tests, the release exe 2,805,248 bytes with the same ten in-box imports (Size, below). "Fails without its fix" means the test was run against the code before its fix, where it failed, unless the row says otherwise.

| Finding | Fix | Tests | Against the acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R35** (P2) | `6e1a6e7`, with the internal review's follow-ups in `f1d8b51` (below): stdin is read all the time. At 64 responses waiting for stdout the server is behind: a notification, a cancel among them, is acted on at once; a request is held back (`Deferred`), in arrival order, as its raw bytes (read again when let through, so the byte bound is exact), at most 64 or 16 MiB (`MAX_DEFERRED`, `MAX_DEFERRED_BYTES`; one more message, up to 4 MB, can pass the bytes), and only a full queue stops the reading of stdin. Answers decided on arrival (a malformed or too-long line, an open id's refusal) are held as finished lines in their place. When stdout catches up, what is held back goes first, in order (`replay`). A held-back request's id is open (`-32600`, `Invalid request: a request with this id is still waiting to run`), and a cancel for it drops it, unrun and unanswered. **The stall rule:** while behind, a stdout that takes no byte for the stall limit ends the server as if stdout had broken (`sqlh mcp: ending (stdout stalled)`): the calls cancelled, what is held back dropped. The limit is the longest of the listed profiles' stall seconds (each its query timeout), a 0 counting as 60 s since `f1d8b51` (`STALL_WHEN_NO_TIMEOUT`; `6e1a6e7` had no limit at 0). **At EOF** the held-back requests run as stdout drains, as long as it takes bytes (the stall rule bounds it, `stdout stalled during the end`), and only then do the calls get `END_GRACE` (`f1d8b51`; `6e1a6e7` ran `END_GRACE` from EOF). `--verbose` counts what never ran: `N responses were never written (M for requests that never ran)`. | Process (`tests/cli.rs`, `mcp::`; stdout a pipe held open, pre-filled and never read): `a_cancel_reaches_a_connecting_call_while_stdout_is_unread` (`-t 0`, 120 pings, 56 of them held back: the cancel closes the connecting call's connection within 3 s) and `a_cancel_reaches_a_running_batch_while_stdout_is_unread` (a fake SQL Server inside the test, on loopback, with a synthetic login whose password `fake` comes in `SQLCMDPASSWORD`: the batch's attention reaches it); `a_request_held_back_counts_as_open_and_a_cancel_drops_it` (the open id's `-32600`, the cancelled call never runs, every other answer once and in order once the client reads); `the_end_of_input_ends_the_server_with_stdout_unread` (at `-t 2` since `f1d8b51`: exit 0 in 1.5 to 5 s, `120 responses were never written (56 for requests that never ran)`, no cancel logged); `a_client_that_stops_reading_is_taken_for_gone` (`-t 1`, stdin open: under 5 s). All five failed against `src/mcp` before `6e1a6e7`. Unit `requests_held_back_are_bounded_in_order_and_cancelled_by_key` (new code, no old form). `f1d8b51`'s are in the internal review's table below. The existing `a_gone_client_ends_the_server_with_stdin_still_open`, `a_slow_reader_gets_a_long_last_response_whole` and `a_steady_slow_reader_is_not_taken_for_stopped` still pass. | Met: with stdout held open and unread and the response queue full, a cancel during a connect and during a batch takes effect at once while requests are held back below their bound, and once they are at it (where stdin isn't read) the transport ends within the documented finite stall bound, the query timeout or 60 s at `-t 0` (`f1d8b51`), the calls cancelled, all without stdout being drained; EOF the same. The memory bounds hold (64 waiting, 64 or 16 MiB held back; mcp-mode.md §2, Bounds, "In all"), and piped input with a reading client is unchanged. |
| **R36** (P2) | `6e1a6e7`: console events are taken at every wait of the end, after stdin closed, the settling, `close_all` and the flush, which is now an async wait (`End::wait`, every 10 ms) where it was a `thread::sleep` loop. **One deadline** (`End`): an event cuts the remaining calls and held-back requests at once and sets the deadline to the earlier of the current one and now plus `Signal::grace`, so a later event never extends it; the closing writes get up to `FLUSH_WAIT` (1 s) past it. So the end takes about 2 s at most after a close, logoff or shutdown and about 3 s after Ctrl+C or Ctrl+Break, within Windows' 5 s. The flush ends as soon as every queued line is written (it waited for the stdout thread to finish, which a cancelled call still settling held up). `sqlh mcp: EVENT during the end` is logged. The event source is a trait (`Events`), so a unit test scripts it. | Process `ctrl_break_after_stdin_closed_ends_the_server_at_once` (a call connecting to a silent listener, stdin closed, Ctrl+Break 400 ms later: exit 0 within 2 s of the event, the call's `exit 6 (cancelled)` logged, nothing on stdout) and `ctrl_break_during_the_last_writes_ends_them_within_its_grace` (a 2,000,000-byte `-32022` read about 40 KB/s, which would hold the flush to `END_GRACE`: exit within 3 s of the event, the answer cut short, `1 responses were never written`); both failed before `6e1a6e7`. Unit `mcp::tests::a_close_logoff_or_shutdown_during_the_end_bounds_it_by_its_grace` (scripted events, the engine's fake TDS server with an attention never acknowledged: a close, logoff or shutdown 300 ms into the end ends it at 300 ms plus 1 s, a Ctrl+Break at 600 ms doesn't put it off, nothing written, the unsettled call's connection closed once the runtime drops it); the old code had no injectable event source, so it was checked by a mutation instead: letting a later event extend the deadline fails it (2.6 s). | Met: real-process Ctrl+Break during the EOF finish wait and during a final write, and close, logoff and shutdown injected for their shorter deadline, each within its bound, its sessions released (the call settles, or its connection is closed with the runtime). The "stalled final write" is played by a slow reader: with a client that has stopped reading, `FLUSH_WAIT` ends the flush after 1 s with or without the fix, so a stopped reader can't show it. No user console or machine shutdown is triggered: Ctrl+Break goes to a child in a process group of its own, and the other three are scripted. |
| **R37** (P2) | `2504b7c`: `Aggregate::for_level` picks ordered `STRING_AGG` at compatibility level 110 and up, `FOR XML PATH` only at 100 and when the lookup returns no level; the `nvarchar(max)` cast, ordering and quoting unchanged. Owner decision 21 (`5cc3670`) allows the live test (as built, below). schema-commands.md §3 and DESIGN.md §9 updated in this docs pass. | Unit `lists_are_aggregated_as_the_database_level_allows` (100, 110, 120, 130 and 170, and from the lookup's reply to `describe`'s SQL: `FOR XML PATH` at 100 only; with the old cutoff it fails, `left: ForXml, right: StringAgg`). Fixture `a_changed_compatibility_level_is_put_back_even_when_a_step_fails` (no server: a failed save or change still runs the restore, before the saved level's table is dropped). | Met: the 100/110/120/130 boundary, an SQL Server regression with a valid XML-invalid name at 110 and 120, and the ordinary level-100 case; the spec's threshold updated. No older-server branch added. |
| **R38** (P3) | `6e1a6e7`: an id is a string or an integer `serde_json` reads exactly (`is_i64() \|\| is_u64()`); anything else is `-32600` without an id, `Invalid request: id must be a string or an integer within 64 bits`: fractions, exponent forms, `1.0`, `-0` and integers past both ranges. `protocol::key`, the id's JSON text, is the one rule for calls, held-back requests, subscriptions and cancels (subscriptions compared values before). No new crate, no `serde_json` feature change. | Process `ids_are_kept_exactly_and_compared_as_written` (the i64 and u64 bounds echoed exactly; `18446744073709551616`, `18446744073709551617`, `1.5`, `1e2`, `1.0` refused without an id; `"1"` and `1` two ids; a subscription under the u64 maximum doesn't block `18446744073709551614`; cancels by the same rule, one with a `requestId` past 64 bits matching nothing; the exact message count): failed before `6e1a6e7`. Unit `lines_are_requests_notifications_or_errors`, extended (`-9223372036854775809`, `-0`, each accepted id's key as written); not run against the old code, which accepted those inputs. | Met: numeric boundaries (all but `9223372036854775808`, one past the i64 maximum, which the code takes as a `u64` and no test sends), the two large integers, a fraction, strings against numbers, cancellation and open-id collisions; distinct accepted ids stay distinct and echo exactly. The 64-bit range is imposed deliberately and refused before anything runs, never with a rounded id put in place. Size remeasured (below). |
| **R39** (P3) | `6e1a6e7`, follow-up `4663978` (below): each method's envelope is checked before any work, `-32602` `Invalid params: MEMBER must be WHAT`. `tools/call`: `arguments` left out is none, present it must be an object (`null` refused); `inputResponses` an object, `requestState` a string. `subscriptions/listen`: `notifications` required, an object; `toolsListChanged`, `promptsListChanged`, `resourcesListChanged` booleans, `resourceSubscriptions` strings; a malformed filter takes no slot. `tools/list`: `cursor` a string (`cursor must be a string`; a string still `no such cursor`). `initialize`: `protocolVersion` a string, `capabilities` an object, `clientInfo` an object with string `name` and `version`; a malformed one agrees no version.
