**The regressions the review asked for (question 1)**, `c3c4a63` and `c0d02cf` (tests only; `c0d02cf` gives a `USE` gone silent the driver's clock 1 s of slack: an elapsed time of at least 10 s where it was 11 s, its connect time still at least 10,000 ms), against the pooled fake TDS server, with one addition to it, `Conn::refusing_login` (error 18456, class 14, then a DONE with the error bit, then the close). None had a fix to remove, so each was checked by breaking the engine on purpose, and fails then:
- `a_setup_acknowledged_within_the_attention_wait_keeps_its_connection_and_its_peer`: a reused setup answered at 9 s succeeds in 9 to 12 s on the same connection, no new login, both idle connections kept, connect at least 9,000 ms (fails if the probe never keeps a cut connection).
- `a_setup_silent_past_the_attention_wait_is_replaced_with_its_idle_peer`: answered at 11 s, a third connection, the healthy idle peer closed with the dead one (fails if the retry stops dropping the idle peers).
- `a_replacement_that_cannot_log_in_fails_the_call_and_frees_its_slot`: `--pool 1`, exit 2 with the exact 18456 error and the profile's hint, no third attempt, the permit released, the next call connects (fails if a failed connect leaks the permit).
- `a_cancel_during_the_probes_attention_wait_is_neither_set_up_again_nor_retried`: a cancel at 6 s; acknowledged at 7 s, the connection pooled after `SET NOEXEC OFF` and the rollback, no transaction left, the next call reusing it with a reset; never acknowledged, dropped at about 10 s, not retried; both end `query timeout after 5 s in the session setup`, exit 4, which `sqlh mcp` never sends (fails if either cancel check is removed).
- `a_connection_that_dies_at_the_calls_use_is_retried_with_its_use`: closed after the setup, or the `USE` unanswered past a 6 s timeout: retried once, the setup and `USE [d];` again on the new connection; the silent one in 10 to 16 s, connect at least 10,000 ms (fails if a failure at the `USE` is never retried).

**The exact-output cases the review asked for (question 7)**, `c3c4a63`, which pin wording (not checked by breaking the code; they found the code matching every row of mcp-mode.md §4's tables): `output::tests::in_mcp_footers_and_the_result_set_note_name_the_calls_limits`; `output::text::tests::in_mcp_the_limits_a_run_met_are_named_as_its_reader_can_change_them`, `in_mcp_a_stopped_count_offers_what_the_call_can_change`, `in_mcp_a_cut_definition_names_the_servers_output_limit_and_max_cell`; `run::tests::the_dirty_read_note_and_an_unconfirmed_count_stop_name_what_can_change` (both forms; the CLI's unconfirmed count stop's first test); engine `in_mcp_a_batch_timeout_offers_what_the_call_can_raise` and `in_mcp_a_lookup_timeout_offers_what_the_call_can_raise` (server limits 30, 0 and 1), `in_mcp_the_wait_for_a_free_connection_says_what_was_busy_and_what_can_change` (the only connection, all 2, at the limit), `in_mcp_the_check_note_names_the_tool`, `in_mcp_the_dirty_read_note_names_the_profiles_setting`, `in_mcp_an_unconfirmed_count_stop_names_the_profiles_exact_count`. They pinned one thing the docs didn't state: an unconfirmed count stop leaves the result it cut with no footer, and the status line without `counting stopped in N` (now DESIGN.md §6). The engine has 31 tests (20 before). The exe size is unchanged by them.

**Also merged:** `42d8d7c`, outside the review: the smoke test's JSON golden held the checkout's own path as each batch's `source`, so `the_smoke_script_matches_its_golden_output` failed in any other checkout, a worktree's included; `normalize_json` now writes it `<repo>/tests/smoke.sql` (DESIGN.md §12).

**The internal review of the fixes** (six verdicts on the merged R35–R39 fixes, all confirmed; one P2 and five P3, each reproduced with the debug exe, synthetic loopback profiles and stdout pipes, or confirmed from the code). Fixed in `f1d8b51`, `4663978` and `a32859e`:

| # | Finding | Fix | Tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 (P2) | **R35 was still open at `-t 0`** (or a profile's `query_timeout = 0`): `stall_limit` returned none for a 0, so once 64 answers waited and 64 requests (or 16 MiB) were held back, a cancel and stdin's end waited unread for good. Reproduced: 128 pings then a cancel, the call's connection still open after 15 s; EOF after 128 pings still running after 15 s; at `-t 2` both ended at about 2 s. `6e1a6e7`'s tests used 120 pings, under the threshold. | `f1d8b51`: `STALL_WHEN_NO_TIMEOUT` (60 s) stands for a 0 in the stall limit alone (a query's own timeout is unchanged), so the transport ends under a finite bound whatever `-t` is. Beyond the brief: the longest listed profile's limit still wins, so a profile over 60 s listed beside one at 0 sets it (for the reviewer to confirm, mcp-mode.md §10). Debug builds take `SQLH_DEBUG_MCP_STALL_MS` in place of the 60 s, so the tests needn't wait; a release build reads no variable. | Unit `the_stall_limit_is_always_finite` (written against the new function, so it doesn't build on the old). Process, debug only, at `-t 0` with the limit at 1 s, behind a full queue: `a_cancel_behind_a_full_queue_at_t_0_ends_within_the_stall_bound` (a connecting call's connection closed within 4 s of the unread cancel), `a_cancel_of_a_running_batch_behind_a_full_queue_at_t_0_ends_within_the_stall_bound` (the batch's attention within 4 s), `the_end_of_input_behind_a_full_queue_at_t_0_ends_within_the_stall_bound` (full by count, 130 pings, and by bytes, 64 pings and five of 4 MB: exit 0 within 5 s). Each hangs at `6e1a6e7`. The two cancel tests fill the queue by count alone, so the form full by bytes is played only at the end of the input; the bound is the same `Deferred::full`, whose bytes the unit test `requests_held_back_are_bounded_in_order_and_cancelled_by_key` checks. |
| 2 (P3) | **A steady slow reader that pipes its requests and closes stdin lost answers `a8c5390` delivered:** reading ahead, the server saw EOF early, and the one `END_GRACE` after it had to cover both dispatching the held-back requests and their work. Reproduced: 120 pings and a call, read at about 1 KB/s: 90 of 121 answered, the call never ran, `31 responses were never written (12 for requests that never ran)`; `a8c5390` answered all 121. | `f1d8b51`: after EOF, first the held-back requests run for as long as stdout keeps taking bytes (no limit of its own; the stall rule, now always finite, ends a stopped client, and a console event drops what is left), then, nothing held back, the calls get `END_GRACE`. `End::wait` takes an optional limit for that first wait. A held-back request the end drops counts among `M for requests that never ran` and is never logged as a cancel. | Process `a_steady_slow_reader_gets_every_answer_after_stdin_closes` (135 requests, the first 64 answered with about 2 KB each, a call among the 64 held back, read 2 KB every 170 ms: every request answered once, nothing `never written`); reverting only this fix, `31 responses were never written (31 for requests that never ran)`. `the_end_of_input_ends_the_server_with_stdout_unread` now runs at `-t 2`, since its held-back requests now wait for the stall rule, not `END_GRACE` (adjusted, not a regression test). |
| 3 (P3) | **The serving loop could sleep up to 30 s with requests held back:** it re-read `behind` after `replay`, and armed its poll on that alone, so a stdout that caught up in between, without waking the loop, left them held back until the 30 s sweep. Forced in a scratch copy with an inserted delay: 96 pings took 29.7 s, a 29.4 s gap, against 0.336 s without it. | `f1d8b51`: the poll is armed whenever stdout is behind or anything is held back, read once after `replay`; only the serving loop changes the held-back queue, so the stdout thread can't empty it between the two reads. | None: no input makes the stdout thread finish a line in that instant, and no delay hook was added to the product's code. |
| 4 (P3) | **R39 left a request's `params: null` taken as no params,** the null-means-omitted rule it removed for `arguments`. Reproduced: `tools/list`, `ping` and `server/discover` with `params: null` answered; `tools/call` with it got `tools/call needs a tool name`. JSON-RPC 2.0 §4.2: params, when present, MUST be structured. | `4663978`: only `params` left out is none; a request's `null` or other non-object is `-32602` `Invalid params: params must be an object`, with its id; a notification's goes without, unanswered. | `lines_are_requests_notifications_or_errors` (`null` for four methods; a notification's) and `method_envelopes_are_checked_before_anything_runs` (ids 41 to 43, and a `notifications/cancelled` with `null`): reverting only this fix fails both. |
| 5 (P3) | **R39's `_meta` audit missed the declared `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel`** (2026-07-28's `RequestMetaObject`, a deprecated `LoggingLevel`): `5` or `null` was accepted. | `4663978`: `META_LOG_LEVEL` and `Kind::LogLevel`, the eight strings; anything else `-32602` `Invalid params: _meta io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel must be one of debug, info, notice, warning, error, critical, alert, emergency`. | `the_era_follows_the_request_and_initialize` (`5`, `null`, `verbose`, `INFO` refused; the eight taken) and the process test (27 to 29 refused, 40 taken): reverting only this fix fails both. |
| 6 (P3) | **A run killed while the level test had changed `sqlh_test`'s compatibility level left the other live tests to run at it** until the level test's own next run put it back, and a run that filtered that test out never did (from the code; not reproduced by killing a run on the owner's server). | `a32859e` (`tests/cli.rs` only): the start-of-run repair (decision 21 as built, below). | `live::a_level_a_killed_run_left_is_put_back_before_any_live_test_runs` (no server: a saved level with a shared and with an alone first hold, nothing saved and nothing written, a failed restore, a failed check); it fails against four mutants of `hold_scratch` (no start step, main's behavior; the restore without the exclusive lock; a failed step still taking the lock; the step on every hold). `a_changed_compatibility_level_is_put_back_even_when_a_step_fails` also checks that the level test arms the same restore (`restore_level`). Live, under the exclusive lock: a saved 170 with the level left at 120, then `check_never_executes`: main's code left 120 and the table, the fixed code put back 170 and dropped it; a saved level the server refuses (99): every live test in the process failed with the reason (error 15048) and the SQL that finishes it, from one attempt. `sqlh_test` was left at 170 with no saved table. |

**A review of those follow-up fixes** (`f1d8b51`, `4663978`, `a32859e`; three findings, P3, each verified by a skeptic): two confirmed, both wording, fixed in the comments (the commit after `367f8b0`). The `END_GRACE` and `end` docs in `src/mcp/mod.rs` said the stall rule bounds the replay of held-back requests after stdin closes; it ends a client that stops reading, and the replay has no limit of its own while stdout takes bytes. The live module's and `hold_scratch`'s docs claimed more for the start-of-run repair than it does: it covers a run killed before this process's first hold, and one killed later, in another process, is repaired at the next process's start (the live fixture's limits, below). One refuted as a regression: after the replay, the answers still queued get the flush wait's `END_GRACE` (6 s) at most, so a reader too slow to take them in that time loses the rest, which `--verbose` counts (120 answers of about 2 KB read at 12 KB/s: 31 never written, the same at `a8c5390`); kept as built and documented (mcp-mode.md §2, The end), and listed below.

**Decision 21, as built** (`2504b7c`, `a32859e`; `tests/cli.rs`, `live`):
- **The saved level:** the level test saves `sqlh_test`'s level in a table of its own with a fixed name, `dbo.probe_compatibility_level` (`SELECT ... INTO`), and arms the restore before the save (R24): the restore sets the saved level back (`ALTER DATABASE CURRENT SET COMPATIBILITY_LEVEL`, inside TRY/CATCH) and drops the table only once the level is set back, so a failed restore keeps the table and the level to put back. A table a killed run left is kept, since its level is the original one. A change of level also requires the table.
- **The server-side guard:** every batch that saves, changes or restores the level starts with `IF DB_NAME() <> N'sqlh_test' OR OBJECT_ID(N'dbo.sqlh_scratch', N'U') IS NULL THROW ...`, so the server refuses it anywhere but the marked scratch database (the test checks, read-only, that `master` refuses it).
- **The lock:** every live test holds a lock on a file in the temp directory (`sqlh-live-scratch.lock`), so it holds across processes (the debug and release runs, another worktree's): shared, through `target()`, from a test's first call until its thread ends; exclusive, through `target_alone()`, for the level test, so no other live test, here or in another process, runs while the level is changed (about 9 s of changed level in each run). A worktree whose code predates the lock doesn't take it.
- **The repair, before the first shared lock** (`a32859e`): once per test process (a `OnceLock` reached from both `target()` and `target_alone()` through `hold_scratch`), the first live test takes the lock exclusively, checks read-only for the saved level's table and, if it is there, runs the level test's own guarded restore (`restore_level`), then lets the lock go and takes its own hold. If the check or the restore fails, no live test in the process takes the lock: each fails with the reason and, for the restore, the SQL that finishes it, and the step isn't tried again. This holds for a run that filters the level test out, too. **Limits:** a level test killed in another process after this process's start step leaves this process's later tests at the changed level until the next process starts; and the start step waits until no other process holds the lock shared, so a live run in another worktree can hold this one at its start, as `target_alone` already could (a gentler design, checking under the shared lock and taking it alone only when the table exists, wasn't taken).

**Size: decision 20's gate was exceeded, and the owner amended the decision on 2026-10-18 (option C, below; §6).** `cargo build --release --locked` at `367f8b0`: 2,805,248 bytes, 317,440 (310.0 KiB) over Phase 4's 2,487,808, 10,240 bytes past the 300 KiB (307,200-byte) gate, and 340,480 under the 3 MB budget. `6e1a6e7` added 13,824 bytes, to 2,803,712 (308.5 KiB over); the internal review's fixes 1,536, to 2,805,248; `2504b7c` and the test-only commits nothing. Past the gate read as 300,000 bytes too, so the reading question no longer decides it. The review allowed no silent raise and no trimming of correct behavior for the margin; trimming tried in `6e1a6e7` (a non-generic `End::wait`, 3 KiB, and a `Vec` for the held-back queue, 2 KiB, kept; `close_all` as its own task, 9 KiB more, reverted; `#[inline(never)]`, nothing). `cargo bloat` isn't installed, so the growth was located by comparing MSVC linker map files: code +12,320 bytes, strings +1,376, spread over the new code. **Options put to the owner:** (A) raise the MCP gate to 350 KiB; (C) build `serde_json` with `opt-level = "z"`, measured at 2,795,520 bytes (307,712 over, 300.5 KiB), and raise the gate to 350 KiB; (B) `opt-level = "z"` alone, within 300 KiB, no longer fits. **The owner chose C:** `serde_json` built for size, the gate 350 KiB in 1,024-byte KB; as built, 2,795,520 bytes at the commit that applied it: 307,712 over Phase 4, 300.5 KiB, 50,688 bytes (49.5 KiB) under the 350 KiB (358,400-byte) gate, and 350,208 under the 3 MB budget.

**Still open, for the reviewer's fix verification and the owner:**
- **The stall limit with several profiles** (internal review, finding 1): the longest listed profile's limit wins over the 60 s that stands for a 0; for the reviewer to confirm.
- **The flush wait's `END_GRACE` after stdin closes** (the review of the follow-up fixes, above): kept as before R35; lifting it for a reader that keeps taking bytes would be a design change, for the reviewer or the owner.
- **The live fixture's two limits** (above): a level test killed in another process after this process's start step, and a concurrent run waiting at the start step.
- **No automated test yet** (mcp-mode.md §9 has the list): the poll fix (finding 3); a console event while the server is still serving, Ctrl+C itself and a real close, logoff or shutdown; the flush wait's `FLUSH_WAIT` end for a client that stopped reading below 64 waiting answers; the stall limit from several profiles and its 60 s in a release build; held-back requests let through while serving; `close_all`'s `END_GRACE` bound; the exit with a name lookup still running; an `allow` profile's `read_uncommitted = true` in MCP; the lookup timeout's CLI message; the 4060 and 18452 hints in MCP; the probe at `timeout` 0, and a probe-cut setup that dies or runs out at its setup again; the launch's console-stdin refusal, escaping and listener failure; the argument refusals and echoes the unit test doesn't reach; a running call's reused id, and `server/discover` and the other malformed forms under an open id; `sqlh --profile x profile`; the JSON `connect_ms` and `info`'s connect time after a probe or a retry.
- **Still advising the CLI in sqlh mcp** (unchanged from the list above: the launch's resolution errors, the unknown-flag parenthetical, the 18456 hint under `SQLCMDPASSWORD`, a `GO N` past 1000 labeled `-Q`).
- **The one-off live failure at `9b5318d`** stays unexplained (the review: keep it visible, and capture the test's name and output if it recurs). 5.4 found one possible cause, not confirmed: every Reader login of a test process had one name, so two Reader tests running at once could refuse or drop each other's login (fixed in `fbbe1a8`; Phase 5.4, below).
- **5.4's reset comparison** (the review's question 3): a fresh login against the next call on the same SPID after `SET ROWCOUNT`, `TEXTSIZE`, `LANGUAGE`, `DATEFORMAT`, `DATEFIRST`, `CONTEXT_INFO` and a global cursor, effects as well as values; app locks and `sp_set_session_context` at the session layer or when the `EXEC` allowlist changes. No speculative reset statements. **Done in 5.4:** none of them survives the reset, and the setup is unchanged (Phase 5.4, below).
- Then 5.4 (live tool tests, done 2026-10-18), 5.5 (interop) and 5.6 (agent trial 3).

### Phase 5.2 and 5.3 fix verification (October 18, 2026)

Reviewed `a8c5390..c50dd3c`. **R34–R39 are closed; Phase 5.2 and 5.3 are accepted with one new nonblocking P3 interop finding, R40.** Next are 5.4–5.6, not a claim that live tool coverage and client interop are already complete.

| Finding | Verification |
|---|---|
| R34 | Closed. The unsupported default-public-grant parenthetical is removed; the corrected prerequisites, refusal/error distinction, CONTROL rationale and coverage/traversal limits remain. |
| R35 | Closed. Notifications pass while stdout is behind; held requests have count/byte limits and the full-queue fallback always has a finite stall limit, including `-t 0`. Independent release probe with 120 pings: cancellation closes a pending prelogin immediately with stdout unread. EOF with unread stdout at `-t 1` exits in 1.094 s. Debug `-t 0`, full held queue, 800 ms seam: exits in 0.891 s, counting 64 requests that never ran. Full-queue input can still delay a cancel until the stall policy ends the transport; that is the accepted bounded-stalled-client alternative, not a promise of control priority beyond every queue cap. The full standard check also passed the real-batch, byte-cap, held-id and slow-reader regressions. |
| R36 | Closed. Every end phase polls events, the deadline only shrinks, and the async final flush has its documented one-second floor. The whole event-driven end is about three seconds for Ctrl+C/Break and two for close/logoff/shutdown. Independent Ctrl+Break after EOF exits in 31 ms, with 16 ms serving control. The real-process flush-event and scripted shorter-event tests passed. No actual close/logoff/shutdown event was sent. |
| R37 | Closed. Independent release CLI captures select XML at 100 only and STRING_AGG at 110/120/130/170; ordering, casts and quoting remain. The live fixture saves/arms restoration before changes under the exclusive scratch lock and checks odd names at 110/120, ordinary ordered output at 100 and restoration. Its reported live pass/failure-under-old-cutoff was reviewed, not independently rerun. |
| R38 | Closed for loss/collision. Accepted i64/u64 boundaries echo exactly, and former colliding out-of-range ids and nonintegral 1.5 are explicitly refused without a substituted id. Calls, subscriptions, deferred requests and cancels share one key rule. New R40 concerns integral numeric forms, not renewed rounding. |
| R39 | Closed. Eleven independent malformed-envelope cases returned -32602, while valid argument objects with missing/wrong SQL produced isError. Extension metadata and the normal subscription lifecycle remain accepted. The original null arguments, filter and allocation defects are fixed. |

**R40 [P3], `src/mcp/protocol.rs:136`: integral numeric-id forms are unnecessarily refused.** The new integer-storage check rejects raw `1.0`, `1e2` and `-0`, independently confirmed, although they are exact integral values within the supported range. [MCP ids](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index#requests) require string/integer values, and [JSON Schema's numeric rules](https://json-schema.org/understanding-json-schema/reference/numeric#integer) distinguish values from lexical spelling. Normalize exact integral values losslessly and consistently before keying; do not use a rounded f64 integrality test that could reopen R38. Keep true fractions and unsupported magnitude refused. Acceptance: 1/1.0/1e0 share a numeric identity, -0/0 share one, an exponent-form cancel reaches numeric id 100, string ids stay distinct, and nonintegral/out-of-range inputs never alias accepted ids. Remeasure size. Originally open and nonblocking; **closed by external verification of `c50dd3c..0d4cf2c` on October 18, 2026** (R40 verification below). **Fixed in `aa06856` (owner, 2026-10-18: now rather than with 5.5),** independently verified below. A numeric id `serde_json` doesn't read as an i64 or u64 is decided from the number as the line writes it: `protocol::raw_member` finds the member's text (names decoded, the last of a duplicate, as `serde_json` keeps it) and `protocol::exact_id` shifts the digits by the exponent as text, never through a float, so an exact integer within i64 or u64 is the id as that integer, and a fraction or a magnitude past 64 bits is refused without an id as before. The same rule takes a cancel's `requestId`. Against the acceptance: `1`, `1.0` and `1e0` one request, `-0` and `0` one, a cancel's `1e2` reaching subscription `100`, `"1"` distinct, `1.0000000000000001` (which a float would round to 1) and `1e20` refused, every response echoing the integer: process test `an_integral_id_is_one_request_however_it_is_written` (fails on the old parser); unit tests `an_integral_number_is_its_integer_however_it_is_written` (24 cases, among them `18446744073709551615.0`, `1.8446744073709551615e19`, `-9223372036854775808.0`, `0e999999999999999999999`, `1e-999999999999999999999`) and `a_members_text_is_found_as_the_line_writes_it` (an escaped name, a duplicate, a name inside a string); R38's regressions kept (`ids_are_kept_exactly_and_compared_as_written`, now with `1e2` and `1.0` answered as `100` and `1`). Size: 2,802,688 bytes, 7,168 more than `c50dd3c`: MCP 314,880 bytes (307.5 KiB) over Phase 4, 43,520 (42.5 KiB) under decision 20's 350 KiB gate, and the exe 343,040 under 3 MB. `check.ps1` passes.

**Answers to the six fix-brief questions:**

1. Accept the 60 s stand-in at `-t 0` and longest-profile rule as the initial operator-controlled transport bound. It does not change query timeout semantics. A full queue alongside a 300 s profile can indeed delay recovery 300 s; do not describe that case as quick cancellation. Revisit an independent stall ceiling if trials show that tradeoff is unsuitable. Source/unit selection was checked, not a release 60 s or mixed-profile wall-clock run.
2. Accept 64 or 16 MiB plus one admitted message. Byte accounting covers pop/cancel/clear; the one-message overshoot is bounded. Raw input lines, deferred bytes, parse temporaries, collected results and encoded responses still add to memory. These are bounds, not measured peaks; keep the caps until combined memory is measured.
3. Keep the final six-second flush cap. Replay can progress without its own deadline while held work is bounded and bytes move; a stopped reader still triggers the stall rule. The final cap bounds process exit and can truncate a sufficiently slow consumer after EOF. Clients should await responses before closing stdin or keep reading promptly. Console events remain effective in every phase.
4. R38's collision fix is accepted, but the integral examples do not become nonintegers because serde_json stores them as f64. R40 is the low-priority interop follow-up.
5. The fields consumed by the implemented methods and the original envelope cases are covered. This is not complete recursive schema validation of every unused optional Implementation/capability field or extension payload. No generic schema validator is requested; avoid claiming a proof that every possible invalid nested metadata value is rejected.
6. Accept the documented next-process-start recovery window for the current test workflow and sequential debug/release live checks. OnceLock does not repair a writer killed later while an already-started process waits for a new shared hold. If overlapping kill testing is supported, prefer a durable dirty/generation marker checked on each new admission, with shared hold released before exclusive repair. Do not claim that stronger guarantee now. This test-only limit does not block the engine or R37 closure.

Decision 20's explicit amendment resolves the old gate concern: at `c50dd3c`, 2,795,520 bytes is +307,712 (300.5 KiB) over Phase 4, 50,688 below the new 350 KiB incremental gate and 350,208 below 3 MiB overall. The serde_json opt-level z override is package-scoped; no new dependency or Cargo.lock change. The new engine/probe/USE/advice regressions are tests only, and smoke JSON path normalization is confined to the test's source field.

Independent `scripts/check.ps1` passed in about 55 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, 368 units (five ignored), 88 debug processes (51 ignored), 82 release processes (45 ignored), size and ten in-box DLL imports. Additional unmodified-exe probes cover cancellation/EOF/stall, no-timeout full queue through the debug seam, console events, exact numeric boundaries and malformed envelopes; five synthetic catalog replies confirm the aggregation selector. They do not execute T-SQL. **No independent live server, reset spike, CredMan or smoke-golden run:** developer reports 51 debug and 45 release live passes at c50dd3c, R37's fixture included. The prior transient live failure remains unexplained.

Evidence is local in `artifacts/phase5-fixes-review-check.log`, `artifacts/phase5-fixes-review-probes.json`, `artifacts/phase5-fixes-review-aggregate.json` and their `scratch/phase5-fixes-review-*.py` probes. Full review: ignored `archive/reviews/code-review-phase5-fixes.md`. Prior probes/evidence were preserved. No owner profile/password/database was used; only review/status docs were edited, with no production, Cargo, remote, workflow or signing changes.

### R40 fix verification (October 18, 2026)

Reviewed `c50dd3c..0d4cf2c`, the fix in `aa06856`. **R40 closed; Phase 5.2 and 5.3 remain accepted.** One separate nonblocking P3 follow-up, R41, records the tool-argument precision issue identified in the developer's brief; it does not reopen numeric-id identity or R38.

The normalized integer response is accepted: JSON-RPC correlates by the same numeric value, so 1.0 can return 1, 1e2 can return 100 and -0 can return 0. String ids remain distinct. The narrow raw-member reader is accepted at its 7 KiB cost: it runs only after serde_json validates the line, decodes member names, keeps the last duplicate, and uses fixed shallow lookup paths with bounded linear scans. Its exact digit/exponent arithmetic avoids a float integrality decision. Keep it private to validated JSON; no new parser crate or arbitrary_precision feature is required. The front parser's own numeric/depth limits still apply.

Independent rebuilt-release probes: 736 raw numeric pings agree with Python Decimal's exact integer/range decisions, including i64/u64 boundaries, decimal/exponent variants, the old colliding integers, near-integer fractions, underflow and extreme exponents. Six raw-member cases cover escaped names, duplicate order, nested/string decoys, Unicode, case sensitivity and a distinct name with an embedded NUL. Escaped and duplicate nested params/requestId cancel subscription 100 using 1e2; a rounded fractional requestId does not cancel it. A real driver prelogin stalled on a synthetic loopback listener under call id 100.0 is closed by that exponent-form cancel within two seconds, before EOF. Retained process tests verify numeric equivalence, string distinction, open-id collisions and R38 regressions.

**R41 [P3], `src/mcp/tools.rs:376-391`: integer tool limits are still decided through rounded f64.** Raw `10.0000000000000001` is accepted for each of max_rows, max_cell and timeout; `max_rows: -1e-9999` underflows to negative zero and is accepted as 0 (all rows within max-output). Independent requests reach a closed-port connection failure, whereas max_rows 10.5 and negative tiny timeout fail as usage errors before connecting. This contradicts the declared integer/range contract but neither miscorrelates ids nor lifts server timeout/output caps. Preserve original argument number text and apply exact integral-value validation before range checks; converting an already rounded Number back to text is insufficient. Keep present invalid arguments distinct from omissions. Acceptance: raw process cases reject near-integer fractions, negative underflow, just-outside bounds and unsupported magnitudes before connecting, with the existing isError/exit-3 argument result; integral decimal/exponent forms, valid zero limits, bounds and timeout clamp still work; remeasure size. Originally open and nonblocking. **R41 closed by external verification of `0d4cf2c..3472910` on October 18, 2026; new P2 R42 below requires a fix before this increment is accepted.** **Fixed in `a55c70d` (owner, 2026-10-18: now rather than with 5.5),** independently verified below. `protocol::parse` reads a `tools/call`'s numeric arguments as it reads ids: a member `serde_json` read as a float becomes the integer its text is (`raw_member`, `exact_integer`, the renamed `exact_id`, now shared), or stays a float, and `tools::plan` takes only a `u64` within each bound, so a float that reaches it is refused, never rounded. Against the acceptance: near-integer fractions (`10.0000000000000001` for each of the three), the negative underflow (`-1e-9999` for `max_rows` and `timeout`), just outside the bounds (`max_rows` 10001, `timeout` 0) and an unsupported magnitude (`max_cell` 1e30) each get the run's usage error, `isError: true`, before any connect; `30.0`, `3e1`, `1e1` and a zero written `-0` or `0.0` are taken and reach the connect; the bounds and the timeout clamp are unchanged: process test `a_tool_calls_numeric_limits_are_exact` (a closed port, so an admitted call shows `cannot connect`; fails without the fix), unit tests `a_tool_calls_numeric_arguments_are_integers_from_their_text` (only a tool call's arguments are read so) and the plan's cases in `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema` (`30`, `30.0`, `3e1`, `300e-1` taken as 30; `30.0000000000000001`, `-1e-9999`, `1e30`, `30.5`, `0`, `-5.0` refused for `timeout`; zero however written kept for `max_rows` and `max_cell`). Size: 2,804,736 bytes, 2,048 more than `aa06856`: MCP 316,928 bytes (309.5 KiB) over Phase 4, 41,472 (40.5 KiB) under the 350 KiB gate, and the exe 340,992 under 3 MB. `check.ps1` passes (371 unit tests; 90 debug and 84 release process tests).

Independent `scripts/check.ps1` passed in 159 seconds: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied; 370 units (five ignored), 89 debug processes (51 ignored), 83 release processes (45 ignored); expected ten in-box DLL imports. Release exe 2,802,688 bytes, +7,168 from c50dd3c; MCP +314,880 bytes (307.5 KiB) over Phase 4, 43,520 under the owner's 350 KiB gate and 343,040 under 3 MiB overall. No Cargo/dependency changes. No independent live server, CredMan or smoke-golden run; the developer did not rerun live tests for this protocol-only fix either. Synthetic profiles/loopback only; no owner credentials or data.

Evidence: `artifacts/r40-review-check.log`, `artifacts/r40-review-probes.json`, `artifacts/r40-review-probes-run.log`, and `scratch/r40-review-probes.py`.
