### R41 fix verification (October 18, 2026)

Reviewed `0d4cf2c..3472910`, fix `a55c70d`. **R41 closed for exact integer tool-limit validation. New P2 R42 requires a fix before this increment is accepted.** R40 remains closed; the earlier Phase 5.2/5.3 acceptance records the prior implementation, not acceptance of this new parser-work regression.

The renamed `exact_integer` retains R40's exact arithmetic. Floats normalize only when the original number text is an exact supported integer; other floats remain present and fail the plan's u64/range checks. Independent rebuilt-release raw-JSON cases: 21 passed (16 refused, five admitted), covering all three near-integer fractions and negative underflows, tiny fractions just above each maximum, timeout just below 1, unsupported magnitude, null, omitted defaults, upper-bound decimal/exponent values, signed zero and last-duplicate/escaped three-level paths. Refusals are the existing isError/exit-3 argument result before connecting; admitted controls reach a synthetic closed port. The standard check retains R38/R40 identity/cancellation regressions. Timeout clamp and engine settings code are unchanged; no new live clamp measurement.

**R42 [P2], `src/mcp/protocol.rs:220-229`: generic float-argument rereading performs quadratic work on the serving thread.** Every floating-point argument, including unknown ones, triggers a new whole-line raw_member lookup before tool-schema refusal. With K float members and L bytes, work is O(K * L). `src/mcp/mod.rs:574,792` calls this synchronously inside serving, so cancels, driver tasks, query timers, stall checks and console events cannot be polled while it runs. The 4 MiB byte cap does not adequately bound this repeated work. Independent release scaling: 500/1,000/2,000 unknown `1.0` members (9,720/19,220/38,220 bytes) complete in 0.359/1.296/5.375 s; 4,000 members, 76,220 bytes, do not finish within 12 s and the synthetic child is terminated. Integer `1` controls with 2,000 or 4,000 members finish in 0.032 s. A real pending driver prelogin with the 2,000-float call ahead of its cancel closes only after 4.765 s, versus immediately at timer resolution with integers. No stdout backpressure; EOF is sent only after closure. No SQL/authentication involved.

Fix: normalize only max_rows, max_cell and timeout, keeping a constant number of scans (prefer a shared declaration of those names), or gather raw spans in one bounded traversal. Unknown arguments remain refused and need no normalization. Async yields alone do not remove quadratic work. Acceptance: retain exact-value, escaped/duplicate-name and R40 cases; add a scaling/work-count regression with thousands of unknown float members that fails the current loop, plus process cancellation of a pending call behind that input with an integer control and no stdout backpressure; recheck non-live tests and size. Originally required before acceptance; **closed by external verification of `3472910..ab6eed7` on October 18, 2026** (R42 fix verification below).

**R42 fixed in `e60a1de` (owner, 2026-10-18),** independently verified and accepted below. `protocol::parse` rereads only `tools::NUMERIC_ARGS` (`max_rows`, `max_cell`, `timeout`), the one declaration of those names, which a unit test (`the_numeric_arguments_are_the_schemas_integers`) pins to every tool schema's `integer` properties, each with one scan of the line and only when `serde_json` read it as a float; any other argument, float-valued or not, is refused by name in `tools::plan` with no value read. So the rereading costs a call at most three linear scans of its line whatever it holds; the front parse is `serde_json`'s own `Value` parse, bounded by the 4 MB message limit but not linear in the message (its object maps cost more per member as members grow; the verification's answers, below). Against the acceptance: R40's and R41's value, duplicate-name and escaped-path tests kept and passing; a work regression, `a_calls_line_is_scanned_at_most_once_per_numeric_argument`, counts scans with a test-only per-thread counter in `raw_member` (4,000 unknown float-valued arguments: 0 scans, 1 with `max_rows: 30.0`, 3 with all three as floats, 0 for integers; the loop of `a55c70d` put back for the check scans 4,000 times and the test takes 49.9 s); a real-process test, `a_call_with_many_float_arguments_doesnt_hold_up_a_cancel`, sends a pending connect's cancel behind a call holding 4,000 unknown arguments, with stdout read throughout, and the connect is closed within 2 s whether their values are `1` (the integer control) or `1.0` (with the source files of `a55c70d` restored, the `1.0` form didn't close within 5 s). Inputs under `MAX_MESSAGE`, synthetic profiles only. Size: 2,804,224 bytes, 512 fewer than `a55c70d`: MCP 316,416 bytes (309.0 KiB) over Phase 4, 41,984 under the 350 KiB gate, and the exe 341,504 under 3 MB. `check.ps1` passes (373 unit tests; 91 debug and 85 release process tests).

**Answers to the brief:** restrict the present rereading to declared numeric arguments, or replace it with bounded one-pass extraction; the current generic loop is not accepted because of R42. Accept -0/-0.0 as zero for max_rows/max_cell, consistent with their value domains and R40; timeout's minimum 1 still refuses it. Negative nonzero underflow remains invalid. No lexical minus-sign ban or new parser dependency is requested.

Independent `scripts/check.ps1` passed in 55 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied, 371 units (five ignored), 90 debug processes (51 ignored), 84 release processes (45 ignored), and ten expected in-box imports. Release 2,804,736 bytes, +2,048 from 0d4cf2c; MCP +316,928 (309.5 KiB) over Phase 4, 41,472 below the owner's 350 KiB gate and 340,992 below 3 MiB overall. Passing standard checks do not cover the reproduced R42 regression. No independent live server, CredMan or smoke-golden run; developer also did not rerun live tests for this fix.

Evidence: `artifacts/r41-review-check.log`, `artifacts/r41-review-values.json`, `artifacts/r41-review-scale.json`, `artifacts/r41-review-cancel.json`, with corresponding `scratch/r41-review-*.py` probes. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-r41-verification.md`. Prior evidence preserved. Only synthetic profiles and loopback/closed ports; no owner credentials or data. Only review/status documents changed; no production, Cargo, remote, workflow or signing changes, and no commit/push.

### R42 fix verification (October 18, 2026)

Reviewed `3472910..ab6eed7`, fix `e60a1de`. **R42 closed; this increment accepted. R40/R41 remain closed, with no new R43 finding.** Phase 5.2/5.3 acceptance stands with these follow-ups complete; next are the planned 5.4–5.6, not a claim of completed live tool coverage or client interop.

The production loop visits only tools::NUMERIC_ARGS (max_rows, max_cell, timeout); each float-form declared argument costs one raw lookup, integer forms none. Unknown floats cost none and still receive the existing schema refusal. The shared-declaration/schema test and per-thread operation counter pass: 4,000 unknown floats give 0 argument lookups, one declared float gives 1, all three give 3, and integer forms 0. The counter measures raw_member invocations, not scanned bytes; the fixed-depth scanner and exact arithmetic are unchanged. Id lookups are separate: a float-form tools/call id can add a fourth lookup overall.

Independent release replay of the original probes: 4,000 unknown floats (76,220 bytes) complete in 0.031 s, versus exceeding 12 s before the fix; integer controls remain fast. A pending driver prelogin canceled behind 2,000 floats closes immediately at timer resolution, versus 4.765 s before; integer control 0.016 s, EOF sent only after closure. The full suite's new 4,000-member cancellation process test passes in debug and release. All 21 independent value/boundary/escaped-duplicate cases pass (16 usage refusals before connecting, five admitted closed-port controls). Retained R38/R40/R41 regressions pass.

Two additional near-cap release probes include all three declared float limits plus a float-form id: a 4,194,223-byte long-string message permits cancellation in 0.234 s; a 3,980,269-byte message with 199,000 unknown members permits it in 0.875 s. Timing starts before writing the payload and includes pipe delivery/scheduling. Both return the expected unknown-argument refusal and following ping; no stdout backpressure and EOF only after socket closure. These are observations on this workstation, not worst-case, memory measurements or portable subsecond guarantees.

**Answers to the brief:** keep the two-second process assertion; the five-second socket observation ceiling only bounds a failing test, and the operation count provides the stable work invariant. No evidence supports loosening the threshold now; investigate repeated CI timing failures with the elapsed/control diagnostics before changing it. Accept parsing on the serving thread for this stage. Added rereads are a constant number of linear traversals; do not claim formal O(bytes) for the entire Value parse, whose locked serde_json 1.0.151/default/std implementation uses BTreeMap object insertion and comparison costs. No offload is required now. Revisit on measured client/slow-machine latency; moving parsing to stdin would free engine/console polling but still hold a cancellation line behind an earlier parse, and any worker design must preserve bounded queues, memory, exact numeric text and ordering.

Independent `scripts/check.ps1` passed in 56 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied, 373 units (five ignored), 91 debug processes (51 ignored), 85 release processes (45 ignored), ten expected in-box DLL imports. Release exe 2,804,224 bytes, 512 fewer than 3472910; MCP +316,416 bytes (309.0 KiB) over Phase 4, 41,984 below the owner's 350 KiB gate and 341,504 below 3 MiB overall. No Cargo/dependency changes. No independent live server, CredMan or smoke-golden run; developer likewise did not rerun live tests for this parser-only fix.

Evidence: `artifacts/r42-review-check.log`, `artifacts/r42-review-scale.json`, `artifacts/r42-review-cancel.json`, `artifacts/r42-review-values.json`, `artifacts/r42-review-large.json`, their run logs and corresponding `scratch/r42-review-*.py` probes. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-r42-verification.md`. Previous evidence preserved. Synthetic profiles and loopback only; no owner credentials or database. Only review/status documents changed; no production, Cargo, remote, workflow or signing changes, and no commit/push.

### Phase 5.4, built 2026-10-18 (live tool tests)

The live tool tests of mcp-mode.md §9's list: six tests in `tests/cli.rs`, `live::mcp_tools`, committed in `a33d2ea`, after `fbbe1a8` (below) and before `814ee12`, the fixes from a review of them. Tests only: no product code, Cargo file or behavior changed, so the exe is as at `e60a1de` (2,804,224 bytes, MCP 309.0 KiB). mcp-mode.md §9 (Live tool tests) has each test, what it asserts and the reset table; §5 (Checked live in 5.4; After a cancel; Putting a connection back) what they show of the reset, the setup, a kept cancel and the idle session between calls.

- **How they run:** `sqlh mcp --profile <the test profile> --pool 1 --verbose -t 30` with the machine's real profiles (the profiles test with a scratch file), driven a request at a time (`Mcp`), the calls compared on one connection: one `@@SPID`, a new login first, `connect_ms` 0 after it. `WAITFOR` is refused in every MCP call (DESIGN.md §7), so the timeout and the cancels wait in a read blocked behind another session's held write (`Blocked`), which costs the server no CPU, where a CPU-bound wait would take one of its schedulers from the live tests running alongside.
- **The tests:** the `query` tool's rows, a SQL error (error 208 at line 4, exit 1) and every kind of write refused before anything runs (exit 5, `sqlh mcp is read-only`, in text and JSON, the table untouched); a timeout (exit 4, the MCP advice exactly) and a client's cancel (no answer, `--verbose` logs it), each followed by a clean call on the same session; a call's `USE`, nine `SET`s (its level among them) and a #temp table gone from the next call, values and effects; the review's question 3 (below); decision 17's clean next call after a kept cancel (below); two listed profiles each reached by `profile`, and a missing, an unlisted and an unknown one refused (exit 3).
- **The external review's question 3, answered:** after `SET ROWCOUNT 2`, `TEXTSIZE 10`, `LANGUAGE French`, `DATEFORMAT ymd`, `DATEFIRST 3`, `CONTEXT_INFO` and an open `GLOBAL` cursor, each in force in the call that set it (2 rows of 5, a value cut to 10, `janvier`, a Sunday as weekday 5, `'01/02/03'` read as 2001-02-03, the cursor open), the next call on the same SPID gets the fresh login's values and effects whole (5 rows, 50 characters, `January`, weekday 1, 2003-01-02, `CONTEXT_INFO` NULL, no cursor: `FETCH` gets error 16916). A probe by hand found `STATISTICS IO`, `TIME` and `XML`, `FORCEPLAN`, `CURSOR_CLOSE_ON_COMMIT`, `ANSI_NULL_DFLT_OFF` and `QUERY_GOVERNOR_COST_LIMIT` restored too. Of the states tested only the isolation level survives the reset, and the setup already sets it; the setup's other `SET`s can't be told from the reset by these tests and stay on every call. **No product change:** `run::session_setup` is unchanged, as the review asked (no speculative reset statements). App locks (`sp_getapplock`) and session context (`sp_set_session_context`) are refused by the guard's `EXEC` allowlist (exit 5, asserted), and `WAITFOR` by the guard: for the session layer if either changes.
- **Decision 17's requirement** (a kept cancel leaves the next call clean), met live: the idle session holds no open transaction (decision 17's `@@TRANCOUNT` 0; inside the next call it is 1, that call's own wrapper) and the cancelled call's #temp table is gone with `put_back`'s rollback, but the session keeps the call's `SET`s, language and level until the next call's reset and setup, which give the next call a fresh call's `@@OPTIONS`, language and READ COMMITTED.
- **Checked to fail:** with the reset on reuse removed, tests 3, 4 and 5 fail, on the reset's items; with the setup's READ COMMITTED removed, 3 and 5. The source was restored after each.
- **Found while building:**
  - **`fbbe1a8`:** every `Reader` login of a test process was named `sqlh_reader_<pid>`, so two Reader tests running at once (libtest runs tests in parallel) could refuse each other's `CREATE LOGIN` or drop the login under each other; it is now `sqlh_reader_<pid>_<n>`. A possible cause of the unexplained live failure at `9b5318d` (Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, Still open), not confirmed.
  - **A call that turns on `SET FMTONLY`, `PARSEONLY` or `SHOWPLAN_XML`** (the guard allows each) leaves its wrapper's rollback, and `put_back`'s, unrun, so its connection is dropped, not pooled, and the next call logs in again (148–185 ms, by hand). Not a leak (the server rolls a closed session's transaction back); kept as built and documented (mcp-mode.md §5).
- **The review of the tests** (three P3 findings, each confirmed by a verifier; fixed in `814ee12`, `tests/cli.rs` only, no assertion changed):
  1. Tests 3 and 5 credited the reset with what other steps restore: with the reset removed, the #temp table and `NULL = NULL` still pass (the #temp table is made inside the wrapper and dropped by its rollback, `put_back`'s after a cancel; `ANSI_NULLS ON` is in the setup). The comments now credit each to its step, and so do the docs (mcp-mode.md §5, §9).
  2. The builder's report credited the reset with `NUMERIC_ROUNDABORT` and proposed "only the isolation level needs the setup's statement"; the setup sends `NUMERIC_ROUNDABORT OFF`, `ARITHABORT`, `ANSI_NULLS` and the rest on every call, and with the reset removed they still come back as a fresh call's, so the tests can't tell. Nothing in the tests was wrong; the docs list as the reset's only states the setup doesn't set, and say that of the states tested only the isolation level survives the reset, the setup's other `SET`s staying on every call (mcp-mode.md §5).
  3. The profiles test wrote its scratch profiles file at a fixed path under `target/tmp`, which the debug and release test binaries share, while the shared scratch lock lets their live tests run at once: one process could delete or rewrite the other's file before its `sqlh mcp` read it at launch (reproduced in 2 rounds of 3, and in 1 of 3 by the verifier). `OwnScratch` makes a directory per test and process (`{test}_{pid}`), removed with its files when dropped; it also replaces the fixed directory of `a_blocked_read_fails_after_the_lock_timeout_unless_it_reads_uncommitted`, which had the same race. Six rounds of the debug and release copies run at once passed, with nothing left behind.
- **Verification** (the builder's, at `a33d2ea` and again at `814ee12`): `scripts/check.ps1`'s steps 1–9 pass: fmt, clippy with both feature sets, 373 library unit tests, 91 debug and 85 release process tests, the release build at 2,804,224 bytes of the 3,145,728 budget, ten in-box imports. The live tests are 57 in the debug build (6 debug-only) and 51 in the release build; the six new ones pass in both, the 51 release live tests pass run directly, and the 57 debug ones with `--test-threads 4`. **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` is not green:** its debug live step failed in every full run, each time on one or two timing tests that predate 5.4, fail with `fbbe1a8`'s test file too (all four full runs without the 5.4 tests failed) and pass alone: `the_chunk_loop_keeps_the_deadline_when_the_work_is_sqlhs_own` (6.6–6.8 s against its 6 s bound, in every full run), and now and then `a_fast_narrow_result_is_bounded_by_t_in_every_mode`, `many_small_results_are_bounded_by_t`, `the_read_uncommitted_note_counts_against_the_output_cap` and `a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4`. In those failures, runs under `-t 1` ended about 5 s late (about 6.5 s in all), and the 250 ms count budget counted fewer rows than the tests expect. The likely cause, not confirmed, is the test server's load in a fully parallel run (a small server, and about 150 ms of its CPU for each SQL login, docs/notes/connect-latency.md). The script stops at the first failure, so its release live step didn't run; the release live tests were run directly. `spike_reset` wasn't rerun (it calls `run::session_setup` directly, unchanged). No timing test was changed.
- **Still open, for the owner:**
  - **The timing tests in a full parallel debug live run** (above): the release check's live gate (§7, "live tests are green") fails today on tests older than 5.4. The R42 verification asked that repeated timing failures be investigated with their elapsed times and controls before a bound is loosened; the names and the figures are recorded above. Not decided.
    - **Investigated for `the_chunk_loop_keeps_the_deadline_when_the_work_is_sqlhs_own`** (2026-10-18, at `32c82d5`, with nothing else running on the client). It failed again in the full debug live run, 56 of 57 passing: its `--allow-write` run took 6.58 s by the test's clock against its 6 s bound. sqlh's own status line gave 6,563 ms, of it a 454 ms connect.
      - **Alone,** the same command (`SQLH_DEBUG_ROW_WORK_US=5000`, `--max-cell 10`, with and without `--allow-write`, `-t 1`) ended with exit 4 in 2.0 to 2.1 s, four times in each mode, the connect 160 to 230 ms.
      - **Eight copies run at once,** each building its 16 MB value on the server, reproduced it: seven ended in 6.3 to 6.5 s, each about 6.0 s after its connect (`-t` plus sqlh's own 5 s cap), the eighth in 4.0 s. Under that load the server acknowledges the cancel late; a value still being built isn't interrupted (DESIGN.md §4.4, The grace).
      - **So sqlh kept its documented bound.** The batch was over within `-t` plus 5 s. The test's bound is the problem: `timed` and `timed_with_work` measure the process from outside, so the connect and the session setup count against `-t` plus `GRACE`, and a run that needs the whole 5 s cap fails by its connect time. DESIGN.md §4.4's "the tests allow `-t` plus those 5 s" doesn't say from when. The other tests that use `timed` are exposed the same way; the two count-budget failures (fewer rows counted in 250 ms than a test expects) are a different symptom, not looked at here.
    - **Decided and fixed (owner, 2026-10-19: "measure the batch").** `timed` and `timed_with_work` assert sqlh's own time, the run's time less its connect (`sqlh_time`: the status line's time and `connect N ms`, or JSON's `elapsed_ms` and `connect_ms`), under `-t` plus `GRACE` plus `SETUP` (0.5 s, the session setup and the close), and the whole process under that plus `OUTSIDE` (5 s), checked then only once the run had ended (`within_t`), so a run that hung was never caught (the completion review's R43, below); since `901908f` that bound is enforced while the run goes on (`timed_run` on `run_bounded`). The `timed` cases that ask the budget to end a run "long before `-t`" (under 4 s) now take sqlh's own time too; `a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4`'s like case, and its 6 s bounds, still take the process's (AGENTS.md). DESIGN.md §4.4 says from when the tests count. No product change and no bound loosened: the 1 s plus 5 s is the same, now on the clock DESIGN.md's promise is about.
      - **Checked to fail:** with the deadline ticks taken out of `count_rows`, `the_row_loop_keeps_the_deadline_when_the_work_is_sqlhs_own` fails, sqlh's own time 8.05 s against 6.5 s (the source restored).
      - **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passes,** in 262 s: 57 of 57 debug and 51 of 51 release live tests, with steps 1–9 as before. One run; the count-budget tests (`the_read_uncommitted_note_counts_against_the_output_cap`, `a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4`) failed only now and then before and passed in it, so they stay open: when one fails, record its name and figures.
  - **`WAITFOR`, app locks and session context** stay out of reach of every MCP call; the session layer is to check them if the guard or its `EXEC` allowlist changes (Phase 6).
  - **The connection drop after `FMTONLY`, `PARSEONLY` or `SHOWPLAN_XML`:** kept as built (a login's cost, no leak).
  - **What isn't covered live yet** (mcp-mode.md §9, Not covered): the other tools through `sqlh mcp` (`check` and the schema tools), and the probe, the retry and a dead connection after a cancel, which only the fake TDS server plays.
- **Docs:** mcp-mode.md's status, §1, §4 (R42's wording, below), §5, §7, §9 and §10; this plan; DESIGN.md §10 and §12 (their status and the live tests; no behavior changed); AGENTS.md's Status and live-test notes. The README is unchanged: no agent-facing behavior changed, and its "Every call starts clean" entry is what 5.4 checks. **R42's wording,** corrected in the same pass as the R42 verification asked: the rereading of a call's numeric arguments is at most three linear scans of its line, and the front parse is `serde_json`'s own `Value` parse, bounded by the 4 MB message limit but not linear in the message (its object maps cost more per member as members grow; mcp-mode.md §4, Parser work; R42 fixed, above). The code comment in `src/mcp/protocol.rs` (`a call costs at most three scans`) is left for the next code change.

### CI failure after the push of `ef321ab` (October 18, 2026)

CI run 39171456990, for the push of `ef321ab`, failed at `check.ps1` step 7, the release process tests. `mcp::a_gone_client_ends_the_server_with_stdin_still_open` exited 0 as it should, but its `--verbose` stderr held only `sqlh mcp: serving win`, with no `sqlh mcp: ending (stdout closed)`. The same test passed in that run's debug process tests, and 200 times out of 200 here.

**The cause was a race in the product, not in the test.** `--verbose` lines go to a stderr thread through a bounded channel (`try_send`, so logging never holds the server up). Nothing at the end waited for that thread, but for a 50 ms sleep after the `never written` line. With stdout closed at once, the end has nothing to wait for, so the process could exit, and take the thread with it, before the line was written. The slower runner lost that race.

**Reproduced here under load.** The test was run alone, with 16 busy loops on this machine's 12 logical processors. At `ef321ab` it failed 11 times in 400, every time on that stderr assertion (`tests/cli.rs:3745`); with the fix it passed all 400. Running copies of the test in parallel doesn't work as a load: `scratch()` keys its directory by the test's name, so the copies replace each other's profiles file and exit 3.

**The fix** (`32c82d5`): the end's last step, `flush_log`, hands the thread a flush: an empty line, which no log line is (`Server::log` drops one). The thread answers it through a `Notify` once every line handed over before it is written. The end waits up to `delivery::SIDE_WAIT` (0.5 s) for the answer, the CLI's bound for its own `--verbose` lines (DESIGN.md §4.3). A stderr behind by more than the channel's 256 lines has its lines dropped, as before, and isn't waited for. The 50 ms sleep is gone.

This last wait takes no console events, the one wait in the end that doesn't: by then nothing is left to cut, and every event's grace is longer. A console event's end bound grows by that 0.5 s at most, and only with `--verbose` and a stderr that isn't taking lines: about 2.5 s after a close, logoff or shutdown, still inside Windows' 5 s, and 3.5 s after Ctrl+C or Ctrl+Break (docs/mcp-mode.md §2, The end; DESIGN.md §4.3, §10).

**Tests:**
- **Unit `the_last_log_lines_reach_stderr_within_a_bound`:**
  - a stderr that takes 5 ms a write has all five lines when `flush_log` returns, in under half of `SIDE_WAIT`, so the thread's answer ended the wait, not its bound;
  - a stalled stderr holds the flush for `SIDE_WAIT` and less than three times that, writing nothing;
  - with the thread stuck in a write and the 256-line channel full behind it, the flush isn't waited for (under a fifth of `SIDE_WAIT`), and neither is anything without `--verbose`.
- **Unit `the_end_waits_for_its_last_log_lines`:** drives `mcp::end` itself, after stdout closed and after stdin closed, with nothing in flight and a stderr taking 100 ms a write. `sqlh mcp: ending (REASON)` is written when the end returns, and nothing goes to stdout.
- **The process test** `a_gone_client_ends_the_server_with_stdin_still_open` is unchanged; a stderr pipe with room takes the lines at once.
- **Checked to fail:**
  - the flush skipped;
  - the thread's answer dropped;
  - the flush sent with a blocking `send`, which hangs the full-channel case;
  - the flush moved back to where the 50 ms sleep was, for an end with something never written, which fails the second test.
- **Not tested:** that the answer can come before the wait begins. That is `Notify::notify_one`'s stored permit, as documented by tokio, which the stdout thread's `gone` already relies on. A case with nothing queued didn't catch `notify_waiters` in its place in five runs, since the wait always began first, so it was dropped rather than kept as a claim it can't check.

**Verification** (`32c82d5`, on top of 5.4's `4418c14`):
- **`scripts/check.ps1 -Live`:** steps 1–9 pass: 375 library unit tests, 91 debug and 85 release process tests, the exe at 2,804,224 bytes, ten in-box imports. The debug live step failed only on `the_chunk_loop_keeps_the_deadline_when_the_work_is_sqlhs_own`, 56 of 57 passing: Phase 5.4's open timing failure, investigated there, which this fix doesn't touch.
- **The 51 release live tests,** run directly since the script stops at the first failure, pass.
- **The worktree check** (`pwsh scripts/check.ps1`, before the cherry-pick) passed the same, in 164 s.

**The fix's review.** An internal review workflow (three lenses: concurrency, docs, tests; a skeptic for each finding) found no defect in the code, and nothing it reported was refuted. It found:
- `app::DIAG_WAIT`, R09's bound for last-resort messages, borrowed where the CLI's `--verbose` bound is `delivery::SIDE_WAIT`; both are 0.5 s;
- statements left saying that the end takes console events in every wait, and DESIGN.md §10's bound without the 0.5 s;
- the test gaps closed above.

All are fixed here. It also noticed two stale status lines in DESIGN.md §10 (R35–R39 "awaiting" verification, and decision 20 "pending"), which the 5.4 docs pass (`4418c14`) had already corrected.

**Size: none.** The exe stays at 2,804,224 bytes. A first version gave the channel a message type of its own, an enum of a line or a flush. It made the exe 9,728 bytes larger with a oneshot to answer the flush, and 8,192 with a `Notify`: linker maps of the two builds put the growth in a second copy of std's channel code (`try_send`, `recv`, drop glue) for the new type, where the `String` channel's copy had been shared with stdout's. So the channel stays `String`.

### Phase 5.4–5.6 external completion review (October 21, 2026)

Reviewed `ef321ab..a6a4bc2`, last production change `3915347`. The supplied brief is dated October 22; this review uses the client's October 21 date. **No blocking production finding; reviewed increment accepted with two nonblocking P3 follow-ups, R43 and R44.** The six live tests are structurally sound evidence for the stated reset/cancel outcomes. Live SQL Server passes, hosted CI and client sessions remain reported evidence, not independent reruns. The timeout agent trial, count-budget flakes and first-release matrix remain open; acceptance does not mark them verified.

| Finding | Evidence, action and acceptance |
|---|---|
| **R43 [P3], tests/cli.rs:7600-7616,7773; shared run at 43-50** | OUTSIDE is checked only after live/live_with returns through blocking Command::output. It detects an eventually finishing slow process, not a hang: the assertion is never reached if collection never returns. An isolated harness copied the exact runner, launched its own 30 s sleeper and remained blocked past the 11.5 s outside bound; the reviewer killed only that helper tree after 12 s. This is an inherited runner limitation exposed by the new hang-guard claim, not a new production timeout failure. Add an active child deadline with concurrent pipe draining, kill/reap and captured diagnostics, shared by timed/timed_with_work; retain the 6.5 s own-clock assertion. Acceptance: a synthetic never-finishing child produces a bounded test failure and leaves no process behind. Until fixed, call OUTSIDE a post-completion assertion. Open, nonblocking test-infrastructure follow-up. |
| **R44 [P3], src/output/mod.rs:1276** | The new MCP suggestion COUNT(*) returns int and fails above 2,147,483,647 rows (or returns NULL under the documented arithmetic settings). For an unknown large cardinality, suggest COUNT_BIG(*) instead, which returns bigint. [SQL Server COUNT documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/sql/t-sql/functions/count-transact-sql?view=sql-server-ver17). Update exact strings, DESIGN/mcp-mode and README summaries consistently. No huge live scan is needed; assert the advice names COUNT_BIG and retains the existing MCP/CLI distinction. Outcome/guard/count algorithm are unchanged. Open, nonblocking advice follow-up. |

**R43 and R44 fixed in `901908f`** (owner, 2026-10-22), **closed by external verification of `a6a4bc2..5db639b` on October 22, 2026**; increment accepted with new nonblocking P3 R45 below.

- **R43, the watchdog** (`tests/cli.rs`):
  - **`bounded_child(child, started, limit)`, the watchdog under every wait for a run's end:**
    - **While the child runs:** it closes a piped stdin still in `child`, as `Child::wait_with_output` does, so a child reading it sees its end. It drains the piped streams on threads into shared buffers, so a child that fills one can't stall, and polls the child.
    - **At the limit:** it kills the whole process tree (`taskkill /F /T`, falling back to `Child::kill`), because the shell tests run sqlh under cmd.exe or PowerShell and a sqlh left running would hold the pipes. Then it waits for the child, and gives the readers 10 s, so a pipe another process holds can't hold the watchdog up. What they read by then is kept, with a mark that the rest was left.
    - **It returns** the run (with what the child wrote before a kill), the time, whether it was killed, and the PID.
  - **Its two entry points:**
    - `run_bounded(command, limit)`, for a test's own limit.
    - `finish(child)`, at `HANG` (300 s): it fails the test with the captured output if the child had to be killed. `run`, `sqlh_args`, `run_closed`, `run_with_stdin`, `live`, `live_with`, `live::collect` and the concurrent-`-o` test's run A all go through it now, so those runs' ends are hang-bounded, not only the timing tests'.
    - The fixtures' `Live::write` uses `bounded_child` directly and reports a hung run as a failed run (code −1) rather than panicking, since cleanups call it while a failed test unwinds.
    - `run_with_stdin` and `Live::write` write their stdin on a thread (`feed`), so the watchdog covers the write too. Before, a child that stopped reading held a write over the pipe's 4 KiB (13 KB to over 1 MB in the MCP tests) for good, before any watchdog started.
    - Spawned runs already used `wait_within` or the `Mcp` driver, which kill at their own limits. The one wait for a run's end left unguarded is `run_measured`, which must keep the child's handle to read its peak memory.
  - **Waits for a run's progress, not its end, are not bounded:**
    - a fake server's `accept`, in several connect tests and in the concurrent `-o` test before run A reaches `finish`;
    - the Ctrl+Break tests' loops reading for a `--verbose` line;
    - the MCP slow-reader tests' reads to EOF before their `wait_within`.

    For those and `run_measured`, CI's 60-minute cap is the bound (AGENTS.md names them).
  - **`timed_run`** is shared by `timed` and `timed_with_work`. It runs each `-t 1` run under `run_bounded` at `1 s + GRACE + SETUP + OUTSIDE` (11.5 s), and fails with the captured output if the run had to be killed. The 6.5 s own-clock assertion (`sqlh_time`) is kept. Other live timing tests (`a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4`, `a_timeout_that_is_not_in_hidden_rows_is_still_exit_4`, `a_large_result_is_cut_within_about_a_second`, the lock-timeout and deadlock tests) still check the process's time once it has ended; a hang in them is now ended by `finish` at 300 s.
  - **The test,** `a_bounded_run_kills_a_child_that_never_ends` (not live, so CI runs it), 2.4 s:
    - **A child that won't end:** sqlh reading its script from a stdin that stays open waits, before it connects anything. Under a 1 s limit it is killed, the run comes back between 1 and 6 s, and `tasklist` no longer lists its PID.
    - **A process tree:** the same sqlh under `cmd.exe /c`, isolated as `sqlh()` is (`isolated`: no profiles, no `SQLH_PROFILE` or `SQLCMD*`), is killed with its tree, and its pipes close at once (no reader fallback).
    - **A child that ends on its own** (`--version`) isn't killed, and its output is whole.
    - **A backstop:** the stdin's writer is dropped after 20 s, so a watchdog that failed to kill would make this test fail then, not hang the suite.
  - **Checked to fail:**
    - With the kill taken out, the test fails at 20 s, as the backstop ends the child: an unkilled child, and its time.
    - With `taskkill` not given `/T` (the child killed, not its tree), it fails at the `cmd.exe` case: sqlh still held the pipes, and the reader fell back.
    - A kill without the wait isn't caught. On Windows the killed process drops out of `tasklist` at once, and there are no zombie processes, so the wait is good hygiene, not observable. The internal review that asked for these cases also refuted that the `tasklist` check is pointless: it fails a watchdog that reports a kill without making one.
  - **CI's job is capped at 60 minutes** (`timeout-minutes`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`), for anything the watchdog doesn't cover; a run takes about 15.
  - **Docs:**
    - DESIGN.md §4.4: the `-t 1` timing tests' process bound is enforced while the run goes on, and the other runs are under `finish`.
    - AGENTS.md: which tests bound which clock, every runner and its watchdog, the one exception, and that a new test never calls a bare `Command::output` or `wait_with_output`.
    - The 2026-10-19 decision record above, amended in place.
  - **Two internal review rounds** (a review workflow with a skeptic per finding) shaped this fix.
    - **The first** found that the docs claimed every deadline test was bounded while `run` and `live` still waited for good, and that the synthetic test would hang CI if the watchdog broke. That led to `finish`, the backstop and the CI cap.
    - **The second** found the unbounded stdin writes (`feed`), the stdin left open (now closed), the `cmd.exe` case's isolation, the readers' lost output, and a claim still too broad (now limited to waits for a run's end, the progress waits named).
    - Every finding of both was fixed but one: in the first round, a claim that the `tasklist` check was pointless was refuted.
- **R44, `COUNT_BIG`** (`src/output/mod.rs`, `stopped_note`): under MCP the advice is now `a COUNT_BIG(*) query counts every row, as does the profile's exact_count = true`. The comment gives the reason: the count was stopped because the result is large, and `COUNT` returns `int`, which fails past 2,147,483,647 rows (error 8115, or NULL with `ARITHABORT` and `ANSI_WARNINGS` off).
  - **Tests:** the exact strings in `the_notes_advice_follows_what_stopped_the_count` and `in_mcp_a_stopped_count_offers_what_the_call_can_change` are updated, and a new assertion checks that no front end's advice, for any kind of stop, names `COUNT(` itself. The CLI's `--exact-count` advice is unchanged.
  - **Docs:** DESIGN.md §5.1, mcp-mode.md §4, the README, PLAN.md 5.6, AGENTS.md and the trial 3 note.
- **The trial note's sentence** the review questioned ("every figure the tools returned was carried over correctly") now says the dropped ranking row is an error of fidelity, and that its counts are one reading's, not a score.
- **Verification:** `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passes at `901908f` in 193 s: 376 library unit tests, 92 debug and 86 release process tests (one more each, the watchdog's test), 57 debug and 51 release live tests, each run's end now under the watchdog; the exe is unchanged at 2,805,248 bytes, ten in-box imports. The debug process suite takes 19 s, as before: the watchdog costs no measurable time.

**Four judgments:**

1. Accept the once-per-run cut-value note and current section proxy for generated SQL. Shown-row admission precedes cell-cut accounting, so hidden/withheld rows do not inflate it. Twelve independent fake-TDS/real-exe cases (query/schema, text/JSON/CSV, 8 KiB and exhausted 4 KiB budgets) keep JSON valid, CSV metadata on stderr, combined bytes within the cap and note counts equal to displayed cut markers. The generic SUBSTRING direction is acceptable; xml/CLR may need conversion, and byte/Unicode indexing is expression-specific. No type tracking is requested; a short generic cast qualifier is a possible later refinement. Existing free-text JSON notes require no contract-version bump.
2. Accept the private empty-string flush marker and the last uninterruptible 500 ms wait within the documented approximate 2.5 s close/logoff/shutdown and 3.5 s Ctrl+C/Break bounds. Server::log excludes empty lines; FIFO and the one-flush notify_one permit are sound; full/broken stderr remains best effort. Full tests pass paced/stalled/full/disabled/end cases. All 100 independent EOF/stdout-closed release probes retain the last line. A copied-function harness forces a 50 ms pre-wait gap: notify_one returns in 50 ms, notify_waiters mutant times out around 560 ms. This demonstrates the stored-permit mechanism in an instrumented copy, not a claimed production scheduling trace.
3. Accept run time minus connect as the integration metric for these one-batch -t tests, with SETUP 500 ms and OUTSIDE 5 s retained. It includes setup/close, not just batch execution, and the emitted clock precedes final output delivery. The reported eight-second row-loop mutation failure is useful, not independently repeated. R43 is needed for an actual outside watchdog. Do not loosen bounds or call the recorded count-budget flakes resolved without their diagnostics.
4. Accept the 5.4 evidence design: each setter demonstrates changed state/effects, then the same SPID/connect_ms 0 next call matches a fresh call. The kept-cancel observer sees idle/no transaction before reuse; next-call wrapper count 1, options, language and isolation distinguish put_back rollback from reset/setup. ROWCOUNT/TEXTSIZE/LANGUAGE/DATEFORMAT/DATEFIRST/CONTEXT_INFO/global cursor include values and effects. Temp-table removal and NULL comparison are outcomes, not reset-specific proof. Reported reset/setup mutation failures were reviewed, not rerun. No session_setup expansion is justified by the supplied passes. OwnScratch and Reader suffix fixes address actual namespace races, without proving the older unexplained failure's cause.

Interop notes record OpenCode legacy, Inspector modern/legacy/auto plus strict schemas, and Claude Code modern plus tools; these sessions and owner-state cleanup fingerprints were not independently reproduced or read from private traffic. Trial 3 supports retaining decisions 14/15 for this model/harness; it does not establish universal client preference or successful timeout recovery. Preserve the explicit ranking/claim errors when assessing answer fidelity; the broad prose that every returned figure carried over correctly is not an accuracy score.

Independent `scripts/check.ps1` passed in 55 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied; 376 units (five ignored), 91 debug processes (57 ignored), 85 release processes (51 ignored), ten expected in-box DLL imports. Release 2,805,248 bytes, +1,024 from R42; MCP +317,440 bytes (310.0 KiB) over Phase 4, 40,960 below the owner's 350 KiB gate and 340,480 below 3 MiB. No Cargo dependency changes. Developer reports 57 debug/51 release live passes at 3915347; these were not independently rerun, nor were CredMan, smoke goldens or actual Windows close/logoff/shutdown events. git ls-files --cached --ignored --exclude-standard is empty.

Evidence: `artifacts/phase5-completion-review-check.log`, `artifacts/phase5-completion-output-probes.json`, `artifacts/phase5-completion-log-probes.json`, `artifacts/phase5-completion-probes-run.log`, `artifacts/phase5-completion-permit.json`, `artifacts/phase5-completion-watchdog.json`, and corresponding `scratch/phase5-completion-*.py`/copied Rust harnesses. Full ignored review: `archive/reviews/code-review-phase5-completion.md`. Previous evidence preserved. Only synthetic profiles/loopback were used; no owner credentials, database or client state. Only review/status docs changed; no production, Cargo, remote, hosted workflow, signing, commit or push actions.

### R43/R44 fix verification (October 22, 2026)

Reviewed `a6a4bc2..5db639b`, fix `901908f`. **R43 and R44 closed; increment accepted with one new nonblocking P3 finding, R45.** No blocking production finding. R43 now actively watches the child; R44 names COUNT_BIG(*), while CLI advice is unchanged. The live/client validation limits and prior count-budget/timeout-trial follow-ups remain open.

Both timed helpers call timed_run, which enforces the 11.5 s process deadline through run_bounded before checking the 6.5 s own-clock bound. The ordinary runners share finish/HANG 300 s; output drains concurrently, piped stdin still owned by Child is closed, and feed moves large stdin writes to another thread. run_closed retains its deliberately broken stream configuration. The new direct/tree/natural-exit process test passes in debug and release. Supplied mutation results were inspected, not independently rerun.

Independent probes copied the exact watchdog/feed functions into a synthetic harness at a 350 ms deadline: natural exit 19 ms; child-owned stdin EOF 18 ms; hanging child after 256 KiB on each stream killed in 451 ms with all bytes retained; plain hang 448 ms; parent/live-descendant tree 450 ms with both known PIDs absent and no reader fallback; blocked 2 MiB stdin write 449 ms, feed unblocked with broken pipe. A separate probe makes taskkill unavailable and verifies direct-child fallback in 363 ms; no fallback tree guarantee is claimed. No server or credentials were used.

**R45 [P3], tests/cli.rs:5650 calling bounded_child (status/wait expects at 144,157; thread startup at 125): preserve the fixture cleanup no-panic contract.** Live::write previously converted process wait/write errors into failed Run; it now handles ordinary watchdog expiry but can panic inside the shared helper on try_wait/wait errors or recoverable thread-start failures. Cleanup during an existing unwind can therefore abort the process instead of reporting failure and attempting later idempotent steps. An isolated copied watchdog with just the status-query result forced to an I/O error panics at child status, caught by catch_unwind; this is fault injection, not a reproduced natural Windows/live cleanup error. Make the watchdog core fallible for OS/startup errors, allow normal test wrappers to assert, and have Live::write convert errors after best-effort kill/reap/drain. Acceptance: injected polling/wait/startup failures become a failed cleanup without unwinding and later cleanup steps execute; keep normal timeout/tree/drain/feed tests. Initially open, nonblocking test-infrastructure follow-up; closed by external verification of `5db639b..135d60c` on October 22, 2026 (R45 fix verification, below).

**R45 fixed in `0d3a5f2`** (owner, 2026-10-22), **closed by external verification of `5db639b..135d60c` on October 22, 2026**; increment accepted with no new finding. Narrow on purpose: only the cleanup path changes, and every other runner behaves as before.
- **`try_bounded_child(child, started, limit, faults)`** is the watchdog core, now fallible. Where polling the child, waiting for the killed child or starting a reader thread (`std::thread::Builder::spawn`) fails, it kills and waits for the child (best effort), and returns `WatchFailed`: the OS error, and the run so far (exit code −1, with what the child wrote). The buffers survive a poisoned lock, and an exit code is read without `expect`.
- **`bounded_child`** is that core with the old panic (now naming the error and the output), so `run`, `finish`, `run_bounded` and every test that uses them are unchanged.
- **`write_never_panicking(command, sql, limit, faults)`** is `Live::write`'s body, moved out so a non-live test can drive it. Every failure comes back as a failed run (code −1) with its error and what the run wrote: a spawn error, a writer thread that couldn't start (also through `Builder::spawn`), a stdin write error, a run killed at its limit, and a `WatchFailed`. `Live::write` calls it with `HANG` and no faults.
- **`Faults`** names the operations a test makes fail on purpose: `status`, `wait`, `thread` (a reader) and `feed` (the writer). Outside the test it is `Faults::NONE`.
- **The test,** `a_cleanup_write_reports_the_watchdogs_failures_instead_of_panicking` (not live), 1.4 s: sqlh reading its SQL from stdin, against a dead address. Each injected fault, and a run killed at its limit, comes back as a failed run naming its error. The write after them, standing for a cleanup's later steps, runs to sqlh's own end (exit 2). **Checked to fail:** with a status error panicking again, as before the fix, the test fails at it.
- **Verification** at `0d3a5f2`: `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passed every step but the last: 376 library unit tests, 93 debug and 87 release process tests (one more each, the R45 test), 57 debug live tests, the exe unchanged at 2,805,248 bytes. Its release live step failed on `a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4`, one of the two known count-budget flakes (AGENTS.md): in its JSON case the 250 ms budget counted 9,000 rows, which the test expects to floor to 10,000 or 20,000 (batch 388 ms, run 942 ms, connect 322 ms, exit 0, `counting_stopped` true), the server sending fewer rows than assumed under the full parallel run. R45 doesn't touch that path; the release live tests run again passed 51 of 51. No bound was loosened.

**Three judgments:**

1. Accept the widened shared watchdog and conservative HANG 300 s without loosening tighter per-test bounds. It bounds child lifetime, not all cleanup: taskkill/reap add time and each pipe can consume READERS 10 s, up to 20 s total. Utility/OS waits have no independent timer. R45 qualifies the cleanup caller's recoverable-error behavior; do not claim an absolute 300/11.5 s total-return guarantee under OS/backend failures.
2. Accept the named existing progress/run_measured gaps as documented debt for this increment. The 60-minute CI job cap bounds hosted CI, not local cargo test or check.ps1 -Live. Keeping a process handle for memory measurement is a current helper ownership issue, not a fundamental obstacle to a future bounded borrowed-child/post-exit callback. Add direct limits when an actual progress/memory hang occurs; do not hide it by widening ordinary deadlines.
3. Accept root-PID safety while Child retains its process handle through taskkill/wait, even if it exits after try_wait. [Windows process-ID lifetime](https://devblogs.microsoft.com/oldnewthing/20110107-00/?p=11803%2F). /T requests descendants; Child::kill fallback handles only the direct child, not the whole tree. [taskkill](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/taskkill), [process termination](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/terminating-a-process). Keep that best-effort limitation explicit. A later PID-only tasklist assertion after Child is dropped neither proves wait nor retains the same identity guarantee. Stronger Job Object containment is not required for this test-harness finding.

R44's full-string tests and no-COUNT( advice loop pass for the appropriate front ends/stop reasons; current DESIGN, MCP spec, README and trial note reflect COUNT_BIG. No huge live scan is needed. Independent scripts/check.ps1 passed in 69 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied; 376 units (five ignored), 92 debug processes (57 ignored), 86 release processes (51 ignored); unchanged 2,805,248-byte exe with ten expected in-box imports. MCP remains +317,440 bytes (310.0 KiB) over Phase 4, 40,960 below the owner's 350 KiB gate and 340,480 below 3 MiB overall. No Cargo/dependency changes. No independent live SQL Server, CredMan or smoke-golden run; developer reports 57 debug/51 release live passes at 901908f. Workflow timeout was source-reviewed, not waited out on hosted CI.

Evidence: `artifacts/r43-r44-review-check.log`, `artifacts/r43-r44-watchdog-probe.json`, `artifacts/r43-r44-fallback-probe.json`, `artifacts/r43-r44-cleanup-error-probe.json`, and corresponding `scratch/r43-r44-*.py`/copied Rust harnesses. Full ignored report: `archive/reviews/code-review-r43-r44-verification.md`. Previous evidence preserved. No owner credentials/database/client state. Only review/status docs changed; no production, Cargo, remote, hosted workflow, signing, commit or push action.

### R45 fix verification (October 22, 2026)

Reviewed `5db639b..135d60c`, fix `0d3a5f2`, with HEAD and a clean starting tree verified locally. **R45 closed; increment accepted, no new finding and no R46.** Only tests/cli.rs changes behavior. No independent remote, hosted CI, live SQL Server, Credential Manager, smoke-golden or client rerun.

The fallible watchdog core returns WatchFailed for poll/wait/reader-start errors after best-effort kill/reap, with output collected by started readers. Builder::spawn makes both reader and cleanup-writer startup fallible; poisoned buffers recover; missing exit codes do not panic. Live::write uses write_never_panicking with HANG and Faults::NONE, returning failed Run for spawn/feed/write/timeout/watchdog errors. The ordinary bounded_child wrapper still asserts on infrastructure failures, which is the right scope. Explicit Faults parameters are appropriate in this integration-test target and create no production setting. The existing Cleanup::drop loop continues after failed runs. Startup before a reader exists cannot promise that stream's output; error cleanup kills the direct child, with the prior best-effort and OS-wait limitations retained.

Independent exact-helper probes passed: injected status, wait, reader-start and writer-start errors; ordinary timeout; a missing executable; broken stdin with output retained; queued stdout and stderr both retained with the status error; and an actual unwind whose Drop returned a failed cleanup then completed a successful second step. These are synthetic children/faults, not naturally occurring Windows or live-database failures. Six lifecycle probes at a 350 ms limit also passed: natural exit 20 ms, stdin EOF 19 ms, full 256 KiB per output stream retained at kill 448 ms, plain hang 443 ms, parent/descendant tree 444 ms, blocked 2 MiB feed 444 ms with broken pipe. The new repository error test passed in both full process suites. Second-reader startup failure and poisoned-buffer recovery were source-reviewed, not separately injected. No regression mutation was independently rerun.

**The count-budget throughput assumption, corrected in `d3ed9fc`** (owner, 2026-10-22), as the judgment below asks.
- **`is_stopped_bound(bound, shown, most)`** (`tests/cli.rs`) states the rule `Tally::rows_total` writes, and nothing about throughput: more than the rows shown (a hidden row was counted); no more than the result has; and one significant digit, or the rows shown plus one where that is more.
- **`a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4`:**
  - its three `[10_000, 20_000]` checks became `is_stopped_bound(n, 5, 20_000)`;
  - the two text cases also check the footer's `-- 5 of ≥N`, and the JSON case its five rows;
  - exit codes, advice, `row_count_exact`, `truncated` and `counting_stopped` are checked as before.
- **The same rule replaced two looser checks.**
  - `a_large_result_is_cut_within_about_a_second` took `100..=5,000,000` with one significant digit, in text and JSON. That accepted an impossible 100, and refused a valid 101: the same throughput assumption in a milder form.
  - `a_fast_narrow_result_is_bounded_by_t_in_every_mode`'s `--exact-count` case took any bound of at least 101.
- **Tests:**
  - The non-live `a_stopped_bound_is_checked_for_its_shape_not_a_throughput` checks the rule. It accepts 6, 9, 10, 90, 100, 9,000, 10,000 and 20,000 for 5 shown of at most 20,000, the failing run's 9,000 among them. It refuses 0, 5, 15, 9,500, 12,000, 20,001 and 30,000, and takes 101 but not 102 for 100 shown.
  - **Checked to fail:** with the product's stopped bound collapsed to the rows shown, `a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4` fails at its first case.
  - No timing bound, budget or allowed list was loosened. The flooring and clamp keep their deterministic unit tests in `src/output`.
- **Still open:** `the_read_uncommitted_note_counts_against_the_output_cap`, the other recorded flake, which needs its own figures and diagnosis.
- **Verification** at `d3ed9fc`: `scripts/check.ps1 -Live` passes in 272 s: 376 library unit tests, 94 debug and 88 release process tests (one more each, the shape test), 57 debug and 51 release live tests, the exe unchanged at 2,805,248 bytes.

**Count-budget judgment: correct the existing test's throughput assumption now.** a_timeout_during_the_count_reports_a_bound_not_exit_4 accepts only 10,000 or 20,000 in three assertions, but a 250 ms counting budget promises no minimum throughput. The supplied JSON 9,000 is a published lower bound; floor_digit can produce it from raw totals 9,000 through 9,999, not necessarily exactly 9,000 counted. Server/network/client/scheduling delay cannot be distinguished from the reported figures. Preserve semantic checks (five shown rows, stopped/inexact count, exit/advice, conservative bound within the known result and truncation consistency), accepting smaller valid bounds with the significant-digit/clamp rule where hidden progress is asserted. If progress itself is required, arrange it deterministically in a fake-server test. Keep floor/clamp unit coverage. Do not add 9,000 to an arbitrary allowlist or change the product's 250 ms budget/timing bounds to conceal this assertion failure. This refines the already open count-budget follow-up; it is not a new numbered finding or an R45 regression. The other known count-budget flake still needs its own diagnostics; the timeout agent trial remains open.

Independent scripts/check.ps1 passed in 61 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing with warnings denied; 376 units (five ignored), 93 debug processes (57 ignored), 87 release processes (51 ignored). Exe unchanged at 2,805,248 bytes with ten expected in-box imports; MCP +317,440 bytes (310.0 KiB) over Phase 4, 40,960 below its 350 KiB gate, 340,480 below 3 MiB overall. Developer reports 57 debug live passes, the recorded release count-budget failure (published bound 9,000, batch 388 ms, run 942 ms, connect 322 ms, exit 0, counting stopped), then 51/51 release live passes. The first -Live run was not wholly green; these results were reviewed, not independently rerun.

### Driver upgrade: mssql-tds 0.3.0 (owner, 2026-10-27)

Microsoft released `mssql-tds` 0.3.0; sqlh pins `=0.2.0`. The owner decided to take it; it comes before classifier v2's Part 1, since it only touches `src/db/` and Part 1's live checks would otherwise be redone. Upstream's CHANGELOG is one cumulative "Unreleased" list that doesn't separate the versions (much of it was already in 0.2.0), and the repository has no version tags, so the difference is read from the two crates' source (58 files changed; new modules for DNS caching, enhanced routing, multi-subnet failover, data classification and trace context). (M)
1. **Analysis, no code change:** one pass per area (connect and login; session recovery and the engine; the data path and server messages; compile breaks, build cost, licences and logging), each difference with its evidence and its effect on sqlh, recorded in `docs/notes/driver-0.3.0.md` with the decisions it needs. Watch points named before the analysis: multi-subnet failover on by default with `multi_subnet_failover` as `Option<bool>`; a process-wide DNS cache and enhanced routing (a password must still reach only the profile's server, R20); idle connection resiliency now active (the engine's reuse, reset and probe, and the read-only wrapper's invariant); driver logging (`db::phases`, and no password in any log); empty `image` values, multi-packet values and the PLP chunk reads; where info messages land (2.12); exe size, imports and licences; each entry of `docs/notes/upstream-issues.md`.
2. **The upgrade, on a local branch:** pin `=0.3.0`, fix `src/db/` only, keep sqlh's behaviour explicit (for example `Some(false)` unless `-M`), `scripts/check.ps1 -Live`, the smoke goldens, and the re-measurements the notes record (connect latency and failures, harness kill, memory, Ctrl+Break, the MCP reuse and reset tests), with new tests for what changed.
**Step 1 done (2026-10-27, `4367b8d`):** [driver-0.3.0.md](notes/driver-0.3.0.md); decisions 40–43 (2026-10-31). **Step 2 done on the local branch `driver-0.3.0` (2026-10-31):** the pin, `Some(...)` for failover, the session-recovery guard, its tests (two fake-server, one unit, one process, two live, and Ctrl+Break under `-M`), and the re-measurements unchanged from 0.2.0 apart from `-M`'s new rows; an internal review confirmed 13 findings, all fixed (one output defect: `-M`'s timeout reported as a missed login deadline), and `check.ps1 -Live` passes on the result: 382 unit, 95 + 89 process, 60 + 54 live tests (driver-0.3.0.md §8). Step 3 reviewed `048aa89..7b28076` externally on October 31, 2026: no blocking code finding, accepted with nonblocking P3 R46 (negotiated-TLS wording; Driver 0.3.0 external review, below). At that review the branch was local and unmerged; it was subsequently merged as `1dd341f`. R46 is now closed (R46 fix verification, below); the reviewer did not merge or push it. Whether on-prem SQL Server acknowledges DNS caching: the IP cache-membership probe was inconclusive. The external review below identified a way to observe the verdict on the original profile, with no R20 exception. **Measured with the owner's approval, 2026-10-31:** the test server's verdict is `allowed=false` in three runs, so the cache never holds it (driver-0.3.0.md §8, DNS acknowledgement).
3. **Record and review:** decisions in §6, DESIGN.md and the notes where behaviour changed, then a brief for the external reviewer before it reaches `main`.

### Driver 0.3.0 external review (October 31, 2026)

Reviewed `048aa89..7b28076` on local `driver-0.3.0`, including decisions 40–43 and the internal corrections. HEAD and a clean starting tree were verified locally. **No blocking code finding; upgrade accepted with one nonblocking P3 documentation finding, R46.** This review does not merge or push the branch. Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-driver-0.3.0.md.

**R46 [P3], DESIGN.md:576 and mcp-mode.md:263, with the same simplification in driver-0.3.0.md §1.2–§1.3: describe credential protection by negotiated TLS mode.** The new text says optional encryption never validates a certificate and encrypts only LOGIN7, and excludes all -C cases from certificate protection. In 0.3.0 handler_factory maps server-required encryption to Mandatory even for optional/PreferOff; ssl_handler::resolve_tls_validation then validates when trust is false. LoginOnly skips validation; Strict validates even with -C, ignoring it. An independent executable using that exact method with equivalent minimal input/output types confirmed optional/LoginOnly skips, optional/server-required Mandatory validates, mandatory/trust skips, and Strict/trust validates. This is a source-policy probe, not an actual TLS handshake. Correct those three current explanations to state that optional can negotiate weaker protection, server-required full TLS with trust false validates, and Strict ignores -C. Keep the recommendation to request authenticated full encryption and the accepted stale-address/routing risks. No code/default/credential-binding change is requested. Acceptance: a consistent description of those four combinations across DESIGN §8, MCP §5 and the upgrade note. Initially open, nonblocking; closed by external verification of `7b28076..e7dc9fb` on October 31, 2026 (R46 fix verification, below). No newly reproduced credential leak.
**R46 fixed in `e7dc9fb` (2026-10-31), closed by external verification of `7b28076..e7dc9fb` on October 31, 2026:** DESIGN.md §8 (and the `-C` row of §4), mcp-mode.md §5, driver-0.3.0.md §1.2–§1.3 and upstream-issues.md U7 describe the four combinations by negotiated mode. Found while fixing it, the same rule's output defect: under `-Ns` a certificate failure's hint said to pass `-C` / set `trust_server_certificate`, which strict ignores; it now names strict's rule and offers `-Nm` (`db::tests::a_certificate_failure_under_strict_encryption_offers_no_trust`; DESIGN.md §6, mcp-mode.md §4). Judgment 4's positive type-21 case is promoted (`db::fake_server::an_acknowledged_enhanced_route_takes_the_login_to_its_database`), and judgment 2's supersession of decision 40's wording is marked on the decision (driver-0.3.0.md §8, External review).

**Judgments:**
1. Keep the production session-recovery guard, connect_retry_count 0 and LOGIN7 test. A debug assertion disappears in release, and a recovery-count check at put_back is too late for work already run in a replacement session. The test and runtime check cover different failure modes.
2. Accept exit 2 for -M's TCP deadline; an accepted socket whose login stalls remains exit 4 at -l + 1. The real release exe independently exercised both. Decision 40's original parallel-timeout exit-4 wording is superseded by the as-built correction in DESIGN §4 and driver-0.3.0.md §8. The string classification is deliberately pinned-driver-specific and must be rechecked on a bump. Its prefix also covers a DNS deadline; preserving the DNS-specific diagnostic could be a later refinement.
3. Accept the DNS-cache owner decision with R46's negotiated-TLS qualification. On-prem support remains unverified and “likely Azure” an inference. The original IP-named profile can be used to observe the acknowledgment before the IP cache-insertion filter, without any R20 exception: DnsCache::apply_verdict emits an exact DNS-cache event with its allowed Boolean even for an IP. A temporary recorder restricted to that target/event can retain only a Boolean; never enable packet-writer/general driver logging. A true verdict proves permission; false/absence alone does not distinguish omitted 0x0B from a negative bit. Temporary receive-side FeatureExtAck instrumentation retaining only presence/bit 0 can distinguish them. No owner login/diagnostic was run here. The earlier cache-membership probe was inconclusive; R20 does not prevent this observation. A pending lookup dropped by an outside deadline does not fall back, but a lookup returning an I/O timeout can; a login without a new FEATUREEXTACK leaves the previous cache verdict in place.
4. Current routing tests correctly pin their refusals. An independent actual-exe probe also passed acknowledged type 21: LogicalDb in the gateway LOGIN7 became PhysicalDb in the target LOGIN7, which JSON reported. A routed MCP call then reported PhysicalDb, one explicit escaped USE [Call]]Db]; switched the next call, and an unnamed call after reset reported PhysicalDb again. Exactly two resets and one USE were observed on one target connection, with no session-recovery feature in its LOGIN7. Promote this positive regression into source tests when convenient; no client defect was found, so no R47/blocker. The fake supplies the database/reset ENVCHANGEs, so this does not verify live SQL Server/Azure reset semantics, a routed TLS deployment or an expanded release target.
5. Keep settle_within; defer adopting set_cancel_timeout_secs. The new knob changes settlement, including finishing the current packet, and a shared OnceLock deadline must be scoped to a fresh operation handle. A later focused experiment should cover withdrawal, partial writes, attention, cancel precedence and pooling. Retain sqlh's outer retirement bound even if adopting it.

**Independent checks at 7b28076:** scripts/check.ps1 passed in 57 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing, 382 units (five ignored), 95 debug/89 release process tests; 60/54 live tests remained ignored. Release exe 2,895,360 bytes, 250,368 below 3 MiB, ten expected in-box imports. Driver dependency tables unchanged, MIT license verified. A source inventory confirmed 58 changed src files plus four package/README metadata files; targeted source review, not a complete security audit of all driver features. The source-diff navigation artifact stops at the first test module and does not stand in for full source inspection.

Independent release-process probes: -M to TEST-NET 192.0.2.1 at -l 1 returned the new exit-2 timeout text in 1.031 s; refused loopback sequential/-M exited 2 in 1.047/1.031 s; a silent loopback prelogin peer exited 4 in 2.032/2.031 s. Acknowledged type 21 succeeded; unacknowledged and early type 21 returned their specific exit-2 reasons with no target LOGIN7. Routed MCP USE/reset and the four TLS-policy cases passed. Synthetic login credentials only, no owner profiles or password; TEST-NET received no login.

Developer reports 60 debug/54 release live passes, the reset spike's seven sections, smoke goldens, SQL login timings/failure and large-value peak-memory measurements; these were source/record reviewed, not rerun. No independent live SQL Server, Credential Manager, owner-profile operation, client session, hosted CI or remote/push verification. The driver-only +80,896-byte same-tree measurement is reported evidence; the final independent exe is +82,944 bytes over the recorded pre-upgrade tree, including sqlh code changes. ### R46 fix verification (October 31, 2026)

Reviewed `7b28076..e7dc9fb`, merged as `1dd341f`. DESIGN §8/§4, MCP §5, upgrade note §1.2–§1.3 and U7 now distinguish requested/negotiated encryption consistently: Strict validates even with trust; Mandatory unless trusted; Optional negotiates checked full TLS when the server requires it and trust is off, otherwise permits LoginOnly without validation or no encryption. Optional's weaker-negotiation risk and the advice to require checked encryption remain clear. The strict hint fixes ineffective trust advice while retaining the raw error and exit 2; the MCP form names profile keys. Non-strict and non-TLS controls retain their earlier paths. Keep -Nm as an explicit opt-out from Strict, not an automatic downgrade or equivalent certificate protection. The broad match selects strict-mode TLS/capability advice, not proof that every matched failure is a certificate failure: generic TLS/Schannel/SEC_E text is intentionally included. MCP with strict/trust true produced isError with exit-2 text, profile settings only and no -C/-Nm flags, then ended normally after EOF. All six cases passed. Non-strict probes framed the TLS handshake in TDS; Strict used direct TLS with tds/8.0 ALPN. No actual SQL Server login succeeded or was needed. Ephemeral certificate/key files were removed, no owner data or trust-store changes. These are actual TLS failure paths, not just constructed error enums; they do not validate successful Strict sessions or live old-server/Azure behavior.

The promoted acknowledged type-21 test passed in the full unit suite: target LOGIN7 database sales and no session recovery, with the existing negative cases retained. The earlier independent MCP routed USE/reset probe remains prior evidence; it was not rerun in this round. Decision 40 now explicitly supersedes its original parallel-timeout exit-4 wording. The subsequent owner-approved DNS diagnostic record supersedes the brief's pending status: three reported accepted logins returned allowed=false on the unchanged test profile. Verdict source logic supports no permission for those observed logins without distinguishing absent 0x0B from a negative bit. ### Versioning external review, decision 44 (October 31, 2026)

Reviewed `7fed369..157334f` on main (c950a8e records the prior R46 review; 157334f implements versioning), with HEAD and a clean starting tree verified. **Accepted with one nonblocking P3 finding, R47; no blocking resource/build/workflow finding.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-versioning.md.

**R47 [P3], build.rs:37,44: validate the complete SQLH_VERSION before emitting Cargo output.** Prefix matching accepts empty, spaced, Unicode, empty-identifier, numeric-leading-zero and newline suffixes despite decision 44's SemVer policy. The exact build script accepted 0.1.0-, 0.1.0-not a version and 0.1.0-01. An independent tiny Cargo fixture with an unchanged copy of build.rs reproduced successful empty/spaced builds and a concrete version split: given 0.1.0-dev followed by a newline and a benign cargo:warning directive, Cargo interpreted the extra line and compiled SQLH_BUILT_VERSION as 0.1.0-dev while ProductVersion retained the full multiline input. This violates the shared-version contract. Validate the whole intended SemVer form before printing the version or writing the resource, retaining the exact Cargo core restriction; reject empty identifiers, controls/whitespace/newlines, invalid identifier characters and numeric leading zeros. Keep valid local/dev/rc forms; explicitly decide metadata support rather than silently trimming/replacing input. No new runtime dependency is needed. Acceptance: valid core/dev.99/rc.1 inputs and local fallback work, malformed forms fail clearly before a version directive/resource, and a Cargo newline regression cannot split code/resource identity. Initially open, nonblocking; closed by external verification of `157334f..8ae9eff` on October 31, 2026 (R47 and decision 45 external review, below). The workflow's fixed inputs are safe; this is trusted build configuration, not a network/SQL or privilege-boundary exploit.
**R47 fixed in `16bb825` (2026-10-31), closed by external verification of `157334f..8ae9eff` on October 31, 2026:**
- `src/build_version.rs::checked` validates the whole `SQLH_VERSION`: `Cargo.toml`'s version, alone or with a semver pre-release of dot-separated identifiers (ASCII letters, digits and `-`, none empty, no leading zero in a number). Build metadata is refused, since nothing makes it and semver ignores it in ordering.
- `build.rs` runs the check before it prints any directive or writes the resource.
- Library tests compile the same file as a module: `build_version::tests::the_release_workflows_versions_pass_and_malformed_ones_are_refused`. It passes core, `dev.99`, `rc.1`, `local`, `0` and `x-y.7`, and refuses the review's cases (`0.1.0-`, a space, `0.1.0-01`, a newline with a `cargo:warning` line, CR, tab, non-ASCII, empty identifiers, `+` metadata, another core).
- Checked by hand:
  - `SQLH_VERSION` with a newline and a `cargo:warning` line fails the build with the message, and no warning directive is taken;
  - `0.1.0-01` fails;
  - `0.1.0-rc.1` builds and reports itself;
  - unset gives `0.1.0-local`.

**Judgments:**
1. Accept the hand-written .res. An independent parser checked entry/block boundaries, UTF-16 value lengths, alignments, flags, translation and fixed fields. Native GetFileVersionInfoW/VerQueryValueW read local/tag/dev/65,535-boundary and Unicode stress resources after optimized MSVC/fat-LTO links. For the exact pre-release declaration, SDK 10.0.26100.0 rc.exe produced a byte-identical 840-byte .res. The tag resource had 0.1.0 strings, fixed 0.1.0.9 and no prerelease bit. These were exact-writer/standalone-link probes, not a full signed tag release. No dropped resource or linker warning was observed; keep the actual-exe process test as the toolchain regression check. rc.exe remains a review tool, not a build dependency.
2. Accept the run number as the fourth u16, with zero for local/check builds. Overflow must fail rather than wrap/clamp; the observed 70,000 case does fail. Plan a migration before 65,535. Reruns share a run number; this is not a unique attempt/source identity, and the base version dominates numeric ordering across backports or version changes.
3. Keep -local: it is a non-release below the matching final version. +local would be ignored for SemVer precedence. The label does not identify the exact source revision; CLI/MCP share app::VERSION as before.
4. Tighten <cargo>-anything as R47 requests; normal workflow-generated versions are unaffected.
5. Accept the Windows PowerShell 5.1 property test: it uses the existing watchdog/shell helper, literal escaped path and Windows consumer API, with no new shipped FFI/runtime dependency. Its eleven-field and --version comparisons pass in debug/release; independent direct Win32 queries complement it.

Independent scripts/check.ps1 passed in 56 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing; 384 units (five ignored), 97 debug processes (60 ignored), 91 release processes (54 ignored). Release exe 2,897,408 bytes (+1,024), 248,320 below 3 MiB, ten expected in-box imports. No Cargo.lock/dependency/import addition. Invalid core 0.2.0 and build 70,000 are rejected. The new verification span extracted verbatim from release.yml passed against a copied actual local-release exe and rejected a wrong expected string and wrong fourth number. Signature/hash phases were not rerun by that local span; signing-job isolation remains unchanged by source review. --help stays 62 lines, README tally is 42 and the JSON contract remains independently versioned at 2.1.

Hosted run 41238698571 was read through gh, with no mutation. The last saved snapshot targets 157334f and has build/Check still in progress; sign/verify/publish success was not independently observed. No workflow dispatch, signing or publication was requested or performed. No independent live SQL Server/CredMan/smoke-golden run or full signed tag build; those are unnecessary to validate this metadata/help increment. The developer's release-style build is reported evidence; default full builds and exact-writer tag/dev probes are independent.
