### MCP client feedback (October 31, 2026)

The owner ran `sqlh mcp` from Claude Desktop (chat and Cowork) against a real SQL Server 2016 in an availability group; [notes/mcp-client-feedback.md](notes/mcp-client-feedback.md) keeps the findings, without the environment's names. Decision 45 (§6) came from the first item.
1. **Built (2026-10-31), decision 45:**
   - a capped result in a batch with later statements counts for 5 s, so they run;
   - a run that still loses them ends `-- partial:` (exit 0) and names the batches;
   - a single-output batch's note says only that its total is a lower bound.
   Tests:
   - `guard::tests::several_outputs_counts_the_statements_a_stop_could_cancel`;
   - `db::tests::a_batch_with_later_statements_counts_for_longer`;
   - `app::tests::a_run_that_lost_statements_to_a_stopped_count_is_partial`;
   - `output::tests::the_note_says_the_rest_did_not_run_only_where_it_could_show_something`;
   - live: `a_capped_result_lets_the_later_statements_run_or_the_run_is_partial`, run against the test server: 200,000 rows let the later statements run; a three-way cross join ends `partial` after 5 s.
   The live tests that pinned the old note were updated, and their batches made single-statement where they test the 250 ms path.
2. **Built, the MCP schema:**
   - `query` takes `exact_count`;
   - `info` takes no `max_rows` (it took no `max_cell` either until R49 restored it);
   - each limit's description gives its default;
   - `check`'s `sql` says it is compiled, not run;
   - the `query` description and the instructions give the status forms, the escapes, `≥N` and the `GO` advice;
   - the MCP advice names `exact_count = true` in place of the profile's key.
   Test: `mcp::tools::tests::the_schemas_state_their_defaults_and_exact_count_reaches_the_run`, plus refusals in `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema`.
3. **Planned, not started:**
   - (a) paging for `definition` (a line range), since a 63,000-character procedure only reached the model through the client's fallback file;
   - (b) `info`: the name the connection dialed, the availability group role (`sys.dm_hadr_availability_replica_states`, which needs `VIEW SERVER STATE`), and the time zone name where the server has `CURRENT_TIMEZONE()` (2019 and later). Its catalog SQL must still run on 2016, which the client used.
4. **Declined:** a key=value status line (`format: json` is that), cut markers moved to a footer, and the `definition` line count (correct). The reasons are in the note.

### R47 and decision 45 external review (October 31, 2026)

Reviewed `157334f..8ae9eff` on main, HEAD and a clean starting tree verified. **Review-time verdict: R47 closed; decision 45 needed R48 [P2] and R49 [P2] before acceptance. Both are now closed by the R48/R49 fix verification below.** Its exit/JSON/budget policy choices are accepted with the limits below. Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-r47-decision45.md. No reviewer push or commit.

**R48 [P2], guard.rs:483/524 and run.rs:1077 onward: account for known multi-result diagnostics and output-affecting session modes.** SET is always silent and DBCC counts as one, but allowed STATISTICS XML/PROFILE queries return later plan/profile output, and SHOW_STATISTICS can return header, density and histogram. The exact guard accepts each and several_outputs returns false. Thus a count stop can discard later visible results at 250 ms, keep ok/exit 0, and now say only that the total is a lower bound, losing even the prior cancellation warning. Independent real release MCP/fake-TDS probes with one shown row, one hidden row and a delayed next result reproduced this: XML and PROFILE cut around 0.28 s with ok and one result; XML with exact_count true delivered both in 1.03 s. PROFILE enabled in a preceding GO batch likewise lost the later result of batch 2. A synthetic SHOW_STATISTICS header/density/histogram sequence cut in density around 0.30 s and omitted the histogram while still ok. The fake supplies the result sequence, not execution of diagnostic SQL; Microsoft documents those results. Fix the conservative multiplicity assumption consistently for budget and partial/note accounting, including a mode established before GO; over-count known diagnostic families if necessary. Do not block the diagnostic SQL to evade the issue. Acceptance: same-batch and preceding-GO XML/PROFILE plus multi-result DBCC regressions; later output arrives below the five-second budget, and a forced stop is honestly partial. Preserve the plain single-result short path, exact-count and cancellation controls. At that review, open P2 output correctness, not a write-guard bypass. Closed by the R48/R49 fix verification below.

**R49 [P2], mcp/tools.rs:94/364/575: info still applies max_cell after rejecting its override.** Tool.limits=false removes both limit arguments, but execute inherits settings.limits and schema::Command::limits does not exempt info. INFO_SQL has string values that a configured cell cap can truncate. Independent real-process fake-TDS tests under launch --max-cell 1 and separately profile max_cell=1 returned cells_cut=1 and advised raise max_cell, then info max_cell:0 was refused as unknown argument with isError/exit-3 text. A query max_cell:0 returned the same complete sample-host string. Restore info's max_cell separately from max_rows, or deliberately exempt info from inherited cell limits (profile and launch), retaining the run-wide output cap. Do not just hide the unusable advice. Acceptance: restrictive profile and launch cases return info intact by policy or allow a documented override to do so; correction advice works. max_rows may stay absent for the one-row tool. At that review, open P2 metadata/corrective-flow regression; ordinary default limits are normally unaffected. Closed by the R48/R49 fix verification below.

**R48 and R49 fixed (2026-10-31), externally verified in `8ae9eff..2b0f130` below:**
- **R48:**
  - `DBCC` counts as two in `guard::several_outputs` (`REPEATING_STARTS`), and so does a `SET` of an option that adds output (`adds_output`: `STATISTICS ...` or `SHOWPLAN_...`, `OFF` included).
  - `guard::sets_output_modes` finds such a `SET` in a batch. `run::run_opened` keeps a run-level `output_modes`, so the batch that sets one and every later batch get the longer count and the honest note and status.
  - Tests:
    - `guard::tests::several_outputs_counts_the_statements_a_stop_could_cancel` (new cases) and `options_that_add_output_are_found_for_the_batches_after_them`;
    - engine tests on a new fake turn, `Turn::Pause` (two rows, a wait, then the result's end and a second result; an attention during the wait is acknowledged and the rest withheld): `output_that_diagnostics_add_after_a_slow_result_arrives` (STATISTICS XML in the same batch, PROFILE before a `GO`, DBCC SHOW_STATISTICS: each, under `max_rows 1` with a 1 s pause, gets its second result, `ok`); `a_diagnostic_result_still_cut_after_the_longer_count_is_partial` (an 8 s pause is cut after 5 s: no second result, the cancellation note, `partial`); `a_plain_query_keeps_the_short_count_and_exact_count_waits` (a single `SELECT` still stops at 250 ms, `ok`, with the lower-bound note; `exact_count` waits the pause out).
- **R49:** `info` takes `max_cell` again (`Tool::rows` now governs only `max_rows`). Its description names the default, and a small configured limit can be lifted per call, which makes the note's advice usable. Tests: `calls_are_checked_against_the_schema` (`info` refuses `max_rows` and lists `max_cell`), `the_schemas_state_their_defaults_and_exact_count_reaches_the_run` (`info`'s `max_cell` schema, and a call's `max_cell: 0` planned).

**R47:** the shared checker implements the documented full ASCII pre-release grammar, explicitly refuses metadata, and runs before Cargo output/resource creation. Twenty independent exact-build-script cases passed: valid core/local/dev.99/rc.1/0/x-y.7 forms and invalid empty, space, numeric-leading-zero, newline-plus-warning, CR/tab, empty components, non-ASCII, metadata, wrong core and leading-space forms. Every invalid case returned empty stdout and no resource, closing the newline identity split. The same source's library table test and actual-exe property tests passed. The earlier versioning Actions run 41238698571 at 157334f is now completed/success for all four jobs by independent read-only gh query; no signed asset was downloaded/inspected and no CI of local-only 8ae9eff is claimed.

**Other judgments:** accept partial with exit 0 and JSON contract 2.1: ok remains an exit-code indicator, not execution completeness, and existing counting_stopped/row_count_exact carry conservative incompleteness. summary is human text, not a new structured enum. A future additive cut field may help automation but is not required by this change. Accept the five-second policy as evidence-driven and revisitable, per counted result and bounded by -t; it is neither a whole-call limit nor a guaranteed process-return time. Last-result over-counting is the documented conservative approximation. Independent two-SELECT controls delivered both results after 1.02 s; withheld output beyond five seconds sent attention around 5.03 s and reported partial/isError false. exact_count true with -t 1 stopped around 1.05 s and reported partial. Cursor/FETCH, IF, loop and keyword-label recognizer probes marked multiple; WAITFOR remains guard-refused, not a live parser proof. Cached-schema argument refusals are acceptable for genuinely unused pre-release fields and name the correction, but R49 invalidates the premise for max_cell. Query exact_count's OR with the profile policy is a valid enabling override; false does not force a true profile back to bounded counting. No new write/reuse policy or driver dependency was added.

Independent scripts/check.ps1 passed in 58 s: locked metadata, fmt, Clippy default/timing; 390 units (five ignored), 97 debug processes (61 ignored), 91 release processes (55 ignored); 2,909,184-byte exe, 236,544 below 3 MiB and ten expected in-box imports. Eight actual MCP/TDS counting/output cases and two info-limit configurations were recorded, plus 13 recognizer cases. No independent live SQL Server/client rerun; the developer's 61 debug/55 release live passes, unchanged smoke goldens and real-link case remain supplied evidence. The SQL Server 2016 AG feedback does not establish a new supported release floor.

### R48/R49 fix verification (October 31, 2026)

Reviewed `8ae9eff..2b0f130` on main, HEAD `2b0f130` and a clean starting tree verified. **R48 and R49 closed; decision 45 accepted with one nonblocking P3 test-reliability follow-up, R50. No new production correctness finding.** Full ignored report: archive/reviews/code-review-r48-r49-verification.md. No reviewer commit or push.

**R48 closed:** `DBCC` and output-affecting SET families are conservatively multiple-output, and `run_opened` applies the same run-level flag to the longer count and partial/cut accounting, including after GO. Independent release MCP/fake-TDS tests delivered the delayed later results for same-batch XML/PROFILE, preceding-GO XML/PROFILE and a synthetic SHOW_STATISTICS header/density/histogram sequence in about 1.03-1.05 s, exact, ok, with no attention. XML and preceding-GO PROFILE withheld for seven seconds sent attention around 5.03 s and reported partial, exit 0, with inexact counts and the explicit rest-cancelled note. A plain single query retained its roughly 0.28 s count and lower-bound/ok semantics; exact-count and two-query controls delivered both results. XML with exact_count and -t 1 stopped around 1.03 s with partial. OFF-before-GO and SHOWPLAN-before-GO took the conservative longer path. A second MCP call after an XML-mode call returned to the plain roughly 0.27 s policy. The peer supplies results, not SQL execution; no real plan or SQL Server permission behavior is claimed from it. Sixteen exact-source recognizer probes also passed.

**R49 closed:** Tool::rows governs only max_rows; definitions and takes consistently expose max_cell on info, using the existing checked override. Under launch --max-cell 1 and, separately, profile max_cell=1, independent release MCP/fake-TDS calls first cut the synthetic info value and advised raising max_cell. A subsequent info max_cell:0 returned the entire sample-host and cells_cut=0 in both configurations. A query override was the control. The schema/plan tests passed, including refusal of info.max_rows and acceptance of info.max_cell:0. Output caps are unchanged.

**Requested judgments:** accept every DBCC and any SET STATISTICS or SET SHOWPLAN_ option as the conservative family boundary for the current permitted diagnostic surface. Existing procedures and loops already count as multiple output. No additional concrete missing family was found; this remains lexical policy, not a proof of arbitrary server behavior. Accept the monotonic output_modes flag: OFF in the text does not prove execution or that all modes are off, and the accepted false positive costs a longer count. The flag is local to run_opened; it does not leak into later MCP calls, as the consecutive-call probe also confirms. The extra scan is linear and short-circuited after the flag becomes true.

**R50 [P3], engine.rs:2061-2065: remove an exact one-second floor from the new diagnostic-tail test.** The first independent standard check failed at unit step 5: output_that_diagnostics_add_after_a_slow_result_arrives had already passed exit/content/no-stopped-count checks, then its preceding-GO PROFILE case failed called.elapsed >= 1 s at **999.7969 ms**. A socket read timeout in Turn::Pause is compared to the call's Instant duration at an unnecessarily exact boundary. The precise OS timing cause was not isolated; this is a demonstrated false test failure, not a production timeout finding. Remove the redundant duration floor, or retain a generous separation from the old 250 ms path, while preserving result/no-stop assertions, the longer-budget partial test and the short/exact controls. Do not loosen product timeout bounds. Acceptance: the test still detects removal of the R48 fix but permits correct receipt just below one second, and focused/full non-live checks pass repeatedly. Three exact focused reruns and the complete standard rerun passed here; that does not erase the first failure. Nonblocking; R48 remains closed.

**Independent checks:** first run passed locked metadata, fmt and both Clippy feature sets, then failed only on R50 (393 units passed). The full non-live rerun passed in 60 s: **394 unit, 97 debug process and 91 release process tests**, locked release build, size and imports; five unit, 61 debug live and 55 release live tests ignored. Exe **2,911,232 bytes**, 234,496 below 3 MiB, ten approved in-box imports. Fourteen query scenarios, one two-call reset scenario and two info-limit configurations passed, plus the sixteen guard cases. Evidence: artifacts/r48-r49-review-check.log, artifacts/r48-r49-review-check-rerun.log, artifacts/r48-r49-targeted-1.log through -3.log, artifacts/r48-r49-process-probes.json and .log, artifacts/r48-r49-guard-corpus.json, and ignored scratch helpers. Earlier evidence preserved.

**Not independently rerun:** live SQL Server tests/diagnostic SQL, interactive MCP clients, hosted CI or signed publication. Developer-reported 61 debug/55 release live passes and real diagnostic/override checks at 2b0f130 remain supplied evidence. The reported dev.7 release was built at the earlier 8ae9eff. Only review/status Markdown and ignored synthetic artifacts changed; no source/tests, Cargo, owner credentials/profiles/database, client state or hosted configuration changed.

### Setting the default profile (owner, 2026-10-26, from manual testing; planned, not started)

`profiles.toml` has a top-level `default = "NAME"` (DESIGN.md §3 and §8: used when no `--profile`, no `SQLH_PROFILE` and no `-S`/`SQLCMDSERVER` is given), but nothing in the CLI sets it after the first `profiles add` (which makes the first profile the default, `profile.rs`, `became_default`). `profiles rm` of the default clears it silently (`Config::remove`), so removing the first of three profiles leaves two profiles, no default and no CLI way to choose one; the only fix is editing the file. `--help` mentions the default only as "Without -S: the default one" on the `--profile` line. (S)
1. **`sqlh profiles default NAME`** sets the default (`NAME` must exist, as the file's load check already requires; case as `find` matches it); **`sqlh profiles default --clear`** removes it. A text note says what it did, and the profiles file's path is written as typed (decision 38).
2. **`profiles rm` says so when it clears the default**, naming the fix: `NAME was the default profile: runs without -S or --profile now need one (sqlh profiles default NAME)`.
3. **`--help`:** one more line under `PROFILES`, and the `--profile` line points to it. Keep `--help` under about 60 lines (it is at 62 now, so trim elsewhere).
4. **Not chosen, with reasons:** promoting another profile when the default is removed (with two or more left it is a guess, and a wrong guess makes a bare `sqlh -Q ...` hit the wrong server silently, worse for an agent than a clear "no profile selected" error; promoting only when exactly one is left was considered and rejected for one rule in every case); an `add --default` flag (redundant once `profiles default` exists).
5. **Same change:** DESIGN.md §8 (the subcommand and the `rm` note), `src/help.txt`, tests (`profile.rs` unit tests with a fake store, a process-level test in `tests/cli.rs`, an `rm` of the default that prints the note), and README's "Built for agents" list only if it changes what an agent gains (likely not: it is a person's setup command).
6. **Open:** the owner has not yet approved the design; record it in §6 as a decision when taken.

### Phase 6: command categorization (VISION priority 4)

This is design work with the owner before any code:
1. List concrete DBA investigation workflows: blocking chains, wait stats, Query Store, plan cache and showplans, index usage, statistics, `sp_who2`, and common community procedures.
2. List their risks.
3. Derive categories (data read, metadata read, server-state read, session scratch, maintenance, data write, DDL, security, external or remote).
4. Map each category to a policy (allow, deny, require `--allow-write` or the `execute` tool).

Classifier v2 implements the result. Showplan support (`SET SHOWPLAN_XML`, summarized) probably belongs here as a first-class DBA read. (L, mostly design)

**Design (2026-10-23): [command-categories.md](command-categories.md).** Steps 1–4 are answered there as a design for owner decisions: the workflows and risks, a two-layer model (six kinds of change a person can grant, named in DBA words: `data`, `maintenance`, `tuning`, `kill`, `remote`, `unvetted`; and reason labels used only in refusals), a policy map per mode, Part 1 (read-only runs and `sqlh mcp`, buildable first) and Part 2 (changes: the deny tier, grants per kind, the `execute` tool), and decisions D1–D15 in the order to take them, all taken on 2026-10-23 and recorded in §6 as decisions 22–36. **Decided:** Part 1 (D2–D9) in full as recommended; Part 2 with the kinds (decisions 22, 32), the deny tier (31), `kill` (33), literal dynamic SQL and per-database runs (34) and trusted entries (35), but no `execute` tool in Phase 6 (36, against the recommendation). Open before building: the live checks in the design's §10, the MSDTC question for linked-server reads (25), and the SNAPSHOT test (24), which needs the owner's approval of a database-level change. It came from six workflow surveys probed against the guard, a draft, a safety critique and a DBA-practicality critique, and a revision checked for coverage and facts; every guard verdict in it was re-run on `f905385` (its Appendix A), and its Appendix B answers each critique issue.

**Interim step, not blocked on the taxonomy: extend the EXEC allowlist** (owner, 2026-10-10: "we will need to allow `xp_readerrorlog` and `xp_instance_regread`, and maybe more if the login permissions allow"). VISION.md keeps selecting individual permitted commands separate from designing the categories, so this can ship first. Today `xp_*` is blocked wholesale (DESIGN.md §7), which rules out reading the SQL Server error log, a basic investigation. The earlier sql-query-runner skill already allows these two, so they come from real use. (S)
- **Principle:** the allowlist says which commands are read-only. Whether a login may run one is the server's call, and database permissions stay the enforcement boundary (VISION.md). So a command belongs on the list because it only reads, not because this login happens to have the rights, and a login without the permission just gets the server's error.
- **First additions:** `xp_readerrorlog` and its wrapper `sp_readerrorlog`, and `xp_instance_regread`. Name them exactly: a prefix match on `xp_*` would also admit `xp_cmdshell` and `xp_instance_regwrite`, which stay blocked. `xp_instance_regread` reads registry values, which can disclose information; record that in the category notes.
- **Already allowed:** the skill's other diagnostics, `sp_who`, `sp_who2` and `DBCC TRACESTATUS`, pass sqlh's guard today (checked against the test server, 2026-10-12), so these two `xp_` procedures are all that sql-query-runner allows and sqlh refuses. The refusal also says the procedure "would write", which is wrong for an unknown procedure; the categorization should give it its own wording.
- **Candidates to evaluate in the same pass:** `xp_fixeddrives`, `xp_msver`, and `sp_configure` in its listing form only (with arguments it changes settings, so it needs the argument check `sp_spaceused` gets).
- **To verify on SQL Server 2025 Standard:** which permission each one needs for a non-sysadmin login (newer versions have finer-grained server permissions for the error log), so the blocked/allowed advice and the docs can say what to grant.
- **Acceptance:** table-driven guard tests (allowed bare and qualified as `sys.`/`master.`; `xp_instance_regwrite` and `xp_cmdshell` still blocked; stacked statements still refused); a live read of the error log; DESIGN.md §7 updated in the same change.
- **Built (2026-10-23).** `PROCS` gains `xp_readerrorlog`, `sp_readerrorlog`, `sp_enumerrorlogs` (it lists the log archives that `sp_readerrorlog`'s first argument numbers; added in the same pass, as a read-only companion), `xp_instance_regread`, `xp_fixeddrives`, `xp_msver` and `sp_configure`. `sp_configure` joins `sp_spaceused` in `ARG_CHECKS`, a table of the allowlisted procedures that write when given one argument: its value (`@configvalue`, named or second) is refused, with `sys.configurations` as the alternative, since `sp_configure` hides the advanced options until `show advanced options`, itself a setting, is on (seen live: `sp_configure 'max degree of parallelism'` failed with error 15123 on the test server). Left out: `xp_regread` (any key, unmapped), `xp_enumerrorlogs` (`sp_enumerrorlogs` covers it), and the writers. Tests: `guard::tests::diagnostic_extended_procedures_are_allowed_by_exact_name` (bare, `sys.`, `master.sys.`, `master.dbo.`, `master..`, bracketed and implicit; `INSERT #t EXEC`; the writing neighbours and prefix-sharing names blocked; other qualifiers blocked; stacked statements refused at their own line) and `sp_configure_with_a_value_is_blocked`; live, `the_error_log_and_server_reads_run_read_only_and_the_server_decides_who_may` reads the error log as the test profile and measures SQL Server 2025's permissions with a `Reader` login and each server permission alone, the table in DESIGN.md §7, on the test server, which is Standard Developer (D9: confirming it on Standard stays on the release checklist): `xp_readerrorlog` needs sysadmin or an explicit `EXECUTE` (not `VIEW SERVER STATE` or `VIEW ANY ERROR LOG`), the `sp_` log procedures need `VIEW SERVER STATE`, `VIEW ANY ERROR LOG` or securityadmin (the last from their source, read with `OBJECT_DEFINITION`, which checks this and only reads), `xp_fixeddrives` returns no rows without sysadmin, and `xp_instance_regread` runs with no server permission and, in a one-off probe, read even the service account's key (a disclosure for the category notes). None of the additions takes an app lock, sets session context or waits (mcp-mode.md §5; checked by reading). **Review (2026-10-23, an internal three-lens review with a verifier per finding; not the external reviewer):** two ways past the argument check, both older than this change in `sp_spaceused`'s, were fixed with tests: a procedure number (`EXEC sp_configure;1 'show advanced options', 1`, which the server takes, checked live) ended the scan at its `;`, and a bare value spelled like an unreserved statement keyword (`EXEC sp_configure GET, 1`, `MOVE`, `THROW`) ended it at the word (`UNRESERVED_STARTS`); the docs' overclaims (unmeasured cells, the edition, a source claim for `sp_configure`) were corrected, the unmeasured cells now measured. The exe grew 3,072 bytes (2,808,320). Still open: the "would write" wording for an unknown procedure, which goes with the categorization below.

### Phase 7: evidence-based evaluations (VISION priority 5)

Exact row counts (from 2.6 and real sessions), the Windows/SQL Server 2016 floor, the md format, and what SmartScreen and antivirus make of the signed exe on locked-down workstations (the signing itself is task 3.6). Agent trial 3 (from trial 2): tasks that meet the row, value and time limits without the model being able to raise them first (rules that forbid changing limits, or results whose size is unknown up front), to see how it reads `≥N … counting stopped`, `…[+N]` and a timeout, and whether 250 ms is the right count budget.

### Phase 8: delivery to agents (skill, connector, plugin)

Raised 2026-10-10. Not a VISION priority: the exe is the product, and these are wrappers that get it to agents. Nothing is decided or started; the owner deferred drafting the skill. In order:

1. **Skill** (S, no code). A `SKILL.md` for Bash-driven agents. `--help` already carries the syntax, so the skill covers workflow: describe before querying, how to read the output format, the limits and exit codes, and when to reach for sqlh. It can be drafted at any time, and is more useful once Phase 4's schema commands exist. From agent trial 2: never add `--allow-write` without the user's explicit go-ahead for that statement; narrow a query before raising `--max-rows`, `--max-cell` or `-t` or using `--exact-count`; compute derived figures (percentages, totals) in SQL or leave them out. From agent trial 1: tell the agent to state only what the output shows (the trial's answer named tables it never queried), to pass `-d` or check `DB_NAME()` first, and to look up column names before writing a query.
2. **Connector = local stdio MCP server** (built in Phase 5(a), not here). It is not a hosted, remote connector: credentials come from Credential Manager and SSPI on the workstation, and the workstation reaches the SQL Server, and neither survives a hosted deployment. Packaging it as a Claude Desktop extension (`.mcpb`) is a possible later step and is unresearched.
3. **Plugin** (S, after 2). Bundles the skill, the MCP config and the exe location so installing is one step. Open: whether the plugin carries the exe or points at an installed path, which ties into the code-signing decision in Phase 7.

Phase 5 chose MCP first (decision 14), so step 2 is built (5.3); the local daemon, (b), is deferred, and would be added beside it on the same engine, not instead of it.

### Azure SQL support and testing (owner, 2026-10-31; planned, not started)

The owner put Azure SQL on the roadmap after the DNS-cache check: the on-prem test server answers `allowed=false`, so decision 42's residual risk is likely Azure's alone, and nothing about Azure has been tested. VISION.md lists Azure as "not a first-release requirement", to be assessed before any commitment. This task is that assessment, then the testing; it changes no first-release scope until the owner decides (step 1). (M–L)

1. **Scope, an owner decision for §6:**
   - which targets: Azure SQL Database, Azure SQL Managed Instance, or both (Managed Instance is much closer to on-prem);
   - which authentication: SQL logins first, or Microsoft Entra ID too (none is built: sqlh has SQL and Windows SSPI only, and the driver's federated-auth support is unverified for sqlh);
   - the promise: supported, or "tested opportunistically" (DESIGN.md §12).
2. **A test target, the owner's:** provisioned by the owner, its cost the owner's call, and reached through a profile made with `sqlh profiles add` on the owner's machine. Its server name and credentials never go in the repo, as for `test`.
3. **Analysis, no code:** each difference with its evidence and its effect on sqlh, in `docs/notes/azure-sql.md`, before any change. Watch points known now (unverified on Azure unless marked):
   - **`USE` is unsupported in Azure SQL Database** (from Microsoft's documentation, not tested). This affects `sqlh mcp`'s per-call `database` (a `USE`, decision 15, mcp-mode.md §5), `dbs` and other schema commands, decision 34's per-database runs, and three-part names. `-d` is the login's database, so it should still work.
   - **Gateway routing:** Azure's redirect connection policy routes the login (ENVCHANGE 20, or 21 with a database). Decision 43 follows it with the same credentials. R20's binding is by the profile's name, and the certificate is checked against each hop's own name (R46). Check what `info`, the status line and JSON's `connection` report after a route.
   - **The DNS cache** (decision 42, U7): likely acknowledged by Azure. Rerun the event-only recorder (driver-0.3.0.md §8, DNS acknowledgement) against the target.
   - **The reset:** on Azure SQL Database, `sp_reset_connection` also resets the isolation level (Phase 5.2 and 5.3, built 2026-10-17, the isolation-level finding). The session setup sets the level on every call anyway, so `db::spike::spike_reset`'s SERIALIZABLE assertion needs a per-edition expectation.
   - **Server-level features** absent from Azure SQL Database: the error log and the other `xp_` allowlist entries (DESIGN.md §7), `sp_configure`, `KILL` of other sessions (decision 33), server permissions (`VIEW SERVER STATE` becomes `VIEW DATABASE STATE`). The refusals and server errors an agent gets should stay clear.
   - **Transient faults and latency:** Azure's transient login errors (for example 40613) and an internet round trip. Check them against connect-failures.md's bounds and the engine's one silent retry (decision 19).
   - **Encryption:** Azure requires it, and supports strict (TDS 8). `-No` should still end in full TLS there, checked (R46).
   - **The live tests' own needs:** a scratch database (`sqlh_test`, made by the fixtures), `CREATE LOGIN` for `Reader` (a contained user in Azure SQL Database), `KILL` in the MCP retry test, and setting the compatibility level (decision 21). These decide which tests can run there.
4. **Live run:** the smoke script and the live suite against the target. Classify each failure as expected (a missing feature), a sqlh defect, or a documentation gap, and record the results in the note.
5. **Changes:** catalog SQL with Azure-specific paths needs a supported-target decision and a maintenance justification (AGENTS.md, Catalog SQL). Fixes land with tests, and with DESIGN.md (§1, §12) and, if support is promised, the README.

## 5. Sequencing rationale

- **1.1 (F04) first.** It is the only finding where the tool states something false while possibly executing writes.
- **The test harness comes before the other fixes,** so every fix lands with a process-level regression test.
- **2.1 (connect latency) early, but time-boxed.** It is cheap to measure and could change Phase 5 entirely. Building pooling to hide a fixable 150 ms would be the wrong order. Bounded output and cancellation under a stalled reader (2.2–2.4) are release requirements, so they come before pooling or more discovery features, whatever 2.1 finds.
- **The Runner refactor (3.1) after the correctness fixes and before schema/MCP work.** Fixing defects first keeps the refactor behavior-neutral and easy to verify; schema commands and MCP both need its policies.

## 6. Owner decisions

Decided 2026-10-10:

1. **Where sqlh runs: workstations only.** The SQL Server is assumed to be remote in about 90% of use cases. The first release is validated on Windows 11 x64 workstations against a remote SQL Server 2025 Standard. Running on the database server is not a target.
2. **Exit code 7: output could not be delivered.** DESIGN.md §6 defines exactly what "undeliverable" means.
3. **Substitution order: split first, then substitute per batch** (today's behavior). Variables can't create batch separators. DESIGN.md §4.3 is corrected to match.
4. **Global temp tables (`##`) are blocked in read-only mode.**
5. **A scratch database and a low-privilege test login on the dev/test server are approved.** The scratch database is `sqlh_test`. The test login waits until its password can be stored without a console (planned with 3.4), so it never passes through a command line or a file. **How (owner, 2026-10-12):** a live-test fixture, connected through the `test` profile, creates the login with only `db_datareader` on `sqlh_test` (and StackOverflow2010 while it is still used) and `VIEW DEFINITION`; its password is generated at random in the process and never printed or saved to a file. As built (3.4), it isn't stored at all: the SQL that sets it reaches sqlh on stdin, and runs as the login get it from `SQLCMDPASSWORD` in their own environment; the test profile's stored password is for its own login only, so sqlh takes the variable. The login is dropped after each test. The guard and permission tests then run as that login. Also with 3.4: synthetic tables in `sqlh_test` replace StackOverflow2010 in the live tests, and the fixtures drop what they create and shrink `sqlh_test` when they finish, since the drain and memory tests grew it and tempdb on the dev server.

Decided 2026-10-11, except where an item gives a later date:

6. **The drain default: a bounded count for read-only runs (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** Following [notes/drain-experiment.md](notes/drain-experiment.md): in read-only runs, sqlh counts hidden rows for about 250 ms and then stops (an attention), reporting a lower bound floored to one significant digit (`100 of ≥800000 rows shown (counting stopped)`), so a result that ends inside the budget stays exact and the same query gives the same output. The exact count stays under `--allow-write` (a stopped count skips later statements, which can include a `COMMIT`), for input that mentions `XACT_ABORT`, and on request (a flag such as `--exact-count`). Independently, `-t` expiring while only hidden rows are being counted reports `≥N` with the run's own exit code, not exit 4, since the rows shown are complete. A stopped count is visible to machines: the status line says so (`ok: 2 batches, counting stopped in 1`) and each JSON batch gets a flag. Accepted cost: "limits never change what runs" no longer holds strictly, because a stopped count cancels the rest of that batch; in a read-only run nothing it skips outlives the run, and the output says it happened. Task 2.15, built 2026-10-11. **Clarified in building it, confirmed by the owner 2026-10-11:** `-t` expiring during the count stays exit 4 (i) with writes allowed, because a stop would skip a later `COMMIT` and the run would report success, and (ii) with `XACT_ABORT` in the input, because the attention rolls the read-only wrapper back, so the run would end with exit 1, or exit 0 with the wrapper gone, in place of the timeout. The 250 ms start at the first hidden row, so a stall before it is bounded by `-t` (default 60 s). Each JSON result also says whether its `row_count` is exact, and the contract is 2.0 (R16).
7. **Agent session defaults (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** Every connection gets `SET DEADLOCK_PRIORITY LOW` (the agent is the deadlock victim, error 1205) and `SET LOCK_TIMEOUT 10000` in both modes, so a blocked query fails after 10 s with error 1222 instead of waiting out `-t`; the lock timeout can be changed by a flag and a profile setting. The isolation level stays the database default (`READ COMMITTED`, which honors read-committed snapshot where the DBA turned it on): `READ UNCOMMITTED` is opt-in only (a flag such as `--read-uncommitted` and a profile setting), for read-only runs only, never under `--allow-write`, and the output notes it, because dirty reads can silently return uncommitted, duplicated or missing rows. Task 2.8, built 2026-10-11. The flag in a run whose profile allows writes (`write_policy = "allow"`) is a usage error too, since that run can write.
8. **A Git remote and CI: GitHub, with Actions (owner direction, 2026-10-10).** 3.3 becomes CI (3.5), and 3.6 adds a signed release workflow like ssms-executor's. **Decided 2026-10-12:** the repository starts private (GitHub Pro) and goes public when the owner is ready to present it. Before it goes public, the history is rewritten with `git filter-repo` to drop the review files (12 commits hold them, one with a local path), keeping the rest of the history; nothing else private was found in the history. **Done 2026-10-12, before the first push** (owner): with `git filter-branch`, since filter-repo needs Python, which this machine doesn't have. `code-review.md`, `code-review-phase1.md` and `code-review-phase2.md` were removed from every commit of `main` and `drain-prototype`; the nine commits that only added a review were dropped (114 commits became 105, the 13 merges kept), and every other commit kept its tree minus those files, its author, dates and message. Commit ids cited in messages were rewritten to the new ids in the same pass, and those in the docs in the commit after it (a dropped commit maps to its parent's rewrite, which has the same code). `main`'s tree is unchanged. Every commit's ids changed except the first. A scan of every blob and message of the new history found no local user path but the synthetic `/c/Users/me`, no address outside loopback, RFC 5737's documentation ranges and one test value, no email address, and none of the owner's server names. The old history is in `artifacts/sqlh-history-before-rewrite-2026-10-12.bundle`, with the old-to-new map in `artifacts/history-rewrite-commit-map.txt` (local, ignored). Not rewritten: Codex's two local checkpoint refs under `refs/codex/`, which point at trees, not commits; one still holds two review files. They are never pushed unless the whole repository is mirrored. Releases build on `workflow_dispatch` and version tags, with the version taken from the tag (3.6). **Name, decided 2026-10-12: sqlh, repository `tkwj/sqlh`.** "sqlharness" collides with `rgonek/sqlharness` on GitHub (an active C# project, July 2026: "safety-focused SQL benchmarking and optimization harness for coding agents" for SQL Server, Azure SQL and PostgreSQL, with MCP), and "harness" is close to Harness Inc.'s Database DevOps product; the exe name `sqlh` is free on crates.io and clashes only with small, unrelated Go libraries. The profiles folder and the Credential Manager names moved from `sqlharness` to `sqlh`, with a one-time migration of existing profiles and passwords (DESIGN.md §8). The local checkout folder can keep its name. **License, decided 2026-10-12: MIT** (owner: one license; MIT for its familiarity, and it matches the mssql-tds driver; every dependency is under a permissive license: MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD, Unlicense or Unicode-3.0). `LICENSE` and `Cargo.toml`'s `license` field say so. The release zip must also carry the dependencies' notices, which MIT and BSD require in binary distributions (3.6).
9. **Ask upstream to redact the password in packet dumps?** At debug level, mssql-tds hex-dumps every TDS packet before TLS encrypts it (`io/packet_writer.rs`), including the login packet ([notes/connect-latency.md](notes/connect-latency.md)). The password in it carries only the scramble the TDS protocol requires (nibble swap and XOR 0xA5), which anyone can reverse. It's a logging-hygiene issue (CWE-532), not a remote vulnerability: nothing leaks unless an application turns on debug logging for the driver. Other drivers' traces usually mask the password. The channel is the owner's choice: a GitHub issue, or Microsoft's security process. Check `main` first; this was read in 0.2.0. **Decided 2026-10-12 (owner): no, sqlh doesn't ask.** Its own rule stays the defense: never install a general logger while mssql-tds runs (AGENTS.md, Driver logging). Re-check the dump when bumping the driver.
10. **Provenance in the text output: always, on the status line (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** Every text and csv run that connected ends its status line with the server, the database the run started in and the connect time, for about 10 to 15 tokens a call: `-- ok: 1 batch, 241 ms; db01/master, connect 195 ms` (csv: on stderr with the rest of the metadata). Left out when nothing connected (usage error, guard refusal, failed connect). Names are escaped and cut like other metadata, within the ending's reserve. Auth method and encryption stay in JSON and `--verbose`. Shown every time, not only when `-d` was missing, so the shape of the status line doesn't depend on how the run was invoked. Rest of task 2.11, built 2026-10-11.
11. **Paths in metadata lines keep the escape rule (owner decision, 2026-10-11).** A backslash in a path inside a `-- ` line is written `\\`, like any other escaped field (DESIGN.md §5.1), and a reader decodes it like any other field. Input is never escaped: paths, SQL text and `-v` values are taken exactly as typed, so a path from Explorer's "Copy as path" (double-quoted, single backslashes) works as is after `-i` or `-o` in cmd.exe, PowerShell and Git Bash; only Git Bash eats an unquoted backslash, which is the shell's rule. Rejected: forward slashes (rewrites what was typed, and some cmd built-ins refuse them) and escaping a backslash only before an escape letter (changes the reader rule, and `C:\new` would still be doubled). Done 2026-10-11 in 2.13's follow-up: DESIGN.md §5.1 says paths follow the rule, the `--help` FORMAT lines say so without growing, and a process-level test runs a quoted "Copy as path" spelling, spaces included.
12. **Agents and the write switch (owner decision, 2026-10-12, from agent trial 2): new profiles refuse writes.** `sqlh profile add` saves `write_policy = "deny"` for a new profile unless `--write-policy` is given; existing profiles keep their policy, and a profile saved without the key is still `flag` (DESIGN.md §8). Built with task 2.16. **Decided 2026-10-12 (owner): `profile list` keeps showing the server's address and the login.** Every run's status line already names the server, and a login isn't a secret; hiding them would make choosing a profile harder for an agent without protecting anything. A control over what agents may see or change in profiles would be a separate design. The background: An agent asked to clean up a table added `--allow-write` on its own and ran a 927,269-row `DELETE`; only the profile's `deny` stopped it. Options: make `deny` the default for new profiles (so writes take an explicit change to the profile's policy), keep `flag` and rely on agent guidance (the skill, Phase 8), or both. Also: `sqlh profile list` showed the agent the server's address and the login; whether it should, in agent hands, is part of the same question. Precedent (comparison with the owner's earlier sql-query-runner skill, 2026-10-12): that skill gives agents no write path at all (a query must start with `SELECT` or `WITH`, or be one allowed `EXEC` or `DBCC TRACESTATUS`), so trial 2's cleanup would have stopped at its refusal. Its check is weaker than sqlh's, though: it looks only at the first word and runs with no rollback, so `WITH x AS (...) DELETE ...`, `SELECT 1; DELETE ...` and `SELECT ... INTO` pass and write. The nearest sqlh equivalent of "no write path for agents" is `deny` as the default for new profiles, which keeps the stronger guard and makes writing an explicit change to a profile's policy. That change can run unattended, so an agent that can run `profile add` can make it (R19): `deny` stops an agent reaching for `--allow-write`, it doesn't make writing a person's decision. A human-only control over profiles, if wanted, is a separate design.
13. **`profile list` is text only (owner decision, 2026-10-13, from R25).** Profiles are ancillary to the product: listing them is setup an agent does once per session, and text is the cheapest format to read. A json listing would need a document shape of its own (R25 found that the anonymous batch it used breaks contract 2.0's batch metadata), which isn't worth its cost now, so `--format` is refused for `profile list` and the JSON contract is unchanged. Search (`--search`) stays. Revisit only if a consumer that can't read text needs the list (Phase 8's MCP connector might).
14. **Connection reuse comes through MCP first (owner decision, 2026-10-15, Phase 5).** The reuse engine (connections kept open, reset between calls, the cleanup and clean-cancel rules) is built once as a library module, and `sqlh mcp` (docs/mcp-mode.md) is its first front end. A local per-profile daemon behind the CLI, which would speed up Bash-driven use too, is deferred: it would be added on the same engine only if evidence (an agent trial with MCP) shows Bash-driven use stays the main path. Why: VISION.md asks that reuse preserve execution policy, isolation between calls and reliable cleanup, and under MCP the harness owns the lifecycle and cancellation is part of the protocol, where a daemon would make sqlh build auto-start, stale-process, idle-exit, pipe-security and cross-process cancellation itself; MCP also gives native tools and separate read and write tools a harness can gate, and Phase 8 already plans the connector. The cost, accepted: until a daemon exists, a CLI call still pays its connect (about 195 ms on the dev LAN, 2.1).
15. **MCP calls are stateless, for now (owner decision, 2026-10-15, Phase 5).** Before every call the reused connection is reset (TDS RESETCONNECTION: login defaults restored, temp tables dropped, a stray transaction rolled back, the profile's database restored), then the session setup and, for reads, the per-call wrapper transaction run: each call behaves exactly like one CLI run, without the connect. No `session_open` handles, so docs/mcp-mode.md §5's sessions, and the hour-long read-only transaction they needed, are not built. Why: isolation between calls and reliable cleanup (VISION.md) come from the reset itself, a cancelled call can leave nothing behind but the connection, and agents and sqlh have no handles, expiry or long transaction to manage. Sessions can be added later on the same engine if an agent trial shows agents need state across calls. The cost, accepted: work that needs state across calls isn't possible read-only (a wait-stats delta is two snapshots the agent compares itself); within one call, a script's batches still share one connection and its temp tables. **As built (5.2, 2026-10-17; the decision is unchanged):** on SQL Server (all but Azure SQL Database) the reset doesn't restore the transaction isolation level, so a call's `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL` would outlive it on its pooled connection; each call is still like one CLI run because the session setup sets the level on every call, a reused connection's included (`READ COMMITTED`, or `READ UNCOMMITTED` for a profile that reads that way; `run::session_setup`). Found by the self-review (Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 1, P1); docs/notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md §1, DESIGN.md §4.3.
16. **MCP is read-only, for now (owner decision, 2026-10-16, Phase 5).** The first `sqlh mcp` offers only read tools (`query`, the schema commands, `info`), each in the read-only wrapper with the guard, whatever the profile's `write_policy`; there is no `execute` tool, so docs/mcp-mode.md's write tool and write sessions are not built. A write goes through the CLI, with its safeguards: the profile's policy (`deny` by default for new profiles, decision 12), `--allow-write` and the guard. Why: the speed win matters most for the many repeated reads of an investigation; agent trial 2's agent added `--allow-write` on its own and only the profile's `deny` stopped a 927,269-row `DELETE`, and under an `execute` tool the last check would be the harness's permission prompt, which users often set to always allow; and Phase 6's command categorization is where which statements need explicit approval, and how, gets decided, so `execute` is added with it rather than designed twice. The cost, accepted: a write during an MCP investigation pays the CLI's connect.
17. **A connection is reused after a cancel only when the server confirmed it (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** A call that ends with a TDS attention (a cancel from the client or harness, `-t`, or the bounded count of hidden rows stopping at its budget, decision 6) keeps its connection when the server acknowledged the attention within the settle bound (R15 B); the reset before the next call (decision 15) clears what the cancelled call left: an open wrapper transaction, temp tables, `SET`s (as built, all but the isolation level, which the session setup sets on every call: decision 15's as-built note). Anything less discards the connection and the next call reconnects: no acknowledgement (today's `COUNT_STOP_UNCONFIRMED`), the settle grace running out, a protocol error, a lost connection. Why: count stops end any query with many hidden rows, so cancels are routine, and always discarding would make the calls agents make most pay the login again; the reset is the mechanism connection pooling relies on, and the unclear cases are the ones discarded. Required before it ships: a live test that a confirmed cancel leaves the next call clean (`@@TRANCOUNT` 0, the cancelled call's temp table gone, the agent session's `SET`s restored), and fake-server tests that an unacknowledged or stalled attention discards and reconnects. The cost, accepted: more code and tests than always reconnecting, and a residual risk of driver state the reset doesn't clear, which those tests are there to catch (the driver can be swapped behind `src/db/`). **Amended 2026-10-17 (owner), after spike 5.1:** mssql-tds 0.2.0 gives no receipt for an acknowledged attention, so "confirmed by the server" can't be checked; a connection the driver kept (`is_connection_dead()` false, which covers an acknowledged attention, a cancel before the request was sent and a half-sent request withdrawn with EOM|IGNORE, all in step with the server) is reused, and one it marked dead, or one past sqlh's settle bound, is discarded. The check that a reused session is clean is the next call's reset, whose acknowledgement the driver verifies (the server's ResetConnection ENVCHANGE must come first, or the request fails with `ConnectionResetNotAcknowledged` and the connection is marked dead); then the connection is discarded and the call retried once on a new one (decision 19). Why: what matters is whether the session is clean and in step, which the verified reset answers on every reuse after any kind of cancel; the spike found every kind clean after it (docs/notes/mcp-reuse-spike.md §2). The missing receipt is U4 in docs/notes/upstream-issues.md, for the owner to raise; the rule can tighten if upstream adds one. **The live test it requires is built (5.4, 2026-10-18):** `mcp_a_cancel_the_driver_kept_leaves_the_next_call_clean`: the idle session holds no transaction, and the next call on the same session gets a fresh call's `@@TRANCOUNT` (its own wrapper's 1), `SET`s, language and level, with the cancelled call's temp table gone (Phase 5.4, above).
18. **An MCP server reaches the profiles listed at its launch (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** `sqlh mcp --profile test --profile reporting` serves those profiles and no others; each tool takes an optional `profile`, which must be one of them (with one listed, it is the default). The server's instructions name the allowed profiles with their descriptions (4.1), so the agent picks the right server in one call; a profile's connection opens on its first use. No tool argument names a server, a login or a password (docs/mcp-mode.md §3 stands for those). Why: the owner sets the reach once, in the harness config, and the agent never gets more; profile descriptions keep their job under MCP; one copy of the tool definitions keeps the agent's context cost flat however many profiles are listed; and with read-only tools (decision 16) the risk within the list is reads from servers the owner chose. Strict separation stays possible: a profile can get its own server entry, listed alone, so the harness gates it separately. The cost, accepted: within one server's list, the harness can't prompt per profile. **As built (5.3, 2026-10-17; the decision is unchanged):** `profile` is optional only with one profile listed (the default); with several, every tool's schema lists it in `required` and `tools::plan` refuses a call without one (`missing required property profile: one of A, B`, exit 3; Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 1). A call's `profile` matches in any ASCII case, as the launch's `--profile` does (since `becb56f`; until then it had to match the profiles file's spelling exactly), and the schemas' enum and the instructions show the file's spelling (mcp-mode.md §3).
19. **MCP connection limits (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** Per listed profile, up to 2 connections by default (`--pool N`, 1–8), opened only when a call needs one, so a second exists only when calls overlap. A connection idle for 15 minutes is closed (`--idle MINUTES`; 0 keeps connections open while the server runs). A reused connection found dead (a network drop, a killed session) is reconnected and the call retried once, silently; if the retry fails, the call gets the real error. Why: harnesses make several tool calls at once (parallel discovery), and two connections cover that without loading the server; 15 minutes covers a human pause mid-investigation and ends idle sessions on production servers well before firewalls drop them; and a retry is safe because every call is a read in a rolled-back wrapper (decisions 15, 16). The cost, accepted: the first call after a long pause pays the login again, and a busy session holds up to two server sessions per listed profile. **As built (5.2, 2026-10-17, `becb56f`, `6e7a0d8` and `4519341`, its boundary stated in `dc24d4b`): the decision's implementation, recorded for the owner's code review; the decision is unchanged, and the implementation raises no question of its own, since only a dead connection is replaced. Accepted as built by the external review (2026-10-18, its question 1), its ten-second boundary explicit, with the regressions it asked for kept since `c3c4a63`.** A reused connection shows it is dead at its first requests, the session setup (`run::setup`, which carries the reset) and the call's `USE` when it names a database (`run::use_database`), before any of the call's own SQL, so the call is retried on a new connection when either fails with the connection dead or retired (`engine::dead_before_work`): closed while idle (`Lost`), a reset the server didn't acknowledge (`Protocol`), or gone silent (a timeout whose attention went unanswered within the driver's 5 s). Not retried: a SQL error (a `database` that doesn't exist), a cancel (nor anything once the call's cancel is set), and a timeout at the call's own limit on a connection the driver kept, which ends `query timeout after N s in the session setup` (or `... changing to the call's database`). **The probe** (`REUSE_PROBE`): a reused connection's setup gets at most 5 s when the call's timeout is longer or 0; the `USE` keeps the call's timeout and runs once on its connection, after the setup has worked. Why: a connection gone silent while idle (a firewall's idle cut, a half-open socket) would otherwise hold the call for its whole timeout and the driver's 5 s attention wait and then fail it; with the probe it is found dead in about 10 s and the call runs again on a new connection, which is what the decision asks of a dead connection. **A setup the probe cuts on a live connection** (the server acknowledged the attention within the driver's 5 s attention wait, so within about 10 s of the setup being sent: slow, not dead) is reset and run again, the setup alone, on that same connection with the call's own timeout, not replaced. Why: a slow server is most likely one under load, when a DBA is investigating, and there a new login is the costly step and the one most likely to fail; replacing a live connection would also drop the profile's healthy idle ones. The probe costs a slow live setup its 5 s and the attention's acknowledgement, which the call's connect time reports (DESIGN.md §5.1). **The boundary** (round 5's second half, finding 1; stated in `REUSE_PROBE`'s doc in `dc24d4b`, whose `dead_before_work` doc names the 5 s condition, the code as built): a live server that doesn't acknowledge the attention within those 5 s, stalled more than about 10 s on the setup, can't be told from a half-open connection. The driver marks the connection dead, so it can't be kept, and it is replaced like a dead one: the profile's other idle connections are dropped, healthy as they are, and the call depends on a new login, which costs the call nothing more when it works (reproduced: ok at about 10 s on a new connection) and fails it when it doesn't (exit 2). Kept as built: taking such a connection for dead is inherent to the probe, and dropping the peers is right for the case the probe is for, a firewall's idle cut, where they most likely died the same way; it needs a stall of more than about 10 s on a trivial request against a server that still answers. History: until `becb56f` no timeout was retried, so a silent reused connection failed its call with a bare `query timeout` after the call's timeout plus 5 s, which round 3 found departs from this decision (Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 3, finding 5). `becb56f` added the probe, over the setup and the `USE`, and retried a setup it cut on a live connection on a new one, beyond "found dead", recorded then for the owner to confirm; round 4 found that costly (a failed login could fail a call that would have succeeded, finding 1), and `6e7a0d8` narrowed it to the above. `6e7a0d8`'s loop around the cut setup also caught a `USE` that ran past the call's own timeout on a live connection, so the `USE` ran twice and the call took about twice its timeout; round 5 confirmed it (P2) and `4519341` fixed it: the engine calls `run::setup` and `run::use_database` apart, and the loop runs the setup alone (Phase 5.2 and 5.3, round 5, first half). Round 5's second half found the boundary above undocumented, the code's comments and the docs saying a slow server keeps its connection without the driver's 5 s, and `dc24d4b` recorded it (round 5, second half, finding 1); mcp-mode.md §5.
20. **MCP ships in the one exe, behind a size gate (owner decision, 2026-10-17, Phase 5).** `sqlh mcp` is a subcommand of `sqlh.exe`, not a separate `sqlh-mcp.exe`. Its code runs only when a harness launches `sqlh mcp`, so CLI-only use pays nothing for it at run time: no startup time, no memory, no open connections; the cost is disk, estimated at about 0.2 MB (295 KB as built, below). **The gate:** MCP's measured cost (`cargo bloat --release --crates` and the exe size before and after, in 5.3) must stay within its 300 KB budget, and the exe within the 3 MB size budget; past that, this decision is reopened. Why: one exe is a hard constraint (AGENTS.md); a split would double the release work (two files to sign, version, ship and document) and let the CLI and MCP drift apart in version while they share the profiles and the output contract; and "only as much tool as you need" is about what runs and what can surprise a user, which dormant code does neither. Splitting later stays cheap, because the reuse engine is a library module (decision 14). Measured: JSON handling (`serde_json`) alone costs 65 KB (spike 5.1). All of Phase 5, measured 2026-10-17 (5.3) at `dc24d4b`: the release exe is 2,789,888 bytes against 2,487,808 at the end of Phase 4, so 302,080 bytes, 295 KB (at 1,024 bytes a KB, like the 65 KB), for the engine, the run's split, `src/mcp`, `serde_json` and the `describe` fix found while building it; 268 KB as first built, before the self-review's fixes, 274.5 KB at `0183d25`, 287 KB at `e8175d4`, 290 KB at `05032c0`, 293 KB at `becb56f`, 294 KB at `6e7a0d8` and 295.5 KB at `4519341`. Within the gate, with 5 KB to spare (read as 300,000 bytes, it would be 2,080 bytes over; the gate's figures have been 1,024-byte KB throughout, and which reading holds is the owner's), and the exe 355,840 bytes under the 3 MB budget. Checked by the exe's size alone: `cargo bloat` isn't installed on the dev machine, so the split by crate is unmeasured. **Reopened 2026-10-18:** the external review accepted 295 KB at `a8c5390` and asked for a remeasure after its fixes, with no silent raise; at `367f8b0` the exe is 2,805,248 bytes, 317,440 (310.0 KiB) over Phase 4, past the 300 KiB (307,200-byte) gate by 10,240 bytes (17,440 over 300,000, so past it under either reading of a KB), and 340,480 under the 3 MB budget. Options put to the owner: (A) raise the MCP gate to 350 KiB; (C) build `serde_json` with `opt-level = "z"` (measured 2,795,520 bytes, 300.5 KiB) and raise the gate to 350 KiB; (B), `opt-level = "z"` alone, no longer fits (Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, Size). **Amended by the owner, 2026-10-18,** after the R35–R39 fixes took MCP to 310.0 KiB (Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, Size): the gate is **350 KiB**, counted in 1,024-byte KB, and `serde_json` is built for size (`[profile.release.package.serde_json] opt-level = "z"`), which saves 9,728 bytes and doesn't matter to MCP's speed at its message sizes. As built: 2,795,520 bytes at the commit that applied it: 307,712 over Phase 4, 300.5 KiB, 50,688 bytes (49.5 KiB) under the 350 KiB (358,400-byte) gate, and 350,208 under the 3 MB budget. Past 350 KiB, this decision is reopened again.
21. **A live test may set `sqlh_test`'s compatibility level (owner decision, 2026-10-18, for R37).** Only `sqlh_test`, the scratch database, and only after the `dbo.sqlh_scratch` marker check; the test reads the level first and arms the cleanup that restores it (`ALTER DATABASE ... SET COMPATIBILITY_LEVEL`) before it changes it (R24), so a failed or killed test leaves the database as it found it, or the next run's cleanup puts it back. No other database-level setting, and no other database. **As built (2026-10-18, `2504b7c`, `a32859e`; the decision is unchanged):** the level is saved in the tests' own table `dbo.probe_compatibility_level`, the restore armed first, and kept until the level is set back; every level batch starts with a server-side check that refuses all but `sqlh_test` with its marker; the level test runs alone, under an exclusive file lock every other live test shares; and "the next run's cleanup" is a start-of-run repair: once per test process, before any live test shares the lock, a saved level a killed run left is put back, or every live test in that process fails with the reason (Phase 5.2 and 5.3 review fixes, Decision 21, as built, with its two limits). Why: R37's acceptance needs SQL Server itself to run `describe`'s aggregation at levels 110 and 120 with a name holding an XML-invalid character, and no other database on the test server runs at those levels; a one-off manual check was the alternative, but a test keeps the threshold from regressing.
22. **Kinds of change are granted separately, per profile and per run (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D1(b)).** Changes come in six kinds a person can grant, named in DBA words: `data`, `maintenance`, `tuning`, `kill`, `remote` and `unvetted` (code sqlh can't classify). A profile's `allow` list, written by the owner, says which kinds it permits, and it holds even under `write_policy = "deny"`; a run uses a listed kind with `--allow KIND`. `--allow-write` stays as the blanket switch for everything outside the deny tier. `read`, `scratch` and the deny tier are never switch names; finer reason labels (contention, settings, cost, ...) appear only in refusal text and the docs. Why: today a `deny` profile, every new profile's (decision 12), has no way to update statistics, force a plan or kill a runaway session short of moving to `flag`, which opens data writes too; separate kinds let an owner grant the narrow change without the broad one. Retiring `--allow-write` (option (c)) was not chosen: it would break existing scripts and agents' habits for little gain. The mechanics (the `allow` list's defaults by `write_policy`, `--allow` limited to it, which kinds keep the rollback wrapper) are D11; the deny tier is D10; the `execute` tool is D15. Like `deny`, the list is an accident guard: an agent that can run `profile add` can change it (R19).
23. **Refusals say what they are, and what lifts them (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D2(a)).** Now, independent of classifier v2: an unknown procedure is refused as `EXEC X isn't on sqlh's list of read-only procedures, so sqlh can't tell what it does` (implicit calls: `... it isn't on sqlh's list of read-only procedures`), with `Rerun with --allow-write to run it as written`; an unknown DBCC command as `DBCC X isn't on sqlh's list of read-only DBCC commands`, naming its documented replacement first where there is one (`PAGE` → `sys.dm_db_page_info`, `LOGINFO` → `sys.dm_db_log_info`, ...); DDL refusals name the statement (`ALTER EVENT SESSION`, not a bare `ALTER`); `sp_query_store_flush_db` gets the aggregation advice in place of a flag. With v2: every refusal names its kind or reason label (decision 22) and the narrowest switch that lifts it, or says that none does (the deny tier). No JSON `kind` member for now (contract 2.2, if a consumer needs it). Why: "would write" is wrong for a procedure or command sqlh merely doesn't know, and steers agents to `--allow-write` for reads. It changes the output contract: DESIGN.md §5.1 and §7, the golden tests and README's "Built for agents" change with the code.
24. **Read-only runs and `sqlh mcp` refuse lock-holding SQL; SNAPSHOT becomes a run setting after a live test (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D3(a) and (i)).** Refused, and lifted by the `data` kind (decision 22): the hints `UPDLOCK`, `XLOCK`, `TABLOCKX` and `HOLDLOCK`; `SERIALIZABLE` and `REPEATABLEREAD` (hint or isolation level) and `REPEATABLE READ` after `ISOLATION LEVEL`; `SET DEADLOCK_PRIORITY` with anything but `LOW` or a literal from −10 to −5; `SET LOCK_TIMEOUT` outside a literal from 0 to the run's own lock timeout (10,000 ms when the run has none; never −1 or a variable); with `WAITFOR`, `sp_getapplock` and `sp_releaseapplock`, refused already. `TABLOCK` alone, `NOLOCK`, `READPAST` and the other hints stay reads. The advice names what the caller can change: remove the hint; `--lock-timeout`; in MCP, the profile's `lock_timeout`. Why: inside the wrapper these hold their locks to the run's final rollback, the one risk the wrapper makes worse, and the in-SQL `SET`s override decision 7 from inside the input; the rare legitimate SERIALIZABLE read gives way. **SNAPSHOT:** a run setting like decision 7's READ UNCOMMITTED (`--snapshot`, a profile's `snapshot = true`), set in the session setup before `BEGIN TRANSACTION`, exclusive with `read_uncommitted`, once a live test shows it works inside the wrapper; then an in-SQL `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL SNAPSHOT` is refused with advice naming it. The test needs `ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_ISOLATION ON` on a test database, a database-level change outside decision 21, so it waits for the owner's approval of that step.
25. **Linked-server reads are allowed with a note; paths an agent chose are refused (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D4a(i) and D4b(i)).** **D4a:** a four-part read through a linked server a DBA configured (`SELECT ... FROM Lnk.db.sys.x`) is a read in read-only runs and `sqlh mcp`, with a note naming the linked server and saying the rollback doesn't reach it; its login mapping is a DBA's grant, so permissions decide. `OPENQUERY`, `OPENROWSET`, `OPENDATASOURCE` and `EXEC ... AT` stay refused. Final once the MSDTC question is answered (whether a four-part read inside the wrapper runs, promotes to a distributed transaction, or fails; command-categories.md §10); that test has not run. **D4b:** in the path-taking functions (`fn_xe_file_target_read_file`, `fn_get_audit_file`, `fn_trace_gettable`, `dm_os_enumerate_filesystem`, `dm_os_file_exists`), a UNC, URL, device, variable or expression path is `remote` and refused, matched in every spelling of the name; any other `fn_`/`dm_` function with a non-local literal fails closed; `fn_dump_dblog` is `unvetted`. The docs teach `system_health*.xel`, and a variable path gets two-step advice (read the path, pass it as a literal). A sqlh-owned read of the server's own configured audit and XE paths may follow if audits on shares prove common. Why: a path an agent chose makes the service account authenticate to a host the agent named, and an agent misled by text it read can name any host.
26. **Read list round 2, the exact-shape reads, and EXECUTE AS reads in the CLI (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D5, all of (a)–(e), with (a1)).** Each entry ships only after its live check inside the wrapper (command-categories.md §10). **(a)** Round 2's argument-checked reads: `DBCC SQLPERF(LOGSPACE)`, `DBCC CHECKIDENT (<name>, NORESEED)`, `DBCC CHECKCONSTRAINTS` naming a table or constraint, `sp_autostats` in its display form, `sp_spaceused` with `@updateusage` the literal `'false'`, `sp_helplogins`, `sp_helpsrvrole`, `sp_helpsrvrolemember`, `sp_helpntgroup`, `sp_srvrolepermission`, `sp_dbfixedrolepermission`, `sp_change_users_login 'Report'`, `sp_linkedservers`, `sp_helplinkedsrvlogin`, `sp_helpserver`; the msdb rule (`sp_help_job`, `sp_help_jobhistory`, `sp_help_jobstep`, `sp_help_jobactivity`, `sp_help_operator`, `sp_help_alert`, `sysmail_help_status_sp`, `sysmail_help_queue_sp`, qualified exactly `msdb.dbo.`); the scratch additions (`UPDATE STATISTICS #t`, `CREATE STATISTICS ... ON #t`, `ALTER INDEX ... ON #t`, `MERGE INTO #t`). **(a1)** The one-shot `sp_server_diagnostics` (no repeat interval, or `0`) is allowed in `sqlh mcp` too, a bounded server-side wait of about 5 s inside the call's timeout, once a live test has measured it; its repeat form is refused everywhere. **(b)** `DBCC CHECKDB`/`CHECKTABLE ... WITH ESTIMATEONLY` and `sp_estimate_data_compression_savings` stay `maintenance`: neither is a cheap read. **(c)** `DBCC MEMORYSTATUS` is on the list; the docs and the skill teach `sys.dm_os_memory_clerks`, `sys.dm_os_process_memory` and `sys.dm_os_sys_memory` first, since it returns many result sets. **(d)** Exact-shape reads run alone and without the wrapper when the input is exactly one of them: `RESTORE HEADERONLY|FILELISTONLY|LABELONLY FROM DISK = N'<local literal>'` (optionally `WITH FILE = <int>`) and `KILL <int> WITH STATUSONLY`; the classifier still runs, permissions are the boundary, and the class is built only once live checks show they fail inside the wrapper and work outside it. **(e)** `EXECUTE AS USER|LOGIN = N'<literal>'`, without `NO REVERT` or `COOKIE`, is allowed in CLI read-only runs, inside the wrapper with the classifier on, so a DBA can ask "can user X read this"; `sqlh mcp` refuses it until a reset test shows the reset reverts it. `IMPERSONATE` is the boundary, and the reads run with the impersonated principal's rights. The doc fix rides along: DESIGN.md §7 lists the `sp_help*` names it admits rather than implying a prefix.
27. **A client-side `:pause N` between batches, capped at 60 s (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D6(a)).** A line `:pause N` (whole seconds, 1 to 60) ends the batch before it, like `GO`; sqlh sends nothing for N seconds, then runs the next batch on the same connection, in the CLI's input and in an MCP call's `sql`. The batches share the session and its `#temp` tables, so an agent snapshots any DMV, pauses and computes the delta in SQL. Bounds: each pause and the sum of a run's or call's pauses at most 60 s, at most `-t` when `-t` isn't 0, and in MCP at most the call's timeout; past a bound it is a usage error before anything runs (exit 3); `--check` skips pauses; cancellation during a pause is immediate. Output: one line `-- paused N s` where the pause was. `WAITFOR` stays refused in every read-only run and every `sqlh mcp` call, so MCP's rule on waits is untouched. Why: a client-side pause holds no server worker, works in both front ends and serves any DMV, where `WAITFOR` holds a worker and the refusal pushed agents to CPU spin loops that distort the waits and CPU they measure. It depends on decision 24: with lock-holding SQL refused, the idle wrapper across a pause holds only the session's `#temp` objects and an open transaction. Presets (`sqlh sample waits|io|counters`) may follow on top.
28. **sqlh ships its own `activity` and `errorlog` reads, and `info` reports the login's view permissions (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D7(a)).** Both are sqlh's own SQL (exempt from the classifier, always read-only, inside the wrapper), as CLI commands and MCP tools, like the schema commands. **`activity`** (`--plans` optional): one row per user session that is running, blocked, blocking, or sleeping with an open transaction, with its blocker, a head-blocker flag and chain depth, waits, resource use, login, host, `program_name`, database, command and statement, and the input buffer for head blockers and sleeping sessions with an open transaction. It hides only its own `@@SPID` and, in `sqlh mcp`, that server's idle pooled connections; a busy pooled session shows, marked as the server's own. Without `VIEW SERVER PERFORMANCE STATE` (`VIEW SERVER STATE` before 2022) it says the login sees only its own session. **`errorlog`** (`--since`, `--search`, `--agent`, `--archive`): newest first, so the row limit shows the end of the log that matters; `xp_readerrorlog` with a server-side window where the login may run it, else `INSERT ... EXEC sys.sp_readerrorlog` sorted in SQL (its working inside the wrapper for a least-privilege login is a live check still to run). **`info`** lists the login's view permissions in one line (`VIEW SERVER PERFORMANCE STATE`, `VIEW SERVER STATE`, `VIEW ANY ERROR LOG`, `VIEW ANY DEFINITION`, `VIEW SERVER SECURITY STATE`, sysadmin). Why: MCP is the agents' main path (agent trial 3), and these give it an `sp_WhoIsActive` substitute and a usable error log with no trust question; `sp_readerrorlog` alone returns the log oldest first, so the default row limit hid an incident's lines behind startup messages. Cost: new output shapes (golden tests, DESIGN.md §5 and §9, README), and MCP size against decision 20's 350 KiB gate (310.0 KiB used) and the exe's 3 MB budget.
29. **Showplan hygiene in Phase 6; the plan modes after classifier v2 (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D8(a)).** In Phase 6: when the input mentions `SHOWPLAN_XML`, `SHOWPLAN_ALL`, `SHOWPLAN_TEXT`, `FMTONLY`, `PARSEONLY` or (outside `--check`) `NOEXEC`, sqlh sends the matching `SET ... OFF` before its closing `ROLLBACK`, each `SHOWPLAN_*` as a request of its own, so the rollback runs and `sqlh mcp` keeps the connection; and a batch that holds `SET SHOWPLAN_*` with any other statement is refused locally (`... must be alone in its batch; ... Put GO after SET SHOWPLAN_XML ON`). After classifier v2, as their own task: an estimated-plan mode (`--plan`, an MCP `plan` tool) for reads, then estimated plans of writes and procedures once a live proof shows nothing runs under SHOWPLAN (a `deny` profile may plan writes, since nothing runs), an actual-plan mode, and the plan summary (DESIGN.md §13's M3 `explain`). Why: hygiene is cheap and ends MCP's dropped connection; the modes are output-contract work with golden tests.
30. **Five small Part 1 items, all accepted (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D9(a)–(e)).** **(a)** `NOLOCK` or `READUNCOMMITTED` as a hint, or `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL READ UNCOMMITTED` in the SQL, gives the run decision 7's honesty note, worded for its source (`-- note: some reads were uncommitted (NOLOCK in the SQL): rows may include uncommitted, duplicated or missing data`); they are not refused. **(b)** Host-disclosure reads stay allowed (`xp_instance_regread`, `sys.dm_server_services`, drives, `xp_msver`), documented in DESIGN.md §7 as a disclosure; a DBA stops `xp_instance_regread` by revoking `EXECUTE` from public. **(c)** The login's application name says what the run may do: `sqlh` (CLI read-only), `sqlh mcp` (MCP read tools), `sqlh (writes)` (`--allow-write` or `write_policy = "allow"`), `sqlh (allow: K,...)` (kind grants); a client-asserted courtesy label, fixed at login, so a pooled MCP connection keeps `sqlh mcp` and the `execute` tool needs connections of its own. **(d)** `sys.dm_db_index_physical_stats` with the literal `NULL` or `DEFAULT` as its first argument and the literal `'SAMPLED'` or `'DETAILED'` as its fifth is refused, with advice to name the database and object or use `'LIMITED'`; a variable mode can't be seen and is documented. **(e)** `sqlh mcp` refuses `SET AUTOPILOT`, `OPEN SYMMETRIC KEY`, `OPEN MASTER KEY` and `SET IDENTITY_INSERT` until a reset test shows the reset clears them, with advice to run them with the CLI, whose session ends with the run; MCP loses `DECRYPTBYKEY` reads and `SET IDENTITY_INSERT #t` until then.
31. **A deny tier, refused in every mode, `--allow-write` included (owner decision, 2026-10-23, Phase 6, command-categories.md D10(b) and (e)).** A lexical pass that runs before anything connects, in every mode, with bounded lookahead (linear, no "too complex" refusal). Members: `RESTORE DATABASE` and `RESTORE LOG` in every form; `BACKUP` of any kind to anything but local literals (a non-local `DISK|TAPE|FILE` literal, `URL`, a variable, a logical device); `SHUTDOWN`; `xp_cmdshell` and the `sp_OA*` procedures under any qualification; `DBCC WRITEPAGE` and `DBCC CHECK*` with `REPAIR_*`; `ABORT_AFTER_WAIT = BLOCKERS` anywhere; `ALTER`/`DROP EVENT SESSION` of `system_health`; `ALTER DATABASE ... SET QUERY_STORE CLEAR`; `sp_query_store_set_hints` with `ABORT_QUERY_EXECUTION` or a non-literal hint; `EXECUTE AS ... WITH NO REVERT`; `DROP DATABASE`; and `sp_configure` with a value whose option is any part of `xp_cmdshell`, `Ole Automation Procedures` or `clr enabled`, holds `%`, `_` or `[`, or is not a literal (`'cmdshell', 1` passes `--allow-write` today). **(e):** once the one-literal rule of D13(b) exists, the deny tier also reads one literal of dynamic SQL in every mode (`EXEC (N'...')`, `sp_executesql`'s statement), which catches `EXEC ('xp_cmdshell ''dir''')`. Worded as an accident guard: the refusal says only that no switch lifts it (`... is on sqlh's deny list (<why>); nothing was run. No switch runs it; ask a person to run it outside sqlh if it is intended`), and DESIGN.md §7 says what gets past it (a variable or concatenated dynamic SQL, `EXEC @var`, a synonym, a wrapper procedure); the login's permissions are the boundary. Not added: the candidates (`ALTER DATABASE ...
